# MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY requirements

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R001` | **Bounded derivation steps in EL+/RL and tableau reasoning.** The EL+/RL and tableau reasoners enforce a derivation-step budget. They check consistency and classify the schema. They fail closed with a typed VALIDATION_BUDGET_EXCEEDED error instead of running without bound. Consistency checking reports no unsatisfiable classes on the shipped ontology corpus. Caller chain: served `GraphSchema` attach → `handle_gateway` (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/graph_schema.rs:195`) → `apply` (`graph_schema.rs:413`) → `validate_entering_schema` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs:84`) → `ComposedSchema::require_abox_consistency` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose/compiled.rs:119`) → `abox_consistency_within` (`crates/medulla-reasoner/src/tableau/abox.rs:177`); and pack import `validate_rdf_unions` (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/admin/connector_pack/import/validation.rs:696`) → `check_pack_ontology` (`abox.rs:80`) → `Reasoner::classify_within` (`crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl/budget.rs:99`). Shape: `DerivationBudget`, `BudgetExhausted` (code `VALIDATION_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`) and `StepMeter` in `crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl/budget.rs`; reuse `classify_within` for every reasoning entry point and add no second counter. | Test: `crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl/budget.rs::exact_budget_completes_and_one_less_is_exhausted`. A unit test exhausts the budget and confirms the typed refusal. An integration test checks consistency across the full corpus. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R002` | **Rejection reasons stay out of the core kernel row schema.** When the engine rejects a query or validation, it reports the reason through the caller's log, metric or error channel. The engine adds no rejection field to the core kernel row structure. The kernel row format remains format-neutral and unchanged by the content of any given rejection reason. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `medulla_plan::uql::serve::run_statement` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/serve.rs:50`) → rows encoded as `UqlRow` (`crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_core.rs:877`); a refusal travels as a `UqlPrintError` / domain error, never as a row field. Shape: `UqlRow` in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_core.rs` keeps its row-shape fields only; rejection reasons use `UqlPrintCode`/`UqlPrintError` in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_uql.rs` and the caller's log or metric channel. | Test: `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_core.rs::kernel_row_schema_carries_no_rejection_reason_field`. A contract test confirms the kernel row schema carries no rejection-reason field. A negative test checks logs or error metadata for the rejection reason. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R003` | **Derivation-step budget is enforced once, not duplicated per path.** The derivation-step budget that bounds EL+/RL and tableau reasoning is implemented a single time. It applies to every reasoning entry point, including the path that composes and classifies the schema. This specification defines no separate step-budget enforcement here; that guarantee is owned by the typed-packs-and-catalog specification's schema composition work. Caller chain: schema attach `validate_entering_schema` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs:84`) → `require_abox_consistency` (`compiled.rs:119`) → `abox_consistency_within(&ontology, SCHEMA_ENTRY_ABOX_BUDGET)` (`crates/medulla-reasoner/src/tableau/abox.rs:177`); the EL+/RL path reaches the same meter through `Reasoner::classify_within` (`crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl/budget.rs:99`). Shape: one `DerivationBudget` type in `crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl/budget.rs`; `SCHEMA_ENTRY_ABOX_BUDGET` (`schema_compose.rs:74`) is a value of that type, not a second implementation; the typed-packs-and-catalog spec owns the composition test. | Test: `crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl/budget.rs::exact_budget_completes_and_one_less_is_exhausted`. Covered by the step-budget enforcement test defined under the typed-packs-and-catalog specification; this specification requires no additional test for this item. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R004` | **Reasoning projection rebuilds after any skipped event.** If the reasoning projection misses or skips an update event, the engine triggers a full rebuild of the projection before serving any further reads from it, rather than serving results derived from an incomplete projection state. Caller chain: `reasoning_projection::spawn` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/reasoning_projection.rs:193`) → `process_graph` (`reasoning_projection.rs:317`) → `recovery::rebuild_after_skipped` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/reasoning_projection/recovery.rs:25`) before `read_index` (`reasoning_projection.rs:782`) serves any read. Shape: `process_graph` calls `rebuild_after_skipped` when a claim reports a dead-lettered position (topic `engine.projection.rebuild`, `TOPIC` at `reasoning_projection.rs:68`); reuse the existing rebuild from `GraphCore` and add no new path. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/reasoning_projection.rs::a_dead_lettered_claim_rebuilds_the_projection_before_further_reads`. An integration test injects a skipped projection event and confirms the engine rebuilds before serving reads. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R005` | **UQL `VALIDATE SHAPE` stage.** Stage: query execution, served `Uql`. Caller: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/shape.rs::validate_shape` (shape dialect `SHACL` core through `medulla_shacl::validate_nodes`). The stage keeps conforming or violating rows per `ShapeKeep` and attaches a `ShapeReport` (R005.1). Order and scores stay unchanged. Refusals: `SHAPE_UNKNOWN` for an unnamed shape and `SHAPE_SOURCE_MISSING` when no `USING` document or graph shapes exist. Writes: none. Depends: none. Children R005.700 and R005.1 extend this stage with integrity shapes and violation kinds. The integrity shapes extend the one SHACL validator (`Op::ValidateShape`); no second validator exists. | Test: `tests/query/test_validate_shape_served.py::test_conforming_and_violating_rows` (L4). It serves a conforming and a violating fixture through `Uql`, asserts the kept rows and the report, and asserts `SHAPE_UNKNOWN`. A second tenant sees no rows. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R006` | **Proof-carrying results for `SPARQL` and rule-derived queries.** Stage: query execution, served `Sparql` and `Uql` with `PROOF`. Caller: served `Sparql` → `handle` (`crates/medulla-server-blob/src/sparql_http.rs:196`) → proof assembly `crates/medulla-plan/src/proof.rs::attach_proof` (planned); and served `Uql` → `handle_uql` → `attach_proof`. Code: `crates/medulla-types/src/proof.rs` (planned) defines `Proof{premises, generation, rule_digests}` where each premise is a fact id plus revision. `generation` names the schema and index generation used. The response field is `proof`; the hand-written Python client preserves it unchanged. Refusal: `PROOF_GENERATION_STALE` when the serving generation differs from the checked one. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Depends: MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R044. | Test: `tests/query/test_proof_served.py::test_sparql_and_rule_query_proofs` (planned) (L4). It requests a proof from a `Sparql` call and a rule-derived `Uql` call through the hand-written Python client. It re-checks each premise against the serving generation and asserts a stale generation refuses. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R007` | **Semantic-index reconciliation tombstones only on a complete proof.** Semantic-index reconciliation marks a source's entries as tombstoned only after an authoritative complete-snapshot proof confirms the source is genuinely missing; an interrupted or partial scan leaves the prior live entries untouched. When reconciliation refuses to proceed, it distinguishes an absent source from one whose proof is unissued or expired so the failure names the actual condition. Caller chain: `SemanticIndexService::admit_sql_source_dirty_reconcile` (`crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_index_service/source_turn.rs:61`) → `complete_reconciliation_scan` (`source_turn.rs:175`) → `finalize_source_reconciliation` (`source_turn.rs:214`) → `admit_missing_tombstones` (`crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_index_service/source_scan.rs:176`). Shape: `validate_snapshot_proof` (`source_types.rs:120`) requires a non-zero complete-snapshot receipt digest before a page may be `complete`; `sql_source_deletion_proof` (`source_contract.rs:302`) is the proof builder; tombstones are admitted only from `finalize_source_reconciliation`. | Test: `crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_index_service/reconciliation_regression.rs::missing_sources_become_tombstones_only_after_complete_snapshot_proof`. A regression test interrupts a source scan before the complete-snapshot proof and confirms no tombstone is created, then replays a complete scan and confirms exactly the absent sources are tombstoned. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R008` | **Edge-native text and vector search with stable identity.** The engine indexes and retrieves edges directly by text and vector similarity. It does not require edges to be reified into nodes. It uses a stable identity for parallel edges between the same pair of nodes. Searches apply tenant, purpose and row-level-security prefilters before candidate truncation. Index updates and deletes are safe to replay. The set of indexable targets is defined once and generated consistently into every client. | A correctness oracle compares filtered edge-search results against an unfiltered full scan, a replay test confirms idempotent update/delete behavior, and a generated-contract check confirms the target set matches across clients. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R009` | **Durable user-managed graph-index lifecycle.** A caller can create, check the status of, and drop a graph index through one durable, user-managed lifecycle that extends the engine's existing index-manager authority. Backfill is fenced against concurrent writes, activation is atomic, a blocked or partial index state is diagnosable, and the index recovers correctly after a restart, with the query planner falling back safely when a needed generation is unavailable. Caller chain: served `EdgeIndex` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_edge_method` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch/edge_index.rs:29`) → `handle_edge_index` (`edge_index.rs:56`) → `apply_edge_index_op` (`edge_index.rs:79`) → `create_durable_edge_index` / `refresh_durable_edge_index` / `drop_durable_edge_index` (`crates/medulla-cypher/src/edge_index/durable.rs:160`, `:207`, `:225`). Shape: `EdgeIndex::begin_build`/`activate`/`status` (`crates/medulla-cypher/src/edge_index.rs:247`, `:267`, `:207`) over the `IndexManager` of `medulla-graph-core`; durable generations through `persist`/`restore` (`durable.rs:105`, `:114`); reuse the managed-index status and block types of `medulla-index-types`. | Test: `crates/medulla-cypher/src/edge_index/tests.rs::the_lifecycle_is_visible_in_the_index_manager_and_a_drop_is_fenced`. An integration test exercises create, backfill, activate and drop plus a restart during backfill, and confirms atomic activation, diagnosable blocked state, and correct planner fallback. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R010` | **Tableau reasoning stays bounded on the shipped core schema.** Tableau consistency and classification reasoning over the shipped core schema completes within bounded time and memory. Schema-axiom internalization and backtracking cause no exponential blowup or stack overflow. Schema composition and restart replay that depend on this reasoning path complete successfully under normal resource limits. Caller chain: served `GraphSchema` attach → `handle_gateway` (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/graph_schema.rs:195`) → `apply` (`graph_schema.rs:413`) → `validate_entering_schema` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs:84`) → `abox_consistency_within` (`crates/medulla-reasoner/src/tableau/abox.rs:177`) with `SCHEMA_ENTRY_ABOX_BUDGET` (`schema_compose.rs:74`). Shape: tableau search in `crates/medulla-reasoner/src/tableau/search.rs` and `abox.rs` with absorption (`absorption.rs`) bounding schema-axiom internalization; the corpus budget `CORPUS_ABOX_STEPS` lives in `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/graph_schema/world_model_tests.rs`. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/graph_schema/world_model_tests.rs::full_corpus_stays_within_the_recorded_reasoning_budgets`. A performance and fault test composes and classifies the full core schema under a restart-replay scenario and confirms it completes within declared time and memory budgets without overflow. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R011` | **EL+/RL reasoning is sound against the shipped ontology corpus.** The EL+/RL reasoner derives only sound subsumption and disjointness conclusions. A consistency check over the shipped core ontology corpus therefore reports no unsatisfiable classes. That includes owl:Nothing and its root class. The EL/RL and tableau reasoning paths agree on consistency and disjointness results over this corpus. Caller chain: schema compose `validate_and_compose` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs:53`) → `compose_validated_sources` (`schema_compose.rs:92`) → `medulla_reasoner::owl::validate_supported_profile` → `Reasoner::classify_within` (`crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl/budget.rs:99`); tableau agreement through `abox_consistency_within` (`abox.rs:177`). Shape: EL+/RL rules in `crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl.rs` and `owl/`; consistency over the shipped corpus under `crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs` tests; no second reasoner. | Test: `crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs::shipped_core_corpus_is_coherent_under_the_el_rl_reasoner`. A full-corpus consistency regression test checks the shipped ontology for unsatisfiable classes. The test confirms that EL/RL and tableau results agree. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R012` | **Tableau honors AllDisjointClasses and core disjointness facts.** Tableau-based reasoning expands owl:AllDisjointClasses declarations the same way the EL path does, so schema-entry full-ABox checking enforces the core disjointness between information-content entities, generically-dependent continuants, processes and temporal regions, and between persons, organizations, servers and events. The corresponding fact that a taxon is never an organism is present and enforced. Caller chain: served `GraphSchema` attach → `validate_entering_schema` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs:84`) → `abox_consistency_within` (`crates/medulla-reasoner/src/tableau/abox.rs:177`) which reads the n-ary axiom through the shared reader `crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl/disjoint.rs`. Shape: `owl/disjoint.rs` is the one reader of `owl:AllDisjointClasses` for both engines; the core disjointness and the taxon/organism axiom live in the shipped core ontology; reuse that reader in the tableau and add no copy. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/graph_schema/world_model_tests.rs::a_taxon_is_never_an_organism`. An integration test checks that tableau reasoning rejects instances violating these disjointness constraints, including the taxon/organism fact. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R013` | **Foreign-source resolution is scoped to the owning principal.** Foreign sources use owner and actor keys. A caller with general federated-query access cannot use another principal's stored credentials. Central authorization conceals source identity. Unknown and known-but-ungranted names return the same typed not-found refusal. An exact sharing grant makes the source identity visible. An unqualified visible source name returns stable `ACCESS_DENIED`; use its qualified shared name. Foreign-source rows are not row-level-security filtered. Policy review must approve this clarification before R013 acceptance or native execution. Caller chain: served `Uql` `FOREIGN` → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `served_foreign_leg` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/foreign_leg.rs:12`) → `ForeignSourceCatalog::resolve_for_plan` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs:401`); wire path `exec_uql` (`crates/medulla-wire-core/src/wire/mod.rs:4752`) reaches the same call at `mod.rs:4809`. Shape: `ForeignSourceCatalog` and `OwnedForeignRegistry` (`foreign_catalog.rs:104`, `:112`) keyed by owner scope; unknown and ungranted names return one typed not-found; `shared_name` (`foreign_share.rs:28`) is the qualified form. | Test: `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/tests/foreign_tenancy.rs::foreign_source_resolves_only_for_the_registering_principal`. An integration test registers a foreign source for one principal. It confirms another principal receives the typed not-found response for an ungranted plain name. A second negative uses an existing exact sharing grant. It confirms stable `ACCESS_DENIED` for an unqualified visible name. R014 covers qualified access and revoke behavior. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R014` | **Explicit grant-based sharing of a registered foreign source.** A principal who owns a registered foreign source can share query access to it with another principal through the engine's existing role-based access-control grant mechanism, rather than through an implicit name lookup. The registering principal retains ownership of the source, and access can be granted and later revoked. Caller chain: served `Uql` `FOREIGN` → `served_foreign_leg` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/foreign_leg.rs:12`) → `resolve_for_plan` (`foreign_catalog.rs:401`) → `may_use_shared` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_share.rs:95`) over the RBAC grant minted by `provision_share_role` (`foreign_share.rs:62`). Shape: `share_resource`, `share_role` and `shared_name` (`foreign_share.rs:35`, `:44`, `:28`) name the grant; ownership stays with the registering principal in `OwnedForeignRegistry`; revoke is an RBAC role removal, not a catalog edit. | Test: `crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_share.rs::a_shared_source_needs_an_exact_grant_and_revocation_stops_use`. An integration test grants another principal access to a foreign source, confirms it can query through the grant, then revokes the grant and confirms the query is refused. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R015` | **Read-only enforcement at the shared SQL execution path.** The shared graph-free SQL execution path classifies and refuses DDL, DML, COPY and CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE statements as not read-only. That refusal lives once at the shared path instead of being duplicated per caller. Every caller that reaches this path, including observability log search, is subject to the same refusal. Caller chain: observability log search `search_sql` (`crates/medulla-server-obs/src/obs/search.rs:205`) and the decision-log view → `medulla_sql::sql::exec_sql_over_tables` (`crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/exec.rs:164`) → `require_single_read` (`crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/read_only.rs:25`). Shape: the gate lives once in `crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/read_only.rs` using `medulla_sql_lang::sql::classify`; refusals carry the `READ_ONLY_SQL` prefix; callers add no pre-check. | Test: `crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/read_only.rs::only_a_single_read_statement_passes`. A test sends DDL, DML, COPY and CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE statements through each caller of the shared SQL path. The callers include log search, and every statement must be refused. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R016` | **UQL AS OF uses Unix-second timestamps, not ISO dates.** UQL's AS OF clause takes a Unix-second timestamp. Query stages use the published UQL stage-separator syntax. They do not use Cypher-shaped text or an ISO-8601 timestamp. Text that does not match this grammar is rejected as invalid rather than silently accepted. A conformance test confirms that UQL text emitted by any client parses successfully against the current grammar. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `bind_served_statement` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query.rs:74`) → `medulla_uql::parse_statement` (`crates/medulla-uql/src/lib.rs:70`) → AS OF parser (`crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/basic.rs:82`). Shape: the AS OF production in `crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/basic.rs` takes an integer Unix-second timestamp only; text outside the grammar returns a `UqlError` with a span; no ISO-8601 branch. | Test: `crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/tests/legacy.rs::as_of_rejects_iso_date_and_cypher_shaped_text`. A parser test feeds the correct stage-chained, Unix-second AS OF form and confirms it parses. A negative test confirms that the parser rejects Cypher-shaped or ISO-timestamp text. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R017` | **Dependency tracking covers edge-type and embedding generations.** Traversal and embedding-ranking plans register their dependencies. They name the edge types and embedding generations they read. A write that changes an unrelated edge type or generation does not invalidate the plan. A write touching a real dependency does. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `handle_uql` (`uql_statement.rs:17`) computes `plan_dependency_set` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:26`) → result cache `get_dep`/`put_dep` (`crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs`); a commit notes its footprint through `DepClock::note_footprint` (`crates/medulla-query-cache/src/dep_scope.rs:259`). Shape: `Dim`, `DepSet`, `DepProbe` and `WriteFootprint` in `crates/medulla-query-cache/src/dep_scope.rs`; the footprint is produced in `crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/core_index_changes.rs`; an embedding generation enters through `DepProbe::embedding_generation` (`dep_scope.rs:151`). | Test: `crates/medulla-core/tests/dependency_scoped_edges.rs::typed_traversal_survives_an_edge_of_another_type`. A test performs disjoint writes against unrelated edge types and embedding generations. It confirms that an existing traversal/rank plan is not invalidated. A dependent write does invalidate it. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R018` | **Class-level volatility drives invalidation and cache freshness.** Classes and sources declare an eg:volatilityClass, and a per-class invalidation event fires when matching data changes. Foreign-source result caching only serves a cached result when the relevant source watermark is current, consistent with the volatility class declared for that data. Caller chain: `FreshnessFeed` → `dispatch_query_family` (`dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_freshness_feed` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/freshness_feed.rs:21`) → `build_feed` (`freshness_feed.rs:52`) → `volatility_policy` (`crates/medulla-core/src/freshness.rs:38`); foreign result cache gate `ForeignWatermarks::cacheable` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/foreign_watermarks.rs:82`). Shape: `eg:volatilityClass` resolved by `volatility_policy`; a cacheable plan needs every source watermark present and current in `foreign_watermarks.rs`; the cache key salt comes from `ForeignWatermarks::salt` (`foreign_watermarks.rs:87`). | Test: `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/foreign_watermarks.rs::a_stale_watermark_refuses_caching`. A test changes data in a declared volatility class, confirms the corresponding invalidation event fires, and confirms the foreign-source cache only serves fresh, watermark-current results. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R019` | **Per-bar signal-state advancement with revisable trend records.** On each bar finalization, the complex-event-processing layer advances signal state and records a TrendFlip event; a later correction can revise a previously recorded TrendFlip without losing the revision history. A scanner index exposes the latest signal state per series for efficient lookup. Caller chain: `medulla-h-compute` finance surface (`crates/medulla-h-compute/src/finance/market.rs:10`) → `market::scan::scan` (`crates/medulla-finance-market/src/market/scan.rs:139`) and `market::events::flip_event` (`crates/medulla-finance-market/src/market/events.rs:40`). Shape: `SignalState` upsert in `ScannerIndex::upsert` (`scan.rs:39`) keeps one latest state per key; a revision appends a record with a higher `revision` and `known_at` and never rewrites the log; reuse `TrendFlip` and `Event` types of the crate. | Test: `crates/medulla-finance-market/src/market/record_tests.rs::a_revision_appends_revision_records_and_never_rewrites`. A test finalizes a sequence of bars, confirms signal state advances and TrendFlip records are created, then issues a revision and confirms both the historical and latest-state views are correct. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R020` | **One query-text method surface, with the legacy method retired.** The engine serves exactly one query-text method, Method::Uql. The legacy UnifiedQueryText method and its callers, including remote-engine federation and server tests, are migrated to it. UnifiedQueryText is removed from the codebase with no compatibility alias or deprecation shim. Caller chain: wire method census `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/query.rs` and `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/` → served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`dispatch.rs:138`) → `handle_uql` (`uql_statement.rs:17`). Shape: `Method::Uql` is the one query-text variant; the contract registry in `crates/medulla-capabilities/tests/contract_registry.rs` lists retired method names and fails if one returns; no alias. | Test: `crates/medulla-capabilities/tests/contract_registry.rs::retired_methods_never_reappear`. A contract test confirms UnifiedQueryText no longer exists in the method registry. The L3 integration test `crates/medulla-plan/src/engine_peer_tests.rs::a_verified_pushdown_narrows_the_transfer_without_changing_the_answer` runs remote-engine federation over Method::Uql. |
Depends: none
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R021` | **Full UQL predicate algebra with SQL-consistent evaluation.** UQL's predicate algebra supports the comparison operations, AND/OR/NOT with parentheses, IN, BETWEEN, IS [NOT] NULL, typed literals, JSONPath access and spatial predicates. The per-row three-valued evaluator used by UQL agrees with the equivalent SQL evaluation on every supported predicate, including null handling. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `handle_uql` (`uql_statement.rs:17`) → `run_statement` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/serve.rs:50`) → `execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) → `holds` / `all_hold` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/pred_eval.rs:67`, `:62`); SQL leg `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/pred_sql.rs`. Shape: `holds` returns a three-valued `Truth` in `crates/medulla-exec/src/pred_eval.rs`; the SQL rendering of the same predicate is `pred_sql.rs`; reuse `Pred` from `medulla-types` and add no second evaluator. | Test: `crates/medulla-exec/src/pred_eval.rs::the_sql_leg_and_the_row_evaluator_keep_identical_rows`. A differential test evaluates the same predicate set through SQL and through the UQL per-row evaluator. It uses null and non-null fixtures and asserts identical results. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R022` | **UQL source clauses are first-class, with typed empty-result cases.** UQL treats MATCH, SPARQL, TSSCAN, SENSOR FUSE/ALIGN, SPATIAL/TENSOR SCAN and FOREIGN SCAN/HTTP as first-class source clauses in its grammar and planner. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R032` | **Probabilistic impact propagation with cone-scoped recomputation.** Stage: query execution, served `Uql` `PROPAGATE <model> <edge-pattern> HOPS n`, and the standing-query job. Cycles unroll to depth `propagate_unroll_depth` (default 3, `[1, 8]`). `independent_cascade` runs `propagate_samples` seeded Monte Carlo runs (default 2000, `[100, 100000]`) and reports a 95% confidence interval. The seed comes from the request, else the first 8 bytes of the request digest. The result lists the top 5 contributing paths with per-seed attribution. A standing query seeded from `Incident`, `ConformanceViolation` or `CVE` facts recomputes only the affected cone. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R045` | **Foreign rows carry typed columns for filter and projection pushdown.** A foreign source returns ForeignRows with stable column names, types, null semantics and row provenance, and apply_filter/apply_projection classify each operation as Exact, Inexact or Unsupported; only an Exact result removes the local residual, an Inexact result may narrow transfer only to a superset of authorized matching rows, and an Unsupported result fetches the needed columns and evaluates locally without ever dropping a column required by a residual, ORDER BY, authorization or proof channel. Unit tests confirm: an executed result carries the exact UQL that ran; `plan_only` returns a candidate with `rows: None` and reads no data; malformed planner output is refused with `NlQueryError::ParseFailed` (not executed); and over-budget planner output is refused with `NlQueryError::BudgetExceeded` before parse/execute, in both plan-only and executing modes. |

A sibling method carries the disclosure. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2.2.1` | **Served `NlQueryExplain` returns the executed UQL with the rows.** `Method::NlQueryExplain { text, graph }` takes the same request as `Method::NlQuery`. Both handlers share one run, `run_nl_query` in `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs`. There, `handle_nl_query_plan` returns the planner's exact UQL text with the parsed plan. The same legs, row-level-security snapshot and off-lock run then produce the rows. The engine answers with `medulla_types::nl_query::NlQueryExplainResult`: `{"uql": <UQL text>, "rows": [[node_id, score or null], ...]}`. The engine never caches this result. The `NlQuery` result stays byte-identical. The Python client exposes `QueryClient.nl_query_explain(text, graph=None)`. Caller chain: served `NlQueryExplain` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_nl_query_explain` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs:178`) → `run_nl_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs:269`) → `handle_nl_query_plan` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs:60`). Served tests confirm that `uql` equals the planner's output. They confirm that `rows` equal the `NlQuery` rows for the same text. A parse failure (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`) refuses with the same code and detail as `NlQuery`. An over-budget answer (`UQL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`) does too. A wire test confirms the Python call shape. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R077.2` | **Column vectorization: bind and unbind through the semantic index.** Stage: SQL admin, served `SemanticIndex`. A text column binds as a `SemanticBinding` whose `source_selector` is `SemanticSourceSelector::SqlColumnRef(SqlColumnRef { catalog_id, schema_id, table_id, column_id })`. Bind is `SemanticIndexOp::AdmitBinding`. Unbind is `SemanticIndexOp::DropBinding` at the live generation. No `SqlColumnBind` or `SqlColumnUnbind` method exists, and no `TxnOp::EmbeddingBinding` catalog operation exists. The semantic store is the one durable binding table. The table-store `EmbeddingBinding` record gains no writer. The embedding space is `SemanticBindingDraft.model` (`SemanticModelIdentity`) with `dimension` and `metric`. The policy revision is `SemanticBindingDraft.policy` (`SemanticPolicyComponents`), including `source_acl_revision` and `source_acl_digest`. Caller chain: served `SemanticIndex` → `handle` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/binding.rs:65`) → `admit_binding` (`binding.rs:139`) → `checked_admission_inputs` (`binding.rs:119`) → `check_sql_column_source` (new, same file) → `SemanticIndexService::admit_binding_operation`. `check_sql_column_source` runs only for `SqlColumnRef`. It reads the table schema through the caller's `AuthorizedTable` (`crates/medulla-server-auth/src/sql_catalog_acl.rs:1622`). A column absent from the schema refuses `COLUMN_UNKNOWN`. A column whose type fails `is_embeddable` (`crates/medulla-table-store/src/tables/embedding_binding.rs:670`; `Text` or `Json` only) refuses `COLUMN_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED`. A table the caller may not `SELECT` refuses `ACCESS_DENIED`. Both codes join the `SemanticIndex` entry of `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Unbind removes vectors through the existing `DropBinding` retirement; there is no second removal queue. Writes: the binding record through the semantic store commit only. Depends: MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R044. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_column_vector_served.py::test_bind_text_refuse_integer` (L4). It binds a text column, refuses an integer column with `COLUMN_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED`, refuses an absent column with `COLUMN_UNKNOWN`, then drops the binding. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R077.5` | **Column vectorization: end-to-end column vector search over the semantic binding.** Stage: SQL read. SQL form: `SELECT ... FROM t [WHERE ...] ORDER BY NEAREST(col, 'text') [, ...] LIMIT k [OFFSET o]`, optionally under `EXPLAIN`. Caller chain: served `Sql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_sql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:185`) → `handle_sql_read` (`sql_read.rs:101`) → `catalog_sql_response` (`sql_read.rs:546`; the KnowledgeStream SQL read shares it) → `plan_nearest` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_nearest.rs`) → `search_live` (new, `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/search.rs`) → `authorized_read_store_for_query` (`crates/medulla-server-auth/src/sql_catalog_acl/ann_projection.rs:48`) → `exec_sql_typed_with_tables_hive` (`crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/exec.rs:367`). Recognition: `nearest_form(sql: &str) -> Result<Option<NearestForm>, String>` is new in `crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/nearest.rs`. It strips an `EXPLAIN` prefix with `explained_statement` (`crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/read_only.rs:42`) and parses once. `NearestForm { table: String, column: String, text: String, fetch: usize, explain: bool }`; `fetch` is `k + o`. A statement with no `NEAREST` call is `Ok(None)`. These refuse `INVALID_ARGUMENT`: two `NEAREST` calls, `NEAREST` anywhere but the first `ORDER BY` key, a first argument that is not a bare column of the single `FROM` table, a second argument that is not a string literal, a join or subquery in `FROM`, and no `LIMIT`. Binding resolution: a SQL column has at most one binding, under a canonical id. `SqlColumnRef::binding_id(&self) -> String` is new in `crates/medulla-index-types/src/semantic_index/selector_ref.rs` and returns `binding:{table_id}.{column_id}`. `check_sql_column_source` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/binding.rs:146`) refuses a `SqlColumnRef` binding whose `binding_id` is not that value, with `invalid_semantic_input(req_id, "semantic SQL column binding id")` (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`). `plan_nearest(authority: &CarrierAuthority, persist_dir: &Path, sql: &str) -> Result<Option<NearestProbe>, String>`: (1) `open_authorized_table(authority, persist_dir, &form.table, SqlPrivilege::Select)` (`ACCESS_DENIED`), and table and column names are taken as the table schema spells them; (2) `existing_semantic_service(persist_dir, authority.tenant_scope(), &binding_id)` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index_runtime.rs:329`); an error refuses `COLUMN_NOT_BOUND`; (3) `service.binding()` must be `Some` with `source_selector == SqlColumnRef { catalog_id: "tenant", schema_id: "public", table_id, column_id }`, else `COLUMN_NOT_BOUND`; (4) `search_live(&service, &binding, &form.text, form.fetch)`. `search_live(service: &SemanticIndexService, binding: &SemanticBinding, text: &str, k: usize) -> Result<LiveSearch, String>` with `LiveSearch { generation: u64, hits: Vec<(String, f32)> }`. It reads `SemanticIndexService::read_live` (new pass-through to `SemanticCodeStore::read_live`, `crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/serving.rs:22`); `None` refuses `COLUMN_NOT_BOUND`. The image's `identity()` must equal `(binding.dimension, Some(binding.model_digest))`, else `SEARCH_PROBE_MODEL_MISMATCH`. The query text embeds through the engine-local `ModalityEncoderRegistry` of R039.1.3 (`lookup_by_space`: the entry registered under the space's own id, else the first entry of the exact tuple), at the space tuple `(binding.model_id, binding.dimension, binding.model_revision)`. No entry refuses `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE`. A vector whose length is not `binding.dimension` refuses `SEARCH_PROBE_MODEL_MISMATCH`. The probe is `AnnIndex::from_image(&image.index)?.search(&vector, k)`; its hits are `(member id, distance)`, the distance being `1 - cosine similarity`, where the member id is the row's `SemanticSqlSourceIdentity::source_entity_id`. `search_live` is the only executor over a live semantic generation. Narrowing: `NearestProbe { table, column, binding_id, generation, space: String, hits: Vec<(String, f32)> }` is new in `crates/medulla-table-store/src/tables/nearest.rs`; `space` is `binding.model_digest`. `authorized_read_store_for_query` gains `nearest: Option<&NearestProbe>`; the pgwire caller (`crates/medulla-wire-core/src/wire/mod.rs:2007`) passes `None`. `visible_rows` (`ann_projection.rs:146`) gains a nearest arm for that table: `AuthorizedTable::select_nearest(&self, probe: &NearestProbe) -> Result<(Vec<Vec<Cell>>, NearestRanking), String>` (new, `ann_projection.rs`). Under `with_current_authority`, it scans the rows `select(None)` (`crates/medulla-server-auth/src/sql_catalog_acl.rs:1665`) returns, so the caller's row-level security applies. For each row it derives the entity id with `semantic_record_identity_digest` (`sql_catalog_acl.rs:1306`) and `SemanticSqlSourceIdentity::create(selector, tenant_scope, digest).source_entity_id()`. It keeps the rows whose id is a hit. There is no reverse index from entity id to row, so the scan is linear in the visible rows, as the projection copy already is. Hidden rows that rank in the top `k` are dropped, so a read can return fewer than `k` rows; nothing is oversampled. Order: `NearestRanking { distances: HashMap<String, f64>, explain: String }` (new, `nearest.rs`) maps each kept row's column text to its hit distance; equal texts keep the smaller distance. The projection store carries it through `TableStore::with_nearest(Arc<NearestRanking>)` and `TableStore::nearest() -> Option<&Arc<NearestRanking>>` (new, beside `with_column_usage`, `crates/medulla-table-store/src/tables/store.rs:793`). `exec_sql_typed_with_tables_hive` registers the scalar UDF `nearest(Utf8, Utf8) -> Float64` on the statement's `SessionContext` when `store.nearest()` is `Some`. It returns the distance of the first argument's text, and NULL for a text not in the map. The SQL executor then runs the unchanged statement, so `ORDER BY NEAREST(...)` is ascending distance, nearest first; `WHERE`, later keys, `OFFSET` and `LIMIT` apply as written. A `NEAREST` statement reaching the executor without a ranking refuses `METHOD_NOT_YET_SERVED`. `EXPLAIN`: when `form.explain`, the executor appends one row to the plan relation, `plan_type = 'vector_scan'`, `plan = 'VectorScan binding=<binding_id> space=<model digest> generation=<n>'` (`NearestRanking.explain`). Refusals: `COLUMN_NOT_BOUND` and `SEARCH_PROBE_MODEL_MISMATCH` are new `ServerErrorCode` variants `ColumnNotBound` and `SearchProbeModelMismatch` in `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs`. They join `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE` (R039.1.3) in the `Sql` entry of `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs:267`. `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, `ACCESS_DENIED` and `METHOD_NOT_YET_SERVED` are shared codes. Overstory table explorer consumption is a handoff to `WEBUI` and is not a row here. Writes: none. Depends: R077.2, R039.1.3. | Test: `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/split_tests/medulla_h_query_column_vector_tests.rs::bound_column_nearest_neighbour` (L3, registered in `split_tests.rs`, gated like the S1 dispatch test it extends). The proof is Rust, not Python, because the stage artifacts and their digests are computed by engine types that no Python path reproduces; every step is nonetheless a served operation through `request_boundary::dispatch`, and no test-only operation exists. The reusable helper is `drive_binding_to_live_generation(state, worker, draft, source_manifest) -> u64` (the live generation) in the served-tests crate's support module, extending the S1 sequence of `medulla_h_query_dispatch_pipeline_tests.rs::dispatch_drives_a_source_through_s1_and_publishes_s2`; the stage digests are built with the engine's own constructors (`SemanticBinding::create`, `SemanticStageIntent`/`SemanticStageTransition` and the artifact types of `crates/medulla-index-types/src/semantic_index/`), the way `crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/s6_finalization_tests.rs` builds them for the store. Op sequence, each a `SemanticIndexOp` (`crates/medulla-index-types/src/semantic_index/op.rs`): `AdmitBinding`; `InstallModelConfig` (the S6 gate's model configuration); `SubscribeStageConsumer` once per queue class; then, for each stage in order, `ClaimStageLeases` and `ValidateStageLease`, followed by the completing op: S1 `CompleteSqlSourceStage` (no source row travels), S2 and S4 and S6 `CompleteStage`, S3 and S5 `CompleteGenerationStage`; the S4 and S5 recall measurements go through `RecordProbeMeasurement` under the stage's own lease; and `LiveGeneration` is read to return the published generation. Every stage has an op, so none is added. The test then sends the served `Sql` statements: a five-row text table bound with the model identity of the registered text encoder (the hash encoder of `MEDULLA_UQL_TEXT_EMBEDDER=hash`) returns the rows of `ORDER BY NEAREST(col, 'text') LIMIT k` in the expected distance order (`1 - cosine similarity`), a row hidden by row-level security is absent, `EXPLAIN` names the binding, an unbound column refuses `COLUMN_NOT_BOUND`, and a binding id other than `binding:<table>.<column>` refuses `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. The Python file `tests/retrieval/test_column_vector_served.py` keeps only the row R077.2 bind and unbind journey. |

(3) Handler: `fn serve_lake_file(lake: &LakeManager, store: &dyn ChunkStore, path: &str, scope: &RequestScope) -> RestReply` in `crates/medulla-server-lake/src/lake/rest/catalog.rs`, called from `handle` after `scope_authorized` and before `guard_projection_write` when the route is the files row. It lists input scores, normalization, region, fused score, selected ids, exclusions, count and the policy digest (`selection_policy_digest`, SHA-256 of the policy), plus the reranker and embedding model revisions. `RoundResult.inference` counts `embedding`, `reranker` and `selection` calls apart, and `selection` must be 0. The request carries the measured outcomes as `ActivationBenchmark { digest, baseline, candidate }`, each a `BenchmarkResult { ndcg_at_10, mean_selected }`. Activation compares the policy with a fixed-count baseline on identical candidates, models, corpus and tenant filters. Activation needs two results. The nDCG at 10 may lose at most `knob retrieval.selection_quality_floor` (0.01, [0, 0.1], nDCG). The mean selected count may reach at most `knob retrieval.selection_cost_ratio_max` (0.9, [0.5, 1.0], ratio) of the baseline. The benchmark digest binds data, labels and results. Measured Medulla results are the only accepted evidence. Shape: `SelectionReceipt` (`crates/medulla-result-types/src/retrieval_verdict.rs:315`) exists. `run_round` converts it into one `RetrievalPremise{step, name, value}` defined in `crates/medulla-result-types/src/retrieval_round.rs` (the premise value embeds the `SelectionReceipt`, which `medulla-types` cannot see) and fills `RoundResult.inference` (`InferenceCounts { embedding, reranker, selection }`). The benchmark gate reuses `check_activation` and adds no second comparison path. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.8.4` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, feedback step. Register in the family `REFUSALS` table (R010.100). `RETRIEVAL_FEEDBACK_INVALID` covers forged document ids and incompatible revisions. It also covers evidence outside tenant scope. It covers critiques that alter scope, scores, limits or activation policy. `RETRIEVAL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` covers exhausted budgets and names the terminal verdict and `ExhaustedDimension`. R082 checks round and budget limits before progress or confidence stops. A completed round with valid evidence may stop with `no_new_evidence` or `quality_saturated` without calibration. It keeps `ROUND_NOT_CALIBRATED` and never counts as calibrated sufficiency. Invalid feedback and terminal continuations perform no inference. A continuation whose round or budget limit is already spent is terminal before any row is read (`exhausted_before_retrieval` in `run_round`); feedback whose revisions, limits or critique model revision are invalid is refused before any row is read; the document and evidence ids are checked against the rows the round returned, because the round's own rows are the only evidence of what it returned. A critique made against another model revision is `RETRIEVAL_FEEDBACK_INVALID`; a critique's reformulation is recorded as rejected and never applied. Caller chain: wire `Method::RetrievalRound` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_retrieval_round` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/retrieval_round.rs`) → `run_round` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs`) → `apply_feedback`, whose refusal `handle_retrieval_round` maps to the response error. Shape: The family refusal type is `RetrievalRefusal` (`crates/medulla-result-types/src/retrieval_verdict.rs:36`). Add the two codes there and map them in `handle_retrieval_round`. Register both in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs` and in `RetrievalErrorCode`. `apply_feedback` returns the refusal and calls no provider. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R082.2` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, stop step. Add `decide_stop(table, history, context, progress) -> StopVerdict`, with `round_progress(returned_ids, prior_row_ids, quality, prior_quality, epsilon) -> RoundProgress` measuring the two progress checks, in new `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_stop.rs`. Precedence after feedback validation: round limit, other exhausted budget, no new evidence, quality saturation, then calibrated confidence. The progress checks apply only when `StopContext.completed_round_progress` says the round supplied measured evidence. No new evidence means the returned ids minus `prior_row_ids` are empty. Quality is the mean score of the returned rows. Saturation means the absolute change from `prior_quality` is below `knob retrieval.saturation_epsilon`. A confidence stop needs the last two answer digests equal and `confidence - baseline_confidence > margin_threshold`. Equality at the threshold continues. A margin below the baseline is reported as 0. A missing calibration row continues with `continue_uncalibrated` only when no earlier terminal condition applies. Other continuations use `continue_answer_changed` or `continue_margin_low`. Progress comes from completed-round evidence, not from caller or critique claims. The margin is `knob retrieval.arbitration_margin` (0.10, [0, 0.5], confidence). Support is classified first. A candidate is `supported` when every claim carries a citation. `VerifySpan` of MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R144.1 must accept each one on the snapshot of the query digest. A missing or invalid citation gives `unsupported`. A citation outside the caller's snapshot gives `unknown`. Exactly one supported candidate wins, whatever the confidence. Two supported candidates: the higher confidence wins only if it exceeds the other by more than the margin, otherwise `Abstain`. No supported candidate gives `Abstain`. The policy is deterministic and learns nothing. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R083.3` | **Wiring.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, arbitration step. Caller chain: wire `Method::RetrievalRound` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_retrieval_round` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/retrieval_round.rs`) → `run_round` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs`) → `arbitrate`. The `arbitration` part carries the `prior` and `retrieved` candidates and the query digest. The harness produces both candidates and sends them. Medulla verifies their citations. `run_round` runs `arbitrate` last and returns the verdict in `RoundResult.arbitration`. A call without an `arbitration` part returns none. No production caller of arbitration exists today. This row creates it. Shape: The arbitration stage is the last block of `run_round` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs`). It builds the `SpanVerifier` over the request snapshot and calls `arbitrate`. It sets `RoundResult.arbitration` only when the part is present. A citation that fails verification lowers support and never raises it. Caller chain: wire `Method::RetrievalRound` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_retrieval_round` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/retrieval_round.rs`) → `run_round` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs`) → `arbitrate`, whose abstain verdict `handle_retrieval_round` maps to the response error. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.1.3` | **Multimodal space: wiring.** Stage: query execution. Encoder source (the model-binding configuration, the same source as the text embedder; no other source). `crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/query_support.rs` gains `pub fn bound_modality_encoder(modality: medulla_index_types::embedding::Modality) -> Option<(Box<dyn medulla_exec::exec::TextEmbedder>, TextEncoderIdentity)>` (feature `modality-serving`): for `Image`, `Audio` and `Video` it reads `EmbedderConfig::from_env_for(modality)` (new in `crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/ingest/embedder.rs`: the variables `MEDULLA_EMBEDDING_IMAGE_URL`, `_AUDIO_URL`, `_VIDEO_URL` with the same siblings `_MODEL`, `_DIMS` and `_API_KEY` and the same parsing, bounds and defaults as the unprefixed text variables) and returns `None` when the `_URL` is unset (there is no hash fallback for a non-text modality) and otherwise `TextEncoderIdentity { encoder_id: format!("remote:openai-compatible:{modality}") (`image`, `audio`, `video`), space_id: the `_MODEL` value or `openai-compatible`, dimensions: the `_DIMS` value, revision: TEXT_ENCODER_REVISION }`. A multimodal space shares one `(space id, dimensions, revision)` tuple across its four bindings, so the operator sets the same `_MODEL` and `_DIMS` on every modality that belongs to one space; a binding whose tuple differs from the declared space finds no entry (`QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE`). `register_query_encoders` (`crates/medulla-server/src/query_encoders.rs`) registers the text embedder as before, then each non-text modality for which `bound_modality_encoder` returns `Some`, then, when `MEDULLA_EMBEDDING_ENCODER_DIR` is set and non-empty, calls `modality_encoders().load_dir(Path::new(dir))` (file format and rules in R039.1.6). An unbound modality is simply not registered, and a query whose space binds that modality refuses `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE`. Any registration or load error refuses to boot (the existing `expect` in `compose_server_state`); the load errors are boot-time strings and are not served codes, so no `error_routing.rs` entry is added. The bind step looks up the registry entry whose space tuple equals the request space. Refusal: `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Shape: New `ModalityEncoderRegistry` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/embed_registry.rs`. `bind_modality_encoders` joins `run_unified_bind_embedder` in `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:513`. Encoders implement the existing `TextEmbedder` shape (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:287`). Startup: `compose_server_state` calls `register_query_encoders` (`crates/medulla-server/src/query_encoders.rs`), which registers the bound text embedder (`uql_text_embedder()`) under the identity `uql_text_encoder_identity()` reports (`builtin:hash` or `remote:openai-compatible`, the space id, its dimensions and revision `1`), so a request refuses `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE` only when nothing is bound. Served `tests/query_encoders_served.rs::registered_text_encoder_reaches_the_served_query` (the startup registration is what the served `UnifiedQuery` binds from). L4 `tests/retrieval/test_multimodal_served.py::test_registry_encoders_reach_index`. A served `UnifiedQuery` must reach the registry encoder and the maintained index, and it asserts `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE` for a missing entry. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.1.4` | **Multimodal space: refusal.** Class: BUILD. Stage: query execution. Caller chain: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `run_unified_off_lock` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:864`) → `run_unified_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:325`) → `execute_rows` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/query_support.rs:91`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) → `rank_top`. Shortfall model: `Shortfall` in `crates/medulla-types/src/retrieval.rs` gains `pub missing: u64` (no serde default: hard cutover; the struct keeps `deny_unknown_fields`), after `found`. `requested` is the `k` the plan asked for, `found` is the rows returned, and `missing` is the number of eligible candidates that hold no embedding. The candidate set is the caller's own: `replenish_until_k` in `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/vector_rank.rs` receives candidates already narrowed by visibility and constraints, so `missing` counts only rows the caller may see and a hidden row changes no count. `missing = candidates.iter().filter(|id| semantic.get_embedding(id).is_none()).count() as u64` and is 0 for the reasons `eligible_exhausted` and `budget_exhausted`. Every `Shortfall { .. }` literal (`vector_rank.rs` at the exhausted-eligible, budget and replenish sites) gains `missing`; mirror the field in `Shortfall` of `medulla/generated/models.py`. Rule: when the replenish returns fewer than `k` rows, the reason is `missing_embedding` if and only if `missing > 0`, else `eligible_exhausted`; nothing pads the answer. Refusals: `EMBEDDING_SPACE_INCOMPATIBLE` (registered; `pair_allowed` of R039.1.2) and `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE` (`ServerErrorCode::QueryEncoderUnavailable`, raised by `bind_modality_encoders` of R039.1.3 when the request's `RetrievalRequest.modality` pair names a query modality with no `ModalityEncoderRegistry` entry whose `(space_id, dim, model_revision)` equals the request's space; the served request names the pair through `UnifiedQuery.retrieval.modality` of R040.1.7). The space check runs first, the encoder check second, both before any ranking. R039.1.3 owns the registration of `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE` in `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs` and `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs` (beside `EMBEDDING_SPACE_INCOMPATIBLE`, `error_routing.rs:258`); this row adds no second registration and asserts the code through the served path. Writes: none. Depends: R039.1.2, R039.1.3. Shape: the `Shortfall` field, the `missing` count in `replenish_until_k`, the python mirror. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_multimodal_served.py::test_missing_embedding_shortfall_and_refusals` (L4). It separates missing embeddings (`missing > 0`, reason `missing_embedding`), too few eligible items (`missing == 0`, `eligible_exhausted`) and exhausted budgets (`budget_exhausted`), asserts a hidden row changes no count, and asserts both refusal codes. Unit proof: `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/vector_rank.rs::tests::shortfall_counts_missing_embeddings` (L2). Status: planned; the test file does not exist yet. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.1.5` | **Multimodal space: evidence.** Class: BUILD. Stage: query execution. Caller chain: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `run_unified_off_lock` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:864`) → `run_unified_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:325`) → `execute_rows` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/query_support.rs:91`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`). `RetrievalReceipt` (`crates/medulla-types/src/retrieval.rs`, built by R040.1.5; today its one field is `constraint: Option<TraceConstraint>`) gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] pub modality: Option<TraceModality>`, set exactly when the request carried `retrieval.modality`. New in the same file, each `#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]`, `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` and the `contract-schema` derive of its neighbours: `TraceModality { modality_direction: String, space_id: String, model_revisions: Vec<String>, index_generation: u64, authorized_shortfall_counts: Option<Shortfall>, encoding_calls: Vec<EncodingCall> }` and `EncodingCall { model: String, modality: String, input_digest: String }`. Field meanings: `modality_direction` is `<query>-><target>` over the lowercase modality names of the request's `ModalityPair` (`text->image`); `space_id` is `EmbeddingSpaceRef.id` of the pinned space; `model_revisions` is the distinct encoder `revision` strings of the calls, in first-call order; `index_generation` is the generation counter of the maintained vector index the ranking read, the same value that `TopicReceipt.source_vector_generation` (R040.2.5) reports for the same request; `authorized_shortfall_counts` is the `Shortfall` of R039.1.4 (its `requested`, `found` and `missing` count caller-visible rows only) and `None` when the answer holds `k` rows; `encoding_calls` has one entry per call a registry encoder served for this request, in call order, where `model` is the encoder's `id`, `modality` the lowercase modality name it encoded, and `input_digest` the SHA-256 (64 lowercase hex) of the exact bytes the encoder received. Each model call appears once: `bind_modality_encoders` (R039.1.3) wraps every registry encoder in a recorder that pushes one `EncodingCall` per `embed` call, and `execute_ranked` moves the recorded list into the receipt. Mirror `TraceModality` and `EncodingCall` in `medulla/generated/models.py` beside `RetrievalReceipt`. Writes: the receipt returns in the response; no graph write. Depends: R039.1.3, R039.1.4, R040.1.5. Shape: the types above, the recorder in `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs` beside `bind_modality_encoders`, the python mirror. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_multimodal_served.py::test_receipt_matches_model_calls` (L4). It reads the receipt back and asserts the call list equals the recorded encoder invocations (one entry per call, digest of its input) and the permitted results. Status: planned; the test file does not exist yet. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.1.6` | **Multimodal space: hot-path proof.** Stage: query execution. Entry point: authenticated served `UnifiedQuery`. Caller chain: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `run_unified_off_lock` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:864`) → `run_unified_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:325`) → `execute_rows` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/query_support.rs:91`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) → `rank_top`. The test uses the registry encoders (R039.1.3) and persistent indexes. Decision: CI uses one tiny pinned test encoder per modality, checked in under `tests/retrieval/fixtures/encoders/`; the server under test loads them through `MEDULLA_EMBEDDING_ENCODER_DIR` set to that directory (R039.1.3), the same loader an operator may use in production. Pinned encoder file format and loader (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/embed_registry.rs`; it already depends on `serde` and `serde_json`). A pinned encoder is one file named `<anything>.encoder.json`; the loader ignores every other file name. The JSON object has exactly these keys, and any other key refuses (`#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`): `format` (integer, must be `1`), `id` (string, 1 to 128 chars: the registry id a space's `ModalityBinding.encoder` names), `modality` (one of `text`, `image`, `audio`, `video`), `space` (object with exactly `id` string, `dimensions` integer in `[1, 4096]`, `revision` string: the `(space id, dimensions, revision)` tuple the entry is registered for) and `tokens` (object from token string to an array of exactly `dimensions` finite numbers; 1 to 65,536 entries). A file larger than 16 MiB refuses. Encoding rule (the encoder implements `TextEmbedder`, so a query reaches it as text: the text itself, or the reference an image, audio or video query carries): split the input on ASCII whitespace, look each token up exactly and case-sensitively, add the vectors of the known tokens component-wise in `f32`, ignore unknown tokens, and L2-normalize the sum. An input with no known token, or a sum of zero norm, is `Err("EMBEDDING_UNAVAILABLE: no token of the input is known to encoder `<id>`")`. `impl ModalityEncoderRegistry { pub fn load_dir(&self, dir: &std::path::Path) -> Result<Vec<LoadedEncoder>, String> }` with `pub struct LoadedEncoder { pub id: String, pub modality: String, pub space_id: String, pub dimensions: usize, pub revision: String, pub file: String }` (derive `Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq`): it reads the directory's `*.encoder.json` files in ascending file-name order, validates each as above, registers each through `register(id, space.id, space.dimensions, space.revision, Box::new(PinnedEncoder))` and returns one `LoadedEncoder` per file in that order. A directory that cannot be read, a file that does not parse or validate, and a repeated id (within the directory or against an entry already registered) each return `Err("ENCODER_FILE_INVALID: <file>: <reason>")` (`<file>` is the file name, or the directory path for the directory itself) and register nothing from that call (validate every file first, register after). An empty directory returns `Ok(vec![])`. Fixtures, exactly four files in `tests/retrieval/fixtures/encoders/`: `text.encoder.json`, `image.encoder.json`, `audio.encoder.json`, `video.encoder.json`. All four use `"space": {"id": "pinned-test-space", "dimensions": 4, "revision": "1"}`, the ids `pinned-text`, `pinned-image`, `pinned-audio`, `pinned-video`, and a token table of exactly four tokens each mapping to one unit basis vector: concept `alpha` is `[1,0,0,0]`, `beta` `[0,1,0,0]`, `gamma` `[0,0,1,0]`, `delta` `[0,0,0,1]`. The tokens are `alpha beta gamma delta` for text and `img-alpha img-beta img-gamma img-delta`, `aud-...` and `vid-...` for the other three, so the same concept embeds to the same vector in every modality. Test setup: the server starts with `MEDULLA_EMBEDDING_ENCODER_DIR` pointing at that directory (the `server_env` of `tests/conftest.py`), the graph declares the space through `DeclareEmbeddingSpace` (R039.1.7) with the four bindings `ModalityBinding { modality, encoder: "pinned-<modality>" }`, and for each modality one node per concept is stored with `AddEmbedding` of the vector the pinned encoder gives that modality's reference token (`[1,0,0,0]`, ... as above). For each of the 16 (query modality, target modality) pairs the query is the query modality's `gamma` token and the first answer row must be the target modality's `gamma` node. Rank does not filter by modality, so the target is selected by label: the node of each modality carries node label `Item-<modality>` (for example `Item-image`), and each pair's plan is `[{"Scan": {"label": "Item-<target modality>"}}, {"Rank": {"query": <query vector>}}, {"Limit": {"k": 1}}]`, so the candidates of the pair are exactly the four concept nodes of the target modality and the first row is its `gamma` node. Shape: Add `tests/retrieval/test_multimodal_served.py` on `tests/_client_fixtures.py`. Checked-in encoder weights go under `tests/retrieval/fixtures/encoders/`. The test loads them through the registry loader of R039.1.3. Depends: R039.1.3, R039.1.4, R039.1.5, R039.1.7. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_multimodal_served.py::test_sixteen_modality_pairs` (L4). It proves expected cross-modal matches for all sixteen pairs. It refuses incompatible spaces and missing query bindings. It shows target shortfall and has one cross-tenant negative case. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.1.4` | **Constraints: refusal.** Stage: query execution. Caller chain: `compile_constraints`. Refusals: `CONSTRAINT_INVALID` (malformed or unsupported predicate), `GRAPH_REVISION_STALE`, `CONSTRAINT_UNEVALUABLE` (required constraint the planner cannot evaluate). A refusal ranks nothing. A hard exclusion never becomes a score penalty. An unsatisfiable valid conjunction returns the R039 `Shortfall` with `reason = eligible_exhausted` and zero rows. `graph_revision` `0` declares no pin; any other value must equal the revision the query reads, else `GRAPH_REVISION_STALE`; a pin with no revision to compare against is `CONSTRAINT_UNEVALUABLE`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Shape: The three codes join `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. `compile_constraints` returns them as the typed `ConstraintError` (`code()` is the registered code). The `eligible_exhausted` shortfall reuses the R039 `Shortfall` type. Depends: R040.1.2, R039 (built first: the `Shortfall` type and `replenish_until_k`). | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_constraints_served.py::test_refusals_rank_nothing` (L4) asserts that malformed predicates, a stale pin and an unevaluable pin rank nothing, and `tests/retrieval/test_constraints_served.py::test_unsatisfiable_constraints_return_eligible_exhausted` (L4) asserts that a contradictory valid conjunction returns zero rows and `Shortfall { requested, found: 0, reason: eligible_exhausted }`. |

Refusal: `ENCODE_POLICY_STALE` for an unsupported policy revision, a model revision other than the bound one, or a query policy digest with no generation under it (refused before ranking). Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: one `EncodedDocument` node holding the vector and its receipt, and one `EncodedIndexHead` node holding the generation per tenant and policy digest, through the staged graph of the query gateway so they commit together. Shape: `EncodeDocument` is a durable write method and `EncodedDocumentSearch` a read method; both are served from `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/document_encoding.rs`, and every encoder resolves through `ModalityEncoderRegistry` by the policy's `space: EmbeddingSpaceRef` (a multimodal space takes its text binding's encoder at the space's exact `(id, dimensions, revision)` tuple, a single-modality space the entry registered for that tuple; `model_revision` is only the label the receipt records); the default entry is `builtin:test-hashed-token/1` in space `builtin-test-hashed-token` (8 dimensions, revision `1`) (a position-aware hashed bag of token ids; two poison token ids make one chunk fail), registered at server startup. A document is `token_ids` or `text` (space-separated tokens), never both. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R077.6.2` | **Column advice: workload-first ranking.** Stage: SQL read. `rank_columns(input: &AdviceInput, policy: &AdvicePolicy) -> Result<Vec<ColumnVectorAdvice>, ColumnAdviceError>` is new in `crates/medulla-table-store/src/tables/column_advice.rs`. It is a pure function over its arguments. It holds no store handle and reads no counters itself. `AdviceInput { schema_revision: u64, window_start: i64, window_end: i64, columns: Vec<AdviceColumn> }`. `AdviceColumn { table, column, ty: ColumnType, observed_schema_revision: u64, text_search_uses: u64, avg_length: f64, distinct_ratio: f64 }`. `AdvicePolicy { min_samples, w_demand, w_length, w_distinct, length_norm, max_rows }` carries resolved knob values; the table-store crate cannot read the registry. Type admission runs first with `is_embeddable`, the one type test. A column that fails it is a row with `eligibility = unsupported_type` and `reason = "COLUMN_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED"`. Score = `w_demand * share + w_length * min(avg_length / length_norm, 1) + w_distinct * distinct_ratio`. `share` is the column's `text_search_uses` over the sum of all `text_search_uses` in the input. A positive score needs at least one text-search use. Ties break by `(table, column)`. Fewer than `min_samples` total uses makes every admitted row `insufficient_observations` with score 0. `recommend_vectorization`, `TextColumnStats` and `VectorizationRecommendation` (`embedding_binding.rs:681`) are deleted, with their re-exports in `tables/mod.rs`. Knobs in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs`: `sql_advice.min_samples` 50 [1, 100000] observations; `sql_advice.w_demand` 0.6, `.w_length` 0.25, `.w_distinct` 0.15, each [0, 1] weight; `sql_advice.length_norm` 512 [1, 65536] bytes; `sql_advice.max_rows` 1000 [1, 10000] rows. Writes: none. Depends: R077.6.1. It does not depend on R077.6.3; R077.6.3 depends on it. | Test: `crates/medulla-table-store/src/tables/column_advice.rs::tests::traffic_changes_rank_not_statistics` (L2). Verified by the root slice test `tests/retrieval/test_column_advice_served.py::test_workload_changes_advice`. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R077.6.3` | **Column advice: usage recording and the advice relation.** Stage: SQL read. Caller chain: served `Sql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_sql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:185`) → `handle_sql_read` (`sql_read.rs:100`) → `exec_sql_typed_with_tables_hive` (`crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/exec.rs:366`). A text-search use is a resolved user-table column that is an operand of `LIKE`, `ILIKE`, `NOT LIKE`, `NOT ILIKE` or `SIMILAR TO`. Exact filters, projections and joins are not text-search uses. `text_search_columns(plan) -> Vec<(String, String)>` is new in `crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/filter_shape.rs`. It walks the logical plan once and returns distinct `(table, column)` pairs. The SQL executor passes them to `ColumnUsageCounters::observe` on the store it reads. `ColumnUsageCounters` is new in `crates/medulla-table-store/src/tables/column_advice.rs`. `TableStore` holds one `Arc<ColumnUsageCounters>`, created when `open_or_get` (`crates/medulla-server-auth/src/sql_tables.rs:166`) first opens the tenant's cached handle. Every clone of the handle, including `AuthorizedReadStore`, shares it, so counters are per tenant and never shared. Each entry is keyed by `(table, column)` and holds `observed_schema_revision`, `text_search_uses`, `first_seen_ms` and `last_seen_ms`. The map is bounded by `sql_advice.max_columns` and evicts the least recently seen entry. Counters are process-local statistics, not facts. A restart opens a new window, and advice reports `insufficient_observations` until it fills. A DDL change on a table resets its entries. Uses older than the window are dropped on read. Knobs in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs`: `sql_advice.window_hours` 24 [1, 720] hours; `sql_advice.max_columns` 100000 [1, 1000000] columns; `sql_advice.stats_sample_rows` 10000 [1, 1000000] rows. Read: `information_schema.vector_advice`, synthesized per statement in `crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/vector_advice.rs` (new), like `medulla_index_status` (`crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/index_status.rs`). Columns: `table_name`, `column_name`, `schema_revision`, `window_start`, `window_end`, `sample_count`, `eligibility`, `reason`, `score`. It covers only tables the caller may `SELECT`. `avg_length` and `distinct_ratio` come from at most `stats_sample_rows` rows of the same authorized snapshot. Rows come from `rank_columns` (R077.6.2), bounded by `sql_advice.max_rows`. Writes: counters only; no binding, no queue. Depends: R077.6.2. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_column_advice_served.py::test_advice_relation_reads_production_observations` (L4). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R077.6.4` | **Column advice: refusal scoped to the question.** Stage: SQL read. Caller chain: `handle_sql_read` → `vector_advice.rs` → `rank_columns` (R077.6.2). A pinned read names one column with `table_name = ... AND column_name = ...` on `information_schema.vector_advice`. A pinned read of a column that fails `is_embeddable` refuses `COLUMN_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED`. A pinned read whose counters carry an `observed_schema_revision` other than the snapshot's schema revision refuses `SCHEMA_REVISION_STALE`. `ColumnAdviceError` is a closed `Copy` enum (`ColumnTypeUnsupported`, `SchemaRevisionStale`) in `column_advice.rs` that carries these two codes. A listing never refuses on account of another column. It shows an unembeddable column as `unsupported_type` and a stale one as `insufficient_observations`. Advice checks no embedding model. Model checks belong to binding admission (R077.2). Both codes join a `Sql` entry in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Advice creates no binding and queues no embedding. Writes: none. Depends: R077.6.1, R077.6.2. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_column_advice_served.py::test_unembeddable_types_refuse_without_side_effects` (L4). A pinned integer column refuses, a listing shows it as `unsupported_type`, and zero embedding side effects are asserted. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R077.6.5` | **Column advice: evidence.** Stage: SQL read. Caller chain: served `Sql` → `handle_sql_read` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:101`) → `exec_sql_typed_with_tables_hive` (`crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/exec.rs:367`) → `read_advice` (`crates/medulla-sql/src/sql/vector_advice.rs:159`) → `rank_columns` (`crates/medulla-table-store/src/tables/column_advice.rs:91`) → `register` (`vector_advice.rs:257`). Decision: the receipt is a field of each advice row, not a second relation, a pair or a second return value. `rank_columns` keeps its signature `-> Result<Vec<ColumnVectorAdvice>, ColumnAdviceError>` and fills `ColumnVectorAdvice.receipt` (new field, `crates/medulla-table-store/src/tables/embedding_binding.rs:406`). Shape: `AdviceReceipt { total_text_search_uses: u64, policy_revision: String, demand: f64, length: f64, distinct: f64 }` is new beside `ColumnVectorAdvice` in `embedding_binding.rs`, a plain serde struct with `deny_unknown_fields`, re-exported from `tables/mod.rs`. The row already carries the per-column count (`sample_count`), the window (`window_start`, `window_end`) and `schema_revision`; the receipt carries only what the row lacks, so row and receipt cannot disagree. `total_text_search_uses` is the sum of `text_search_uses` over the input (the denominator of `share`). `policy_revision` is `AdvicePolicy::revision(&self) -> String` (new, `column_advice.rs`): `format!("sql_advice/v1 min_samples={} w_demand={} w_length={} w_distinct={} length_norm={} max_rows={}", ...)` with Rust `Display` of each field, so a changed knob is a changed revision. For an `eligible` row, `demand = w_demand * share`, `length = w_length * min(avg_length / length_norm, 1)`, `distinct = w_distinct * distinct_ratio`, and `score` is computed as `demand + length + distinct`. Every other row has all three at `0.0` and score `0`. The response is the relation: `vector_advice.rs` `COLUMNS` gains, after `score`, `total_text_search_uses` (Int64), `policy_revision` (Utf8), `demand_contribution`, `length_contribution` and `distinct_contribution` (Float64, not null). No field holds query text. Rows cover only tables the caller may `SELECT`, so hidden tables and their columns never appear. Writes: none. Depends: R077.6.2, R077.6.3. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_column_advice_served.py::test_receipt_matches_observations_binding_unchanged` (L4). After real `LIKE` queries, each eligible row's `sample_count` equals the uses issued, `total_text_search_uses` equals their sum, the three contributions sum to `score`, `policy_revision` names the resolved knobs, and the bindings list is unchanged. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.2.2` | **Topic fusion: behavior.** Stage: query execution. Caller chain: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `run_unified_off_lock` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:864`) → `run_unified_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:325`) → `execute_rows` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/query_support.rs:91`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) → `fuse_topic_dense` (new, `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/topic.rs`). Policy `weighted_topic_dense`: `score = (1 - w) * dense + w * topic`, `w` in `[0, 1]`. `dense` is the min-max normalized similarity over the candidate set. `topic` is the sum over the query's kept topics of the topic's query responsibility times the candidate document's responsibility for that topic (the `DocumentTopicAssignment.responsibility` of R040.2.7, in `[0, 1]`). Ties break by candidate id ascending. Hard constraints run before topic scoring. Knob: `knob retrieval.topic_weight` default 0.3, `[0, 1]`, unit ratio. Refusal: `TOPIC_WEIGHT_INVALID` for a non-finite or out-of-range `w`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Shape: `fuse_topic_dense` is new in `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/topic.rs`. It is a pure function over the candidate set and the backend result. The weight knob is read by the R179 knob reader. Depends: R040.2.1, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R179. | Test: `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/topic.rs::tests::order_matches_oracle_w0_w1_mid` (L2). With no graph revision bound the fusion refuses `TOPIC_BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE`. Equal dimension alone is not compatible. Each refuses before fusion. The planner never drops the requested topic contribution silently. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none.
