| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.2.5` | **Topic fusion: evidence.** Class: BUILD. Stage: query execution. Caller chain: `fuse_topic_dense` → `TopicReceipt` (new, `crates/medulla-types/src/retrieval.rs`). `RetrievalReceipt` (`crates/medulla-types/src/retrieval.rs`, built by R040.1.5; fields so far `constraint`, plus `modality` from R039.1.5) gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] pub topic: Option<TopicReceipt>`, set exactly when the request carried `retrieval.topic`. New in the same file (derives as `TraceConstraint`): `TopicReceipt { backend_id: String, backend_revision: u64, centroid_generation: u64, space_tuple: String, responsibilities: Vec<TopicResponsibility>, component_scores: Vec<TopicComponentScore>, fusion_policy: TopicFusionPolicy, source_vector_generation: u64 }`, `TopicResponsibility { topic_id: String, weight_micros: u32 }`, `TopicComponentScore { candidate_id: String, dense_micros: i64, topic_micros: i64, final_micros: i64 }` and `TopicFusionPolicy { name: String, weight_micros: u32 }`; `TopicReceipt` also carries `assignments: Vec<TopicAssignmentTrace>` with `TopicAssignmentTrace { candidate_id: String, topic_id: String, content_revision: u64, responsibility_micros: u32 }` (R040.2.7: one entry per row of the final answer and kept topic, in final order and then `topic_id` ascending; `responsibility_micros` is the responsibility times 1,000,000 rounded half away from zero). The receipt holds ids and numbers only; every real number is an integer in millionths, rounded half away from zero, so a replay is byte-equal. Field meanings: `backend_id` is the backend's stable id, the constant `stored_centroid` for `StoredCentroidTopicBackend` (the test-only `InMemoryTopicBackend` reports `in_memory` and never appears in a served answer). `backend_revision` is the graph revision of the read snapshot the centroid nodes were read at (`GraphCore::version()` of the view; the value R040.2.3 compares with the request's corpus revision). `centroid_generation` is the `corpus_revision` the centroid nodes carry, which equals `TopicRequest.corpus_revision` because any other value refused `TOPIC_CORPUS_STALE`. `space_tuple` is `<id>|<dimensions>|<revision>` of the retriever `EmbeddingSpaceRef` (its `id`, `dimensions` and `revision` fields, joined by `|`); the backend's space equals it because any other value refused `TOPIC_SPACE_MISMATCH`. `responsibilities` are the topics that took part after `top_topics` and `min_weight` of `TopicRequest` were applied, heaviest first, equal weights by `topic_id` ascending; `weight_micros` is the topic weight times 1,000,000, within `[0, 1_000_000]`. `component_scores` has one entry per row of the final answer, in final order: `dense_micros` is the min-max normalized dense similarity of R040.2.2 over the whole eligible candidate set, `topic_micros` is the sum over the kept topics of the topic's query responsibility times the candidate's `DocumentTopicAssignment.responsibility` for that topic (R040.2.7), and `final_micros` is the score the row was ordered by. `fusion_policy.name` is the constant `weighted_topic_dense` and `weight_micros` is `w` times 1,000,000 as resolved from the request or the knob `retrieval.topic_weight`. `source_vector_generation` is the generation counter of the maintained vector index the dense scores were read from, equal to `TraceModality.index_generation` (R039.1.5) for the same request, and to 0 when the dense scores came from an exact scan of embeddings held on nodes with no maintained index; the value is `SemanticStore::generation()` of the read snapshot in both cases, so the exact-scan nuance is not implemented separately (decided: one counter). Reproduction rule (what the test checks): for every entry, `|final_micros - ((1_000_000 - w) * dense_micros + w * topic_micros) / 1_000_000| <= 1` with `w = fusion_policy.weight_micros` (integer arithmetic, truncating division), and the entries are ordered by `final_micros` descending, equal scores by `candidate_id` ascending. `fuse_topic_dense` returns the receipt beside the scores and `execute_ranked` copies it into `RetrievalReceipt.topic`. Mirror the five types in `medulla/generated/models.py`. Writes: response only. Depends: R040.2.3, R040.1.5. Shape: the types above in `retrieval.rs`, the fill in `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/topic.rs`. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_topic_fusion_served.py::test_receipt_reproduces_final_score` (L4). It recomputes each final score from the receipt by the reproduction rule and confirms constraints stayed effective (no candidate excluded by `constraint` appears in `component_scores`). |

The served backend is `StoredCentroidTopicBackend`, the only one served; backend interchange is proven in Rust by R040.2.1 against the test-only `InMemoryTopicBackend` and is not re-proven over the wire. Shape: Add `tests/retrieval/test_topic_fusion_served.py` on `tests/_client_fixtures.py`; the corpus and its centroids load through the real ingest path. Space and encoder: the graph declares one single-modality space through `DeclareEmbeddingSpace` (R039.1.7) with the tuple of `pinned-text` (R039.1.6: `pinned-test-space`, 4, `1`); document vectors are stored with `AddEmbedding` and centroids are `TopicCentroid` nodes (properties `topic`, `centroid`) written with `AddNode`. `UnifiedQuery.retrieval.topic` carries `TopicRequest.space` equal to the declared space and `corpus_revision` equal to the graph revision. Tests never parse error text. No existing served read returns the graph revision (readiness and stats rows carry none), so the `TOPIC_CORPUS_STALE` refusal carries it as a structured detail: the unified-query handler answers a `TopicRefusal::CorpusStale { topics, current }` with `Response::err(id, <text as today>)` whose `result` is the msgpack map `{"topic_corpus_revision": topics, "current_corpus_revision": current}` (both `u64`), the channel the placement redirect already uses (`_send_error_detail` in `medulla/client.py`); `medulla/client.py` raises, before the generic path, `TopicCorpusStaleError(EngineResponseError)` with int attributes `topic_corpus_revision` and `current_corpus_revision` when `code == "TOPIC_CORPUS_STALE"` and the decoded detail holds both integer keys. The helper `_corpus_revision` of `tests/retrieval/test_topic_fusion_served.py` catches `TopicCorpusStaleError` and returns `current_corpus_revision`; the `re.search(r"the corpus is at (\d+)")` parse is deleted, so a change of the refusal text breaks no test. Depends: R039.1.7, R040.2.3, R040.2.4, R040.2.5, R040.1.7 (the served `UnifiedQuery.retrieval` carrier). | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_topic_fusion_served.py::test_topic_fusion_over_stored_centroids` (L4). It proves the score effect of a topic weight over the stored centroids and refuses incompatible centroids, stale generations and cross-tenant topics. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R085.2` | **Behavior: issue approval through a dedicated native issuer.** Subsystem: sources. Method: `ApproveSourceQuery { request: Box<ApproveSourceQueryRequest>, source_approval: Box<ConnectorPackSourceApprovalResult> }` (new, `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_05.rs`, beside `ForeignReadOpen`). `source_approval` is the caller's connector-pack source-approval receipt (`crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/connector_pack/result.rs:308`), a candidate reference that admission re-verifies; apply ignores it. Types, new in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/source_query_approval.rs` beside `host_start.rs`: `SOURCE_QUERY_APPROVAL_KIND = "source.query.approval"`. `ApproveSourceQueryRequest { tenant: String, query: SourceQueryRequest, #[serde(default)] admission: Option<SourceQueryAdmission> }`. `SourceQueryAdmission { approval_id, tenant_ref, owner_ref, actor_ref, authority_digest, policy_digest, identity_digest: String, approval_ttl_ms: u64 }`. `SourceQueryApprovalGrant { schema_version: u16, approval_id, query_id, source_ref, tenant_ref, owner_ref, actor_ref, authority_digest, policy_digest, identity_digest, operation_digest, parameter_digest, query_digest: String }`. `SourceQueryApprovalIssued { outcome: ControlLeaseIssueOutcome, approved: Option<ApprovedSourceQuery>, changed_work_item_ids: Vec<String> }`. All carry `deny_unknown_fields`. Caller chain: wire dispatch → `route_native_lifecycle_ops` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/native_routes.rs:73`, after `delegation::try_handle`) → `source_query_approval::try_handle` (new, `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/source_query_approval.rs`, compiled under `federation-sql`; its `HandleContext` adds `state` to the fields of `work_item::HandleContext`) → `admit_source_query` (same file) → `work_item::try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item.rs:42`) → `commit_verified_work_item` (`work_item.rs:126`) → `apply_native_publication` (`crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:383`) → `source_query::issue` (new, `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/control_lease/source_query.rs`). Authority derivation, one function for issue and verify: `source_query_authority(verified: &VerifiedRequestContext, graph: &str) -> Result<SourceQueryAuthority, String>` (new, `crates/medulla-server-api/src/source_query_authority.rs`). `SourceQueryAuthority { tenant_ref, owner_ref, actor_ref, authority_digest, policy_digest, identity_digest: String }`. With `carrier = CarrierAuthority::from_verified(verified)?`: `tenant_ref = verified.tenant()`; `owner_ref = carrier.owner_scope()`; `actor_ref = carrier.actor_scope()`. `authority_digest = Digest256::framed(b"eg/source-query-authority/v1", &[carrier.authority_digest().as_bytes(), graph.as_bytes()])`; the carrier digest already binds tenant, principal, agent, delegation, policy version, roles and scopes. `policy_digest = Digest256::framed(b"eg/source-query-policy/v1", &[verified.claims().policy_version.as_bytes()])`. `identity_digest = Digest256::framed(b"eg/source-query-identity/v1", &[verified.tenant(), verified.principal_persistence_id(), verified.agent_id(), carrier.delegation_digest().as_bytes()])` (strings as UTF-8). Every digest in R085 is lowercase hex from `Digest256::to_hex`. Admission (`admit_source_query`), in order, before any commit: (1) overwrite `request.query.idempotency_key` with `verified.idempotency_key()`; `SourceQueryRequest::validate` (R085.1); `validate_source_query_sql(dialect, sql)` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation.rs:1266`, R085.4); `query.limits = query.limits.clamp_to_ceiling(&source_query_knobs().ceiling())?` (R085.6). Failures keep their `SourceQueryError` kind. (2) `state.foreign_sources().verify_registered_for(&carrier, store, &source_approval, &query.source_ref)` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs:418`), where `carrier = CarrierAuthority::from_verified(ctx.verified_context)` and `store = state.agent_library()` (the same pair `open_foreign_read`, `foreign_source_handlers.rs:327`, uses). No Agent Library refuses `verification_unavailable`; any `Err` refuses `unauthorized`. (3) `query.schema_digest == source_approval.contract_digest.to_hex()`. (4) `query.source_revision == source_approval.committed_version.to_string()`. (5) `query.snapshot_ref == source_approval.head.pack_digest.to_hex()`, the pinned snapshot rule `submission_evidence` (`foreign_source_handlers.rs:271`) applies to foreign reads. (3) to (5) refuse `stale_source`. (6) `admission = Some(SourceQueryAdmission { .. })` from `source_query_authority(ctx.verified_context, ctx.graph_name)`, with `approval_ttl_ms = source_query_knobs().approval_ttl_s * 1000` and `approval_id = "source-query-approval:" + Digest256::framed(b"eg/source-query-approval-id/v1", &[tenant_ref, owner_ref, idempotency_key]).to_hex()`. A wire value of `admission` is overwritten, as `stamp_retired_by` does. Digests, new on the R085.1 types in `source_query.rs`; each `Err` of `framed` maps to `invalid_query`. Integers are fixed-width big-endian; enums are their serde snake_case name. `SourceQueryRequest::parameter_digest()`: `framed(b"eg/source-query-parameters/v1", fields)`, three fields per parameter in order: `position` (u32), kind name (`null`, `bool`, `int64`, `float64`, `text`, `bytes`), value bytes (Null empty, Bool one byte 0 or 1, Int64 i64, Float64 `f64::to_bits` u64, Text UTF-8, Bytes raw). `SourceQueryRequest::operation_digest()`: `framed(b"eg/source-query-operation/v1", [schema_version (u16), source_ref, source_revision, schema_digest, snapshot_ref, dialect name, sql, parameter_digest hex, max_rows, max_bytes, chunk_rows, chunk_bytes, queue_depth, queue_bytes, deadline_ms, max_resident_bytes, requested_expires_at_ms (each u64)])`. `idempotency_key` is excluded, so one operation has one digest. `ApprovedSourceQuery::compute_query_digest()`: `framed(b"eg/source-query-approval/v1", [schema_version (u16), approval_id, query_id, operation_digest, parameter_digest, tenant_ref, owner_ref, actor_ref, authority_digest, policy_digest, identity_digest, one field per `allowed_operations` name, issued_at_ms, expires_at_ms, hard_expires_at_ms (each u64)])`. It binds every field but `query_digest`; the request is bound through `operation_digest`. `query_id = operation_digest`. `approval_id` is the lease id. Issue: `ApproveSourceQueryRequest::lease_at(&self, committed_at_ms: u64) -> Result<(IssueControlLeaseRequest, ApprovedSourceQuery), String>`. `admission: None` refuses `ACCESS_DENIED`. `issued_at_ms = committed_at_ms`, `hard_expires_at_ms = committed_at_ms + approval_ttl_ms`, `expires_at_ms = min(query.requested_expires_at_ms, hard_expires_at_ms)`. `expires_at_ms <= issued_at_ms` refuses `invalid_query`. `allowed_operations = [ReadSql]`. The lease is `{ tenant, lease_id: approval_id, kind: SOURCE_QUERY_APPROVAL_KIND, grant: { "source_query_approval": SourceQueryApprovalGrant }, pending_call_registration: None, idempotency_key: query.idempotency_key }`, checked with `validate_body()` (not `validate()`). Decision: the grant holds identity and digests only, never SQL text, parameter values or credentials, because `GetControlLease` and `ListControlLeases` serve grants to `lease:read` holders. The SQL and parameters travel in the returned `ApprovedSourceQuery`. `source_query::issue(graph, request, admitted_actor, nodes, crypto, committed_at_ms) -> Result<SourceQueryApprovalIssued, String>` mirrors `host_start::issue` (`control_lease/host_start.rs`). `admitted_actor` is `batch.envelope.operation().authority.actor`; `None` refuses `ACCESS_DENIED`. An existing lease id returns `outcome: Collision`, `approved: None`, and writes nothing. Success writes `lease.row()` and returns `Issued` with the approval. `apply_native_publication` gains an `ApproveSourceQuery` arm beside `IssueHostStartGrant` with the same one-operation rule. Generic issue stays closed: `IssueControlLeaseRequest::validate` (`crates/medulla-work-types/src/control_lease.rs:365`) refuses `SOURCE_QUERY_APPROVAL_KIND` with `ACCESS_DENIED`, as for `host.start`, so `apply_issue_control_lease_row` never issues it. TTL knob: `SOURCE_QUERY_APPROVAL_TTL_KNOB = SourceQueryKnob { knob: "source_query.approval_ttl_s", env: "MEDULLA_SOURCE_QUERY_APPROVAL_TTL_S", default: 300, range: (10, 3600) }` and `SourceQueryKnobs.approval_ttl_s: u64` (`crates/medulla-work-types/src/scheduler_config.rs:906`), read and range-checked by `from_lookup`. Refusals reply as `SourceQueryFailure` (R085.7) with `failed_step: approval`, `query_identity` the request's `operation_digest` and `last_chunk: None`. External SQL never runs during approval. Writes: one control-lease row. Registration (the native work-item write recipe; `IssueHostStartGrant` is the template at every site, and `ApproveSourceQuery` goes beside it in each): (r1) the variant in `method_05.rs`, not feature-gated because `medulla-protocol-types` has no `federation-sql` feature and its request types are unconditional dependencies; a build without `federation-sql` refuses the method in `route_native_lifecycle_ops` (`ApproveSourceQuery requires a federation-sql build`) so a wire `admission` is never committed; (r2) `MethodWriteFamily::WorkItem` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/families.rs:67`, which also makes `carrier_refusal` answer `NativeAuthority`, so the method cannot ride inside a generic carrier (add `approve_source_query_cannot_be_hidden_inside_a_generic_carrier` beside `host_start_cannot_be_hidden_inside_a_generic_carrier` in `carrier.rs`); (r3) the result contract `ApproveSourceQuery(ApproveSourceQuery) => Json<SourceQueryApprovalIssued>` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/result_contract/coordination.rs:132`; (r4) the policy row in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/coordination.rs:59`: `("ApproveSourceQuery", spec(make_policy(true, DurabilityDomain::GraphRedb, "source:approve-query", PolicyFlags { idempotent: true, audited: true, emits_cdc: false }, TxnParticipation::Atomic), PYTHON, Stability::Stable), "immutable source-query approval lease with native issuer provenance; the grant holds digests only")`, and the three name lists of `crates/medulla-capabilities/tests/consistency/snapshots.rs` (`:45`, `:313`, `:587`); (r5) write classification `("ApproveSourceQuery", "dedicated issuer-bound source-query approval in protected ControlLease storage")` in `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation.rs:794`, the domain row `("ApproveSourceQuery", "ControlPlane")` in `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation_batch/tests.rs:468`, and the tenant arm `Method::ApproveSourceQuery { request, .. } => Ok(request.tenant.clone())` in `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation_batch/commit/work_item.rs:122`; (r6) the idempotency arm `Method::ApproveSourceQuery { request, .. } => Ok(request.query.idempotency_key.clone())` in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/digest.rs:280`; (r7) the tenant arm `vec![request.tenant.as_str()]` in `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/native_write_authority.rs:97` (and its list in `native_write_authority/tests.rs:83`), the `work_item::try_handle` match arm in `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item.rs:53`, and `LeaseTarget::Kind(SOURCE_QUERY_APPROVAL_KIND)` in `lease_target` of `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item/lease_kind_policy.rs:51` (no extra issuer check: admission step (2) is the authority); (r8) the audit line `Method::ApproveSourceQuery { request, .. } => Some(format!("APPROVE_SOURCE_QUERY\|{}\|{}", request.tenant, approval_id))` in `crates/medulla-kernel/src/audit/control_lines.rs:615`, where `approval_id` is the stamped `admission` id (`None` prints `-`); (r9) `record ApproveSourceQuery => WorkItem` in `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/catalog.rs:69` and its list in `native/tests.rs:310`; (r10) the macro arm in `work_item_kernel_writes!` (`crates/medulla-store/src/lib.rs:24`); (r11) the two op lists of `crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs` (`:100` and `:593`); (r12) the hand-written client `async def approve_source_query(self, request: Mapping[str, Any], source_approval: Mapping[str, Any]) -> dict` in `medulla/client.py` beside `issue_host_start` (`:4872`), validating both are mappings and calling the regenerated `_gen.coordination.send_approve_source_query`, then regenerate `medulla/generated/*.py`. No new `ServerErrorCode` is added: the refusals are the R085.7 `SourceQueryFailure` codes and `ACCESS_DENIED`. Depends: R085.1, R085.4, R085.6, R085.7, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R075. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item/source_query_tests.rs::approval_requires_authority_and_native_commit` (L3). An unregistered source refuses `SOURCE_QUERY_UNAUTHORIZED`. A changed schema digest refuses `SOURCE_QUERY_STALE_SOURCE`. A generic `IssueControlLease` of `source.query.approval` refuses `ACCESS_DENIED`. A wire `admission` is replaced by the verified one. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R085.3` | **Behavior: verify approval.** Subsystem: sources. Method: `VerifySourceQueryApproval { request: Box<SourceQueryApprovalCheck>, source_approval: Box<ConnectorPackSourceApprovalResult> }` (new, `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_05.rs`). The receipt is the candidate reference; the durable Agent Library record is the authority, as for `ForeignReadOpen`. Caller chain: wire dispatch → `route_process_global_domains` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/pipeline.rs:343`) → `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/foreign_source_handlers.rs:133`) → `verify_source_query_approval(call: OwnerScopedCall<'_>, request, source_approval, target: &ContractWriteTarget<'_>)` (new, same file). Order, with `approved = &request.approved_query` and `now = medulla_server_api::txn::now_ms()` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/txn.rs:167`): (1) `owner = call.owner("VerifySourceQueryApproval")`; `!owner.can_read()` refuses `ACCESS_DENIED`. (2) `approved.validate()` refuses `invalid_query`. (3) Recompute `parameter_digest`, `operation_digest`, `query_id` and `compute_query_digest()` (R085.2); any difference refuses `unauthorized`. (4) Lease: `target.persistence.and_then(as_redb)`, absent, or an `Err` from `read_control_lease(&sanitize(target.graph), verified.tenant(), &approved.approval_id)` (`crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/work_item_read.rs:82`, which calls `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/control_lease.rs:181`) refuses `verification_unavailable`. The key is `(sanitized request graph, verified tenant, approval_id)`. `None`, a kind other than `SOURCE_QUERY_APPROVAL_KIND`, or a `grant["source_query_approval"]` that does not decode as `SourceQueryApprovalGrant` equal to the one `approved` implies refuses `unauthorized`. (5) Status: `Revoked` or `Consumed` refuses `approval_revoked`; `Expired`, `now >= expires_at_ms` or `now >= hard_expires_at_ms` refuses `approval_expired`. (6) Caller: `source_query_authority(verified, target.graph)` (R085.2) is recomputed from this request; any field unequal to the stored grant refuses `unauthorized`. So a policy-version change, another principal, agent or delegation chain, or another graph refuses. (7) Source: `call.state.read().await.agent_library()` absent refuses `verification_unavailable`; `state.foreign_sources().verify_registered_for(&owner, store, &source_approval, &approved.request.source_ref)` failing refuses `unauthorized`. (8) The admission checks (3) to (5) of R085.2 rerun against `source_approval`, and `observed_source_revision`, `observed_schema_digest` and `observed_snapshot_ref` must equal the approved request's; a difference refuses `stale_source`. Success returns `SourceQueryApprovalValidity { approval_id, query_id, query_digest, lease_revision: lease.revision, checked_at_ms: now, valid_until_ms: min(lease.expires_at_ms, lease.hard_expires_at_ms) }`. `lease_revision` is the lease row's CAS revision (`ControlLeaseView.revision`). A presented lease view is never read. Refusals reply as `SourceQueryFailure` (R085.7) with `failed_step: verification`, `query_identity = approved.query_digest` (when (2) fails, the recomputed `operation_digest`) and `last_chunk: None`. It is a read: it writes nothing, grants no SQL effect and mints no bearer credential. Depends: R085.2, R085.7. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/admin/connector_pack/tests/source_approval.rs::changed_or_revoked_approval_refuses` (L3; the file exists and is the crate's federation test module). Changing any request field, digest or expiry refuses `SOURCE_QUERY_UNAUTHORIZED`. Another principal refuses `SOURCE_QUERY_UNAUTHORIZED`. A revoked or consumed lease refuses `SOURCE_QUERY_APPROVAL_REVOKED`. An expired lease refuses `SOURCE_QUERY_APPROVAL_EXPIRED`. A missing lease refuses `SOURCE_QUERY_UNAUTHORIZED`. A changed observed revision refuses `SOURCE_QUERY_STALE_SOURCE`. Success writes nothing. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R085.4` | **Behavior: read-only SQL.** Subsystem: sources. Caller chain: `ApproveSourceQuery` → `validate_federated_sql` in `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation.rs` → the R069 rendering helpers `render_sql_columns` in `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt/columns.rs`. Medulla refuses by classification: DDL, DML, COPY, external-table creation, multiple statements, locking reads and unknown functions or dialects. Typed parameters stay separate from SQL text. The descriptor requires `read_only_transaction: true`. The SDK must open a read-only database transaction (`SCION-SOURCE-INGEST-R004.8`). Medulla cannot enforce that inside the driver. Refusals: `unsupported_dialect`, `invalid_query`. Shape: the one read-only audit `audit_read_only_sql` in `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation.rs` (shared with `validate_federated_sql`, which keeps its messages) refuses multi-statement, locking-read, `SELECT INTO`, write-shaped and table-valued forms. The public `validate_source_query_sql(dialect, sql)` runs it with the closed read-only function allowlist and maps to `SourceQueryError`: a dialect the audit does not certify (only Postgres and MySQL are) refuses `unsupported_dialect`, everything else `invalid_query`. Parameters are bound by position in `SourceQueryRequest` and never appear in the statement text. The test lives in `federation_sql_tests.rs` because the audit is behind `federation-sql`. Depends: R085.1. | Test: `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_sql_tests.rs::approved_source_query_has_no_write_effects` (L1) and the root slice test. Unknown dialects, functions or effects refuse without issuing approval. |

Readers call `source_query_knobs()`, which serves defaults when nothing was installed. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R006.1` | **Temporal rule-derived facts with persisted origin chain.** Stage: reasoning write, served `RunTemporalRules`. `Uql` is `mutates: false`, so it never carries a rule and never writes. Method: `RunTemporalRules { text: String }` (new, `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_02.rs`, beside `RunDatalogReasoning`). Policy: `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/reasoning.rs`, `mutates: true`, `DurabilityDomain::GraphRedb`, `authz_action "reasoning:write"`, idempotent, audited, emits CDC. Caller chain: served `RunTemporalRules` → `try_handle_gateway` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway.rs:57`) → `try_handle_reasoning_policy` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph_routes.rs`) → `commit_gateway` (`medulla_server_ops::api::ingest::graph`) → `apply_run_temporal_rules` (new, `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph.rs`) → `TemporalRule::parse` and `derive` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/rules/temporal.rs:73`, `:195`). This mirrors the `RunDatalogReasoning` materialization route; it adds no second write route. Rule text: `RULE temporal-rules-v1 <head> <- <body> & <body> ...` (`TEMPORAL_RULES_VERSION`). Premise mapping: each live edge whose `relationship` equals a body predicate is one `Premise`. `id` is the edge id, `predicate` is `relationship`, `subject` is the source node id, `valid_from` is `valid_from`. `valid_to` is `valid_until`, or `i64::MAX` when it is absent. An edge with `tx_to` set is not live. `origin` comes from the edge properties `origin_kind` (`sentence` or `axiom`) and `origin_ref`; a missing property is `None`. `DeriveContext.may_write_derived` is the graph write check `check_graph_access` (`crates/medulla-server-auth/src/access.rs:105`) on the request graph. `graph_generation` is the read version. Write: each `DerivedFactRecord` becomes one node, id `derived:<fact_id>`, label `DerivedFact`, with every record field as a property. The writes go through the state-backed `MutationBatch` of `commit_gateway`, like `apply_run_datalog_reasoning`. An existing `DerivedFact` node with the same `fact_id` is left unchanged. A stored `DerivedFact` of the same `rule_digest` that the run no longer derives gets the `retraction` revision. Hard cutover: `derive_facts_mutation`, `DerivedWrite`, `record_bytes` and `DERIVED_FACT_SCHEMA` are deleted; `MutationPayload` has no served consumer. Reply: `RunTemporalRulesResult { rule_digest, graph_generation, committed_version, facts: Vec<DerivedFactRecord>, trace: Vec<DerivationStep> }` in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/derived_fact.rs`. `DerivationStep` moves to that file with serde derives and `deny_unknown_fields`; no alias stays in `medulla-plan`. Refusals: `RULE_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED`, `DERIVED_ORIGIN_MISSING`, `DERIVED_WINDOW_CONFLICT`, `DERIVED_WRITE_UNAUTHORIZED`, `DERIVED_BOUND_MALFORMED`. They move from the `Uql` entry to a `RunTemporalRules` entry in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Every refusal leaves the graph version unchanged. Query proof premises are not a fact producer. Depends: R006, R011, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R003. | Test: `tests/forward_chain_served.rs::chain_covers_model_order_origin_refusal_restart_and_trace` (L3). Two premise edges carry sentence and axiom origins. It covers matching and nonmatching intervals and time-ordered chaining. It reads back the `DerivedFact` node after a restart, checks the reply trace, and asserts each refusal leaves the version unchanged. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5` | **Low-storage ANN selectivity path (rollup).** Stage: query execution and ingest, served `UnifiedQuery`. Root family: retrieval. Children `.1` to `.6` deliver the bounded low-storage ANN. Memory admission, relations and retention belong to `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085` (served `ApplyMutation`, `MemorySnapshot` keyed by tenant, memory id and source digest). This row declares only the retrieval cost bound. Bound: rows visited at most `4 * result_rows + 64` at corpus sizes 1000, 10000 and 100000. Filtered scans (R040) stay separate coverage. This row claims no model-weight change and no forgetting property. Depends: R040, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085. | The root journey test is `tests/retrieval/test_low_storage_ann_served.py::test_selectivity_bound_and_readback` (L4). Nightly group `retrieval/selectivity` reports rows, bytes and visits. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R083.7` | **RETIRED: duplicate of the arbitration behavior and model rows.** Support-aware arbitration and the new fields now live in `R083.1` and `R083.2`. | Retired; see `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R083.2`. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.10` | **Clock basis and bounded second pass.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, second-pass step. Caller chain: wire `Method::RetrievalRound` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_retrieval_round` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/retrieval_round.rs`) → `run_round` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs`) → `choose_clock` and `plan_second_pass` in new `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_clock.rs`. R081.8 owns follow-up expansion. This row adds two things. First, three typed clock fields on evidence rows: `storage_time`, `event_time` and `narrative_time`, each with source and provenance. When values disagree the precedence is `event_time`, then `narrative_time`, then `storage_time`. The result reports the chosen clock basis and the policy revision `clock-precedence`. No other heuristic picks a clock. Second, a bounded second pass. Sufficiency is the fraction of requested slots filled with supported evidence. Below `knob retrieval.second_pass_threshold` (0.5, [0, 1], fraction), `plan_second_pass` builds at most `knob retrieval.second_pass_queries` (3, [1, 8], queries) from the unmet slots. The result lists them. The harness runs one second round. Exhausted query budget returns a partial result with `RETRIEVAL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`. Narrative time comes from the transcript ingest of MEDULLA-MEDIA-VOCAB; this row reads those fields. On main: `choose_clock`, `clock_premise` and `plan_second_pass` are pure functions in `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_clock.rs` with `CLOCK_POLICY_REVISION`. Wire (WIRE work), decided here. Per-row clocks are not request fields: they are read on the round's snapshot from each evidence node's properties `storage_time`, `event_time`, `narrative_time` (Unix ms integers) with their `<clock>_source` and `<clock>_provenance` companions, the names `SEGMENT_CLOCKS` (`crates/medulla-source-types/src/source_transcript.rs:191`) writes. A new `fn row_clocks(view: &GraphView, id: &str) -> EvidenceClocks` in `retrieval_clock.rs` decodes them; a non-integer value is absent. Move the wire types `Clock`, `ClockValue`, `EvidenceClocks` and `ClockBasis` from `retrieval_clock.rs` to new `crates/medulla-result-types/src/retrieval_clock.rs` (the plan crate re-imports them; the functions stay) so the result can carry them. Result rows: `RoundRow` (`crates/medulla-result-types/src/retrieval_round.rs:399`) gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] clock: Option<ClockBasis>`, filled by `run_round_with_state` for every returned row, and each row with `disagreement: true` adds its `clock_premise` to `RoundResult.premises`. Requested slots: `RoundRequest` (`retrieval_round.rs:292`) gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] second_pass: Option<SecondPassPart>` with `SecondPassPart { slots: BoundedVec<SlotRequest, 32> }` and `SlotRequest { name: String, label: String, query: String }`, all `deny_unknown_fields`, names unique and non-empty. A slot is filled when at least one returned row or `hierarchy_selected` id is a node whose `type`, `node_type` or `label` property equals `slot.label`. The engine builds `RequestedSlot { name, filled, query }` and calls `plan_second_pass(&slots, knobs.second_pass_threshold, knobs.second_pass_queries)`. Planned queries: `RoundResult` gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] second_pass: Option<SecondPassResult>` with `SecondPassResult { sufficiency: f64, queries: Vec<String>, unmet_slots: Vec<String>, budget_exceeded: bool }` (new beside `RoundResult`; the plan crate's `SecondPass` becomes this type). `budget_exceeded: true` is the partial result; it adds the premise `{ step: "second_pass", name: "retrieval.second_pass", value: { "code": "RETRIEVAL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED", "unmet": n, "planned": k } }` and is not a refusal. The two knobs `retrieval.second_pass_threshold` and `retrieval.second_pass_queries` register in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs` and are read into `RoundKnobs` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs:38`) as `second_pass_threshold: f64` and `second_pass_queries: usize`. A request with no `second_pass` part leaves the result unchanged. Shape: `row_clocks` in `retrieval_clock.rs`; new `crates/medulla-result-types/src/retrieval_clock.rs`; the request, row and result fields above; two knobs; the regenerated Python models follow. Depends: R081.11, R081.8.3. | Test: `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::clock_basis_and_second_pass_are_bounded` (L3) serves a fixture where all three clocks disagree and one where a clock is absent. It asserts basis, provenance, no second pass at sufficiency 0.5 or above and exactly the bounded queries below it. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R005.1` | **Typed consistency-violation matrix on the validation result.** Stage: query execution, served `Uql` `VALIDATE SHAPE`. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `validate_shape` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/shape.rs`) → `IntegrityShape::parse` → `run_named_shapes` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/shape_integrity.rs`) → `consistency::collect` (new). BUILD. Request (no new clause): the matrix is one more integrity shape on the R005.700 route, `VALIDATE SHAPE 'integrity:consistency[:<ontology_revision_hex>]' [PROJECTION '<base>'] KEEP VIOLATING\|CONFORMING`; `IntegrityShape` gains the variant `Consistency { ontology_revision: Option<String> }` (parse, `shape_id` `integrity:consistency`, severity `fatal`). Declared inputs are read from the queried graph's composed ontology: the `ontology_ttl` documents of `ctx.view.schema_sources` (core then dynamic, the same documents `handle_source_coverage` reads), parsed once per stage with the `medulla_rdf` Turtle parser. Edges are matched to properties and nodes to classes through the stage's `medulla_rdf::sparql::Projection::from_wire(projection_base, "")` (`pred_iri(relationship)`, `node_iri(id)`), the same projection the SHACL path uses; a node's classes are the objects of its `rdf:type` triples in `medulla_rdf::mapping::export_view_triples(ctx.view)`. An edge is live unless it carries `tx_to`; its interval is `[valid_from, valid_until)` (absent `valid_until` is `i64::MAX`), the R006.1 premise mapping. The five checks, each reported once per independent occurrence: (1) `self_loop`: `P rdf:type owl:IrreflexiveProperty` and a live edge `x -P-> x`; axiom id `irreflexive:<P>`. (2) `asymmetry`: `P rdf:type owl:AsymmetricProperty` and live edges `a -P-> b` and `b -P-> a` (`a != b`), reported once with `a < b`; axiom id `asymmetric:<P>`; one direction alone is no violation. (3) `cardinality`: a class `C` with `C rdfs:subClassOf R`, `R owl:onProperty P` and `owl:minCardinality`/`owl:maxCardinality`/`owl:qualifiedCardinality` `n`, or `P rdf:type owl:FunctionalProperty` (max 1 for every subject); a focus node of class `C` (any node for a functional property) whose count of distinct live `P` targets without a `valid_from` lies outside `[min, max]`; axiom id `cardinality:<C>:<P>` or `functional:<P>`. (4) `temporal_conflict`: `P rdf:type owl:FunctionalProperty` and two live `P` edges from one subject to different targets, both with `valid_from`, whose intervals overlap (`a.from < b.to && b.from < a.to`); dated edges are judged here and not counted by (3). (5) `ontology_defect`: a node typed with classes `C1` and `C2` declared `C1 owl:disjointWith C2` or members of one `owl:AllDisjointClasses`; axiom id `disjoint:<C1>:<C2>` with `C1 < C2`, affected terms `[C1, C2]`. `owl:AsymmetricProperty` and `owl:IrreflexiveProperty` join `SUPPORTED_TYPE_OBJECTS` in `crates/medulla-reasoner/src/owl.rs` (today they refuse `OWL_UNSUPPORTED_CONSTRUCT`, so no ontology can declare them); the reasoner draws no entailment from them (they entail only inconsistency) and this collector is where their violations are found; the schema-compose test `unsupported_owl_axiom_fails_closed_with_typed_code` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs`) switches to another unsupported construct, `owl:ReflexiveProperty`. Model, in `crates/medulla-types/src/shape_report.rs`: `ViolationKind` (`Copy`, snake_case: `temporal_conflict`, `cardinality`, `self_loop`, `asymmetry`, `ontology_defect`); `Violation { focus: String, ontology_revision: String, axiom_id: String, detail: ViolationDetail }` with `Violation::kind(&self) -> ViolationKind`; `ViolationDetail` is `#[serde(tag = "kind", rename_all = "snake_case", deny_unknown_fields)]` with per-kind fields `TemporalConflict { property, first_edge, second_edge, first: (i64, i64), second: (i64, i64) }`, `Cardinality { class, property, min: Option<u32>, max: Option<u32>, observed: u32, edges: Vec<String> }`, `SelfLoop { property, edge }`, `Asymmetry { property, subject, object, forward_edge, reverse_edge }`, `OntologyDefect { affected_terms: Vec<String> }`. Kind-specific fields are required by the type, so no field-missing or unknown-kind refusal exists (serde rejects an unknown `kind`). An edge id is `"{source}\|{target}\|{ord}"`, the R735 `EDGES` row id. `ontology_revision` is `ctx.view.schema_sources.composed_digest()` as lowercase hex; the graph revision is the answer's `UqlResult::Rows.snapshot_revision`, not repeated per violation. Output: a kept violating row carries the R005.700 text columns (most severe finding) plus `shape.violations`, the `serde_json` array of that focus's `Violation`s in `(kind, axiom_id)` order, so the served answer delivers them in `UqlRow.provenance["shape.violations"]`. Refusal: a pinned `<ontology_revision_hex>` that differs from the composed digest refuses `ONTOLOGY_REVISION_STALE` before any check; register `OntologyRevisionStale => "ONTOLOGY_REVISION_STALE"` in `ServerErrorCode` (`crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs`) and add the string to a `("Uql", &[...])` entry in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. The stage is read-only and never retracts (a retraction stays the separate authorized step of MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R034). Writes: none. Shape: new file `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/consistency.rs` (`collect(ctx, projection) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, Vec<Violation>>, String>`), called from `run_named_shapes` for `IntegrityShape::Consistency`; no second validator and no new UQL clause. Depends: R005, R005.700 (on main), R006.1 (interval mapping), R735 (edge id form; the form is defined here if R735 has not landed). | Tests: `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/consistency.rs::tests::five_kinds_and_their_controls` (L2): one violating fixture and one repaired control per kind over an in-memory `GraphView` with a Turtle ontology; the control `(a,P,b)` alone yields no asymmetry; two independent defects on one node yield two violations; a pinned stale revision refuses `ONTOLOGY_REVISION_STALE`; `crates/medulla-types/src/shape_report.rs::tests::violation_round_trips_and_rejects_unknown_kind` (L1). `tests/query/test_validate_shape_served.py::test_violation_matrix_five_kinds` (L4): a served graph whose dynamic schema source declares the five axioms; `VALIDATE SHAPE 'integrity:consistency' KEEP VIOLATING` returns each violating focus with `provenance["shape.violations"]` decoding to the expected typed list and the current `ontology_revision`; the repaired controls are absent; a node and edge count read before and after is unchanged. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R078.7` | **Governed company-context logical view.** Stage: query execution, served `Uql` source `COMPANY CONTEXT`. BUILD; nothing of it is on main. Grammar: `FROM name` already names a `LET` binding (the `head` production, `crates/medulla-uql/src/grammar.rs:144`), so the view is a source keyword like `DECISIONS` and `USAGE`: add `p("company_context", Source, &["COMPANY"], None, "\"COMPANY\" \"CONTEXT\" [ \"CATEGORY\" name ]", "CompanyContextScan{category}", "COMPANY CONTEXT CATEGORY data_asset \|> WHERE name = 'orders' \|> LIMIT 20")` in `grammar.rs`, a `("COMPANY", Self::company_context)` arm in `crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/mod.rs:134`, and the printer arm in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_uql.rs` beside `Op::UsageScan` (`:456`). Plan: `Op::CompanyContextScan { category: Option<CompanyCategory> }` in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_core.rs` beside `UsageScan` (`:986`), with `OpKind::CompanyContextScan` in `wire_query_kinds.rs` (proof role `Unproved`, sample in `wire_query_samples.rs`), and `CompanyCategory` = `organization` \| `role` \| `data_asset` \| `permission` \| `time_series` (snake_case serde). An unknown category name refuses at parse time with `UQL_E_COMPANY_CATEGORY`. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`), which injects the verified `PlanScope` (R078.3) before execution, → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) → `apply_core_ops` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch.rs:329`, a new arm beside `Op::UsageScan`) → `company_context_scan(ctx, category)` (new, `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/company_context.rs`). Mapping, read on the caller's pinned row-projected snapshot `PlanCtx.view` (so another tenant's or a hidden node is never reached): a node is in category `organization`, `role`, `data_asset` or `permission` when its `type`, `node_type` or `label` property equals `Organization`, `Role` (`core-foundation-v1.ttl:719`, `:304`), `DataAsset` (`dataplatform-v1.ttl:39`) or `SecurityClearance` (`core-foundation-v1.ttl:325`); the categories are checked in that fixed order and a node takes the first match, so no node is ambiguous. Category `time_series` has one row per kept `data_asset` row whose node holds a string `series_id`: the latest committed point of that series at or before the snapshot's commit instant, read through `PlanCtx.tsdb` with `tsdb_tenant` and `tsdb_graph` set (the committed scoped read `TsScan` uses). The commit instant is `PlanCtx.snapshot_commit_ms`, which `handle_uql` binds from `GraphCore::committed_at_ms(version)`: the graph keeps a version commit record (`VersionCommitLog`, `crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/version_commits.rs`) that `mark_dirty_inner` writes with the wall-clock epoch milliseconds each time the version bumps, retained to the `graph.node_history_revisions` bound, and the lookup answers the newest record at or before the revision; a revision the record does not reach (older than the retained log, or a graph reloaded from a snapshot) refuses `SNAPSHOT_TIME_UNKNOWN` and never falls back to the pinned `now_ms` or the wall clock. The row id is `ts:<series_id>:<timestamp_ms>` and no point value is copied. Per row provenance (the `UqlRow.provenance` map of MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R722.5): `category`; `entity_id` (the node id, or the data-asset node id for a time-series row); `source_id` = the node's `source` property when present (a synced node; MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.9 stamps it), else `native`; `source_revision` = the node's `medulla_catalog_types::open_metadata::EXTERNAL_VERSION_PROPERTY` value when present, else the snapshot revision; `clearance` = the node's `clearance` labels joined by `,`; for a time-series row also `series_id` and `timestamp_ms`. Clearance: a node's `clearance` property (a string or an array of strings; absent means no label is required) must be a subset of the injected scope's `clearance_labels`, else the row is dropped before any time-series read. Refusals: `PLAN_SCOPE_UNVERIFIED` (existing, R078.4) before any read; `COMPANY_MAPPING_MISSING` when the requested category's class is not declared in the snapshot's composed schema (`GraphView.schema_sources`), registered as `CompanyMappingMissing => "COMPANY_MAPPING_MISSING"` in `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs` and added to the `("Uql", &[...])` list in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs:1463`. `SNAPSHOT_TIME_UNKNOWN` (`SnapshotTimeUnknown`, added beside it and to the same `("Uql", ...)` list) refuses a time-series read whose snapshot revision has no retained commit instant. No other code is added: one pinned snapshot leaves no stale revision, the projected view leaves no cross-tenant join, and the fixed category order leaves no ambiguity. No new ontology term. Writes: none. Shape: grammar, parser, printer and `Op` edits named above; new `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/company_context.rs`; one `ServerErrorCode` variant. Depends: R078.2, R078.3, R078.4, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R722.5. | Test: `tests/query/test_company_context_served.py::test_two_tenants_two_clearances` (L4). It asserts per-category source-bound references, denied rows excluded before any source read, and each refusal. The cross-source question is answered from ingested facts, not a hand-written graph. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.2.7` | **Topic fusion: per-document topic responsibilities.** Stage: query execution. Caller chain: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `run_unified_off_lock` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:864`) → `run_unified_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:325`) → `execute_rows` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/query_support.rs:91`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) → `fuse_topic_dense` → `TopicBackend::assignments`. Decision: assignments are backend-computed at query time and are not stored, so no ingest step writes them. Definitions. A document is a candidate node of the dense answer. Its content revision is the node property `content_revision` (unsigned integer; absent means 0), which the writer of the document's content bumps on every content change. The revision its stored embedding was computed from is the node property `embedded_revision` (unsigned integer; absent means the same value as `content_revision`, so a document that carries neither property is never stale); the writer of an embedding (the ingest chunk embedder, a client `AddEmbedding` caller) sets it to the `content_revision` it embedded. `DocumentTopicAssignment{document_id: String, content_revision: u64, embedded_revision: u64, corpus_generation: u64, backend_id: String, backend_revision: u64, space_tuple: String, topic_id: String, responsibility: f32}` (derive `Debug, Clone, PartialEq`) is defined in `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/topic.rs`; `corpus_generation` is the backend's corpus revision, `backend_id`, `backend_revision` and `space_tuple` are the values R040.2.5 defines. Backend method: `TopicBackend` gains `fn assignments(&self, candidates: &[(String, Vec<f32>)]) -> Result<Vec<DocumentTopicAssignment>, String>` (document id and stored embedding of every eligible candidate). For each candidate and for every stored topic (not only the kept ones) it returns one assignment: `responsibility = max(cosine(embedding, centroid), 0) / S`, where `S` is the sum of those clipped cosines over all stored topics of that candidate (all responsibilities are 0 when `S` is 0), so the responsibilities of one candidate sum to 1 or to 0; entries are ordered by `document_id` ascending, then `topic_id` ascending (stored duplicates keep their stored order). `StoredCentroidTopicBackend` reads `content_revision` and `embedded_revision` of each candidate from its own graph view (`self.view`) and stamps them; the test-only `InMemoryTopicBackend` gains `revisions: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, (u64, u64)>` (content, embedded; absent means `(0, 0)`). The backend only computes: a dimension mismatch between an embedding and the space is returned as is, and the checks below are made by `check_assignments`. New `pub(crate) fn check_assignments(candidates: &[(String, Vec<f32>)], assignments: &[DocumentTopicAssignment], kept_topics: &[String], retriever: &EmbeddingSpaceRef, backend_id: &str, backend_revision: u64) -> Result<(), TopicRefusal>` in `topic.rs`, called by `fuse_ranked` after `TopicBackend::topics` and before any candidate is scored, so every refusal precedes truncation and no refusal falls back to dense-only ranking. The corpus-revision comparison is not a trigger of `check_assignments`: `fuse_ranked` already refuses `TOPIC_CORPUS_STALE` (R040.2.4) before assignments are computed, so that refusal precedes the four below, `check_assignments` has no corpus-revision parameter, and the duplicate comparison that `check_assignments` makes today (`topic.rs`, the `CorpusStale` return near line 419) is deleted with its parameter; the unit test that passed a stale revision to `check_assignments` now asserts the refusal from `fuse_ranked`. Refusal triggers, checked in this order: (1) `TOPIC_SPACE_MISMATCH` when an assignment's `space_tuple` differs from `<id>|<dimensions>|<revision>` of the retriever, its `backend_id` or `backend_revision` differs from the values the backend reported for the topics result, or a candidate embedding's length differs from the retriever's dimensions; (2) `TOPIC_ASSIGNMENT_MISSING` when a candidate has no stored embedding (this replaces the `TOPIC_SPACE_MISMATCH` the fusion raised for that case before this row) or has no assignment for one of the query's kept topics; (3) `TOPIC_ASSIGNMENT_DUPLICATE` when two assignments share one `(document_id, topic_id)` (reachable by storing two `TopicCentroid` nodes with the same `topic` property); (4) `TOPIC_ASSIGNMENT_STALE` when an assignment's `embedded_revision` differs from its `content_revision` (the stored vector predates the document's current content). A candidate's topic component is the sum over the query's kept topics of the topic's query responsibility times that candidate's assignment responsibility for the topic, which is the `topic` term R040.2.2 now defines; `fuse_topic_dense` takes the checked assignments in place of its `vector_of` closure (new parameter `assignments: &[DocumentTopicAssignment]`) and `fuse_ranked` builds the candidate list from `ctx.semantic.get_embedding`. Hard constraints, `w`, tie order and tenant scope stay unchanged. Codes: `ServerErrorCode::{TopicAssignmentMissing, TopicAssignmentDuplicate, TopicAssignmentStale}` (new, `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs` beside `TopicSpaceMismatch`) and the matching `TopicRefusal::{AssignmentMissing(String), AssignmentDuplicate(String), AssignmentStale { document_id: String, content_revision: u64, embedded_revision: u64 }}`; the three codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. The topic receipt (R040.2.5) records `assignments` (candidate id, topic id, content revision, responsibility) and `backend_revision`. `ARBORA-FLEET-R014` is unrelated. Writes: none. Shape: `DocumentTopicAssignment`, `check_assignments` and `TopicBackend::assignments` are new in `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/topic.rs`; the three new codes join `ServerErrorCode` (`crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs`) and `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`; `TopicAssignmentTrace` is new in `crates/medulla-types/src/retrieval.rs` and mirrored in `medulla/generated/models.py` with the new `TopicReceipt.assignments`. Unit proofs (L2, `topic.rs::tests`): `assignments_match_the_clipped_cosine_oracle_and_sum_to_one`, `missing_duplicate_stale_and_mismatched_assignments_refuse_in_order`. Depends: R040.2.3, R040.2.5. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_topic_fusion_served.py::test_document_responsibilities_change_order` (L4). Setup as R040.2.6 (space declared through `DeclareEmbeddingSpace`, centroids `[1,0,0,0]` topic `a` and `[0,1,0,0]` topic `b`, candidates `d1` embedded `[1,0.2,0,0]` and `d2` embedded `[0.2,1,0,0]`). It recomputes each candidate's topic component from `TopicReceipt.assignments` and the query responsibilities, then replaces only `d2`'s embedding with `AddEmbedding` (`[1,0.2,0,0]`) and asserts `d1`'s `component_scores` entry is byte-equal while `d2`'s `topic_micros` and the order change. Refusal subtests, each asserting the code and that no rows are returned: a candidate with no embedding (`TOPIC_ASSIGNMENT_MISSING`), a second `TopicCentroid` node with `topic` `a` (`TOPIC_ASSIGNMENT_DUPLICATE`), `content_revision` 2 with `embedded_revision` 1 on `d1` (`TOPIC_ASSIGNMENT_STALE`), and a request `TopicRequest.space` of another revision (`TOPIC_SPACE_MISMATCH`); a hard-excluded candidate never appears in the receipt. The `TOPIC_ASSIGNMENT_MISSING` subtest for a candidate with no embedding runs through a `FuseRrf` plan, because a plain `Rank` plus `Limit` only returns embedded candidates (`exact_rank_candidates`): `{"FuseRrf": {"branches": [[{"Scan": {"label": "Doc"}}, {"Rank": {"query": [1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0]}}], [{"RankText": {"query": "needle"}}]], "k": 60.0}}` over a graph where node `d3` has text property matching `needle` and no embedding, with the same `retrieval.topic` as the other subtests; the refusal is `TOPIC_ASSIGNMENT_MISSING` and no rows return (served L4 in the same test function; the Rust proof stays `topic.rs::tests::missing_duplicate_stale_and_mismatched_assignments_refuse_in_order`). |

## Reviewed mapped definitions
These append-only children preserve the existing parent contracts named below. All remain SPECIFIED. This definition records planned acceptance and does not claim implementation or served receipts.

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.8.100` | **Retriever-bound query strategy and transfer revalidation.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, feedback step. Caller chain: `run_round` → `apply_feedback` → `check_strategy_binding`. Add `QueryStrategy { strategy_id, digest, retriever_id, retriever_version, corpus_revision, model_revision, policy_revision, evaluation_set_digest }` and `TransferReceipt { target_retriever_id, target_retriever_version, strategy_id, strategy_digest, evaluation_set_digest, metric_id, measured, threshold, passed, corpus_revision, model_revision, policy_revision }` in `crates/medulla-types/src/retrieval_round.rs`. Add `check_strategy_binding` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_feedback.rs`. The request carries the strategy in `RoundFeedback.strategy: Option<StrategyReuse>` (`crates/medulla-result-types/src/retrieval_round.rs`: the strategy, the target retriever id and version, the target policy revision and an optional receipt); the round's own corpus and model revisions bind the target. `apply_feedback` calls the check first and returns the verified strategy id as `FeedbackOutcome.strategy_id`. A strategy is reusable without a receipt only on an exact match. Retriever id, version, corpus, model and policy revisions must be byte-identical. A changed target needs a `TransferReceipt` with `passed` true, `measured >= threshold` and the same digests. A receipt below threshold refuses activation and keeps the prior strategy. No strategy is copied to another retriever. Refusal: `RETRIEVAL_STRATEGY_UNVERIFIED`. The `threshold` is a request value that must be at least `knob retrieval.transfer_min_threshold` (0.6, [0, 1], metric score), declared in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs` and read into `RoundKnobs`. Shape: Types `QueryStrategy` and `TransferReceipt` go in `crates/medulla-types/src/retrieval_round.rs`. `check_strategy_binding` goes in `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_feedback.rs`. The digests reuse `Digest256::sha256` (`crates/medulla-types/src/contract/crypto.rs:10`). It performs a byte-equal comparison of the binding fields and copies no strategy. Depends: R081.8.2, R081.8.3. | Test: `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_feedback.rs::tests::strategy_reuse_needs_matching_retriever_or_receipt` (L1) and `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::transfer_receipt_gates_strategy_reuse` (L3). It asserts same-retriever reuse, a version change refusal, a below-threshold refusal and an unchanged active strategy. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R032.100` | **Layer- and history-weighted impact in both dependency directions.** Stage: query execution, served `Uql` `PROPAGATE ... RISK HISTORY`. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `run_unified_off_lock_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:877`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) → `propagate_op` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/propagate.rs:118`) → `historical_risk` (new, `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/propagate_history.rs`). BUILD; nothing of it is on main. Grammar (an optional tail on the existing `propagate` production in `crates/medulla-uql/src/grammar.rs:208`, parsed in `propagate` of `crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/traverse.rs:33`): `"PROPAGATE" model edge [ "HOPS" int ] [ "DEFAULT" num ] [ "RISK" "HISTORY" string "WINDOW" int "TO" int "LAYERS" "(" string num { "," string num } ")" ]`. Example: `MATCH (:Service) WHERE id = 'svc-a' \|> PROPAGATE NOISY_OR -[:dependsOn]- HOPS 4 RISK HISTORY 'deploys' WINDOW 1700000000000 TO 1702592000000 LAYERS ('service' 1.0, 'os' 0.5, 'hardware' 0.25) \|> RETURN impact, history_risk`. The string after `HISTORY` is the dataset id; the two ints are the window `[from_ms, to_ms)`; `LAYERS` is the ordered layer taxonomy with one weight each in `[0, 1]`, at most 16 layers, unique names. Parse refusals: `UQL_E_RISK_HISTORY` (weight outside `[0, 1]`, a duplicate layer, more than 16 layers, `to_ms <= from_ms`), registered as a `UqlCode` beside `InvalidRange`. Plan: `Op::Propagate` (`crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_core.rs:498`) gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] risk_history: Option<RiskHistorySpec>`, with `RiskHistorySpec { dataset: String, from_ms: i64, to_ms: i64, layers: Vec<LayerWeight> }` and `LayerWeight { layer: String, weight: f64 }` beside `PropagateModel`; the printer in `crates/medulla-uql/src/print.rs` prints the tail so the round trip of R728 holds. History source, decided here: the failure history is graph facts on the caller's pinned, row-projected snapshot (`PlanCtx.view`), never a decision-log or usage record (the usage ledger of MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R801 records model calls, not component failures). An observation is a visible `:Observation` node (`core-foundation-v1.ttl:191`) with `dataset` equal to the dataset id, an `:observedEntity` edge to the observed node, `observationTime` (Unix ms) in `[from_ms, to_ms)` and `outcome` equal to `failure` or `success`; any other `outcome` value counts in neither and is reported as `HISTORY_MALFORMED` in `warnings`. A node's layer is its `layer` property (`infrastructure-v1.ttl:296`); a node whose layer is not in `LAYERS` has weight 0. Per node: `observations` = matching observations, `failures` = those with `outcome = failure`, `failure_rate = failures / observations`, `layer_contribution = layer_weight * failure_rate`. Paths: one walk downstream (the edge direction as written) and one upstream (reversed), each at most `HOPS` hops over the same `rel` and `edge_preds` the R032 walk uses; a path never repeats a node; `path_risk = 1 - product(1 - layer_contribution)` over the path's nodes; a node reached by several paths keeps the maximum `path_risk`, ties broken by fewer hops, then node id, then the edge-id sequence. A node with zero observations is `coverage: unknown`, contributes nothing to its paths, and makes `aggregate_risk` null with `partial: true`; otherwise `aggregate_risk` is the maximum `path_risk` over both directions. Result: `HistoricalRisk { target: String, graph_revision: u64, hops: usize, layers: Vec<LayerWeight>, dataset: String, from_ms: i64, to_ms: i64, dataset_digest: String, upstream: Vec<NodeRisk>, downstream: Vec<NodeRisk>, aggregate_risk: Option<f64>, partial: bool, request_digest: String }` and `NodeRisk { node_id: String, path: Vec<String>, edge_ids: Vec<String>, layer: Option<String>, observations: u64, failures: u64, layer_contribution: Option<f64>, path_risk: Option<f64>, coverage: Coverage }` with `Coverage` = `known` \| `unknown`, in new `crates/medulla-query-types/src/impact_risk.rs` (beside the wire types, because `UqlResult` must reference it and `medulla-types` cannot see query types). `UqlResult::Rows` gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")] historical_risk: Vec<HistoricalRisk>`, one entry per input seed row, and each output row gains the score channel `history_risk` (the node's `path_risk`, absent when unknown). `dataset_digest = Digest256::framed(b"eg/risk-history-dataset/v1", [dataset, from_ms (i64 BE), to_ms (i64 BE), each counted observation node id in ascending order])`; `request_digest = Digest256::framed(b"eg/risk-history-request/v1", [target, hops (u64 BE), dataset_digest, then each layer name and weight `f64::to_bits` (u64 BE) in order])`; both lowercase hex. Refusal: `HISTORY_DATASET_UNAVAILABLE` when no visible observation of the dataset exists in the window; the same code answers a dataset the caller cannot see, so the reply is no existence oracle (there is no separate unauthorized code). Register `HistoryDatasetUnavailable => "HISTORY_DATASET_UNAVAILABLE"` and `HistoryMalformed => "HISTORY_MALFORMED"` in `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs` and add both to the `("Uql", &[...])` list in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs:1463`. The `impact` channel and the R032 Noisy-OR or cascade output are computed exactly as before; `historical_risk` is an added dimension. Consumer: `MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R065`. Writes: none. Shape: new `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/propagate_history.rs` (`historical_risk(view, seeds, spec, rel, dir, edge_preds, hops) -> Result<Vec<HistoricalRisk>, String>`) called by `propagate_op` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/propagate.rs:118`) only when `risk_history` is `Some`; new `crates/medulla-query-types/src/impact_risk.rs`; grammar, parser and printer edits. Depends: R032. | Test: `tests/query/test_propagate_served.py::test_historical_risk_both_directions` (L4). Fixtures cover three layers, differing failure counts, a cycle and a node with no observations. It asserts both directions, the formulas, `partial` with a null aggregate, `HISTORY_DATASET_UNAVAILABLE` for an absent and for a hidden dataset, and an `impact` channel identical to the same query without the tail. Unit: `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/propagate_history.rs::tests::path_risk_and_cycles` (L1) and `crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/traverse.rs::tests::risk_history_tail_parses_prints_and_refuses` (L1). |

Entry point: new read-only `Method::GraphLearnPredictNary` with capability action `graph.learn.predict_nary`, which grants no write. It scores ordered n-ary tuples, including entities unseen at fit time, and returns hypotheses only. The binary `GraphLearnPredict` and its write authority stay unchanged. It is routed in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/handlers/graphlearn.rs` as a read, not through the mutation gateway in `gateway_mining_ml.rs`. Caller chain: the `GraphLearnPredictNary` arm in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/handlers/graphlearn.rs` → the new n-ary scorer in `crates/medulla-graph-algos/src/graphlearn/nary.rs`. An empty ordered tuple is refused. A caller-asserted evidence class other than hypothesis is refused. The reply carries no assertion flag. Children `.1` to `.4` deliver it. It also asserts the empty-tuple refusal, the evidence-class refusal and the absent assertion flag. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.101` | **Reusable compressed document state with decoding-free reranking (rollup).** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, selection step. Entry point: served `Method::RetrievalRound`. The engine builds a query-independent document state once and scores queries against stored projections with no decoding or generation. Children `.1` to `.3` deliver it. The encoder is a server-bound `StateEncoder` port, as the reranker is in R081.7.3. | Root L3 journey test: `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::compressed_state_rerank_is_order_independent` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.102` | **Index-time distilled scorer with measured quality and zero query model calls.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, selection step. Learn path: `DecisionFit` submits a `RerankHead` fitted offline by the harness from teacher judgments. The head follows the one head lifecycle (draft, evaluated, published, champion, retired) of MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R805. Medulla stores the artifact with corpus revision, index revision, teacher model revision, judgment dataset digest, artifact digest and metric policy. Medulla evaluates it on a held-out target set. It becomes champion only when the measured metric reaches `knob retrieval.distill_min_quality` (0.8, [0, 1], nDCG at 10). Query caller: `run_round` → `score_with_head` applies the champion as a linear head over stored features. It makes no model-service call and never distills on demand. A below-threshold, stale or unbound artifact refuses with `RETRIEVAL_POLICY_UNVERIFIED` and the prior champion stays active. Caller chain: wire `Method::RetrievalRound` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_retrieval_round` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/retrieval_round.rs`) → `run_round` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs`) → `score_with_head` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_head.rs`). Shape: New file `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_head.rs` holds `score_with_head` as a dot product over stored features, with no I/O. `RerankHead` reaches storage through the existing `DecisionFit` path of `crates/medulla-h-decide/src/decide/stat_log.rs`. Promotion reuses the head lifecycle of MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R805. Depends: MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R805, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R179. | Test: `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::distilled_head_activates_only_above_threshold` (L3) fits, evaluates and promotes a head. Queries make zero model-service calls. After a below-threshold artifact the prior champion stays. |

## Additional reviewed mapped definition
| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.100` | **Joint sparse nonnegative reconstruction selects a diverse document set.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, selection step. Caller chain: wire `Method::RetrievalRound` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_retrieval_round` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/retrieval_round.rs`) → `run_round` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs`) → `joint_sparse_select` in new `crates/medulla-decision-stats/src/sparse_select.rs`. The `selection` part gains `mode` (`SelectionMode`, default `DualScore`), whose `JointSparse { embedding_revision, lambda, tolerance, max_iters, query_vector }` variant selects this solve; an absent `lambda`, `tolerance` or `max_iters` takes the knob value, `max_iters` can be lowered and never raised above its knob, and an absent `query_vector` is the bound text embedder's embedding of the part's `query`. Given query vector q and candidate matrix V, the kernel minimizes `||q - Vᵀx||₂² + λ||x||₁` with x ≥ 0 by cyclic coordinate descent. The update is `x_i = max(0, (v_i·r_i - λ/2) / ||v_i||²)`, where `r_i` is the residual without term `i`. It stops when the largest coefficient change is below `tolerance`. Knobs: `knob retrieval.joint_lambda` 0.1 [0.0001, 10] unitless. `knob retrieval.joint_tolerance` 0.000001 [0.000000001, 0.001] unitless. `knob retrieval.joint_max_iters` 1000 [10, 100000] sweeps. Rows of V are embeddings in one declared space and revision. The result lists document ids, coefficients above 0, residual and solver status, in coefficient-descending then id order. It never replaces the dual-score mode of R081.7. Tenant and result-count bounds apply. Refusals: `RETRIEVAL_EMBEDDING_REVISION_MISMATCH`, `RETRIEVAL_POLICY_UNVERIFIED` for invalid lambda, `RETRIEVAL_SOLVER_NOT_CONVERGED`. Shape: `joint_sparse_select` is a pure `f64` kernel in new `crates/medulla-decision-stats/src/sparse_select.rs`. `medulla-plan` gains a path dependency on `medulla-decision-stats`; no cycle forms because that crate depends only on `medulla-numeric`, `medulla-types` and the decision type crates. `SelectionMode` lives beside `SelectionPart` in `crates/medulla-result-types/src/retrieval_round.rs`; the part's `policy` still supplies the tenant bound `max_chunks`, the policy and corpus revisions and the declared embedding revision, and the result is `RoundResult.joint_selection` (`JointSelection`). Candidates are the plan's executed rows, at most `retrieval.rerank_pool`, with their stored embeddings; a row without one refuses `RETRIEVAL_SCORE_PAIR_REQUIRED`. The solve is also refused with `RETRIEVAL_EMBEDDING_REVISION_MISMATCH` for a dimension mismatch. `medulla-plan` takes `medulla-decision-stats` under its `query` feature. Depends: R081.7.3. | Test: `crates/medulla-decision-stats/src/sparse_select.rs::tests::matches_exhaustive_oracle_on_tiny_fixture` (L2) and `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::joint_selection_prefers_diverse_pair` (L3). The served test uses two correlated documents and a diverse pair, and asserts exact selection and order. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.4.2` | **NL planning context: behavior.** Stage: query planning. Caller: served `NlQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` → `handle_nl_query_plan` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs:58`) → `link_schema` (new, `crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl/context.rs`). Schema linking scores each label and table by token overlap with the request: `score = shared_tokens / request_tokens`. Ties break by name ascending. It keeps at most 60 labels, 60 tables and 16 properties per label. Choice `auto` chooses among the dialects that have an NL planner (UQL, `sql` of R058.4.10 and `sparql` of R058.4.11) by schema match: the score of UQL is the number of linked labels, of `sql` the number of linked catalog tables, of `sparql` the number of linked RDF properties; the highest score wins and a tie or an all-zero score chooses UQL, then `sql`, then `sparql` in that order. `cypher` has no NL planner, so an explicit `cypher` refuses `NL_PLANNER_UNCONFIGURED`. The tables are those of the SQL catalog visible to the principal, read in the same snapshot as the labels. A parse or execution error returns to the planner as a typed message. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.4.7.1` | **Snapshot graph and authorized foreign-source grounding.** `SchemaContext` in `crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl/context.rs` reads the caller's complete row-filtered snapshot. No fixed node-scan bound can classify an unobserved name as absent. It holds each label with its stored property keys, each edge relationship and any declared foreign sources. The schema hint lists them. The served path obtains foreign names from the existing owner-authorized source registry before planning. An absent registry refuses foreign stages with `NL_SCHEMA_UNBOUND_FOREIGN_SOURCE`. `plan_grounded` parses every candidate with the caller grammar. It then checks each label, predicate property, relationship and `FOREIGN` source against the schema, including operations nested in fusion branches. A local `MATCH` scan always uses local labels, even before or after a foreign-source stage. Foreign operators carry ids and scores, while property reads rematerialize the same local snapshot. Foreign replacement, JOIN and mixed fusion cannot exempt an unknown local property. A JOIN can reseed foreign ids when its input is empty, so its output uses the complete local property schema. Inline adapters need the separate authority and schema work in `.9`. Label-bearing keys remain queryable properties, and unlabelled node fields are included for `MATCH ()`. Traversal and propagation predicates use stored edge keys, not node keys. Attribution input properties and Owen grouping properties use the incoming node scope. SPARQL, inferred-class, spatial and tensor source stages reset the incoming node scope. Spatial and tensor layer names use the graph labels their executors read. JSONPath checks exact top-level document keys; nested document shape and wildcard paths retain executor semantics and are not inferred or repaired. The engine rewrites a name that differs from a stored name only in case or separators. The disclosed UQL is then the canonical text of the plan that runs. Any other unknown name is a typed violation. The codes are `NL_SCHEMA_UNKNOWN_LABEL`, `NL_SCHEMA_UNKNOWN_PROPERTY`, `NL_SCHEMA_UNKNOWN_RELATIONSHIP`, `NL_SCHEMA_UNKNOWN_SOURCE` and `NL_SCHEMA_UNBOUND_FOREIGN_SOURCE`. The engine returns each parse error or violation to the planner with its reason. After `max_steps` (default 2), `NlQuery` and `NlQueryExplain` refuse with `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. The refusal carries the text and outcome of every step, and nothing runs. The system prompt states that example names are not the schema. Caller chain: served `NlQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_nl_query_plan` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs:60`) → `nl_schema_context` (`nl.rs:95`) → `SchemaContext::from_view` (`context.rs:220`) and `declare_foreign_sources` (`context.rs:289`) → `plan_grounded` (`context.rs:835`) → `SchemaContext::ground` (`context.rs:370`). Shape: Delivered in `crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl/context.rs`: `SchemaContext`, `LabelSchema`, `SchemaViolation` and `Repair`; the foreign names come from `nl_foreign_sources` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs:115`). Reuse these; add nothing. | A table holds the 26 validation-run questions over ingested ServiceNow fixture data. Each row asserts grounded UQL and the expected rows. A question with no graph data asserts the typed refusal (`context_tests.rs::evidence_questions_are_grounded_or_refused_over_servicenow_data`). A served test refuses an unknown property with its reason and runs a repaired name (`nl_explain.rs::nl_query_grounds_names_on_the_graph_schema_before_running`). |

Each table and column it names must be in `SchemaContext.tables`; an unknown name returns to the planner as typed feedback inside the bounded loop (R058.4.2) and, when the loop ends, refuses `NL_QUERY_STEPS_EXHAUSTED`.
