| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.5` | **Configurable NL planner request.** The standalone planner reads a read timeout and an extra request body. They come from the same configuration as the endpoint and model. The `nl_query` or `llm` config section holds `read_timeout_secs` (default 30, accepted 1 to 600). It also holds `extra_body`, a JSON object that the planner merges into the request top level. The default `extra_body` is `{"chat_template_kwargs":{"enable_thinking":false}}`, so a reasoning model answers directly. The env keys `MEDULLA_NL_READ_TIMEOUT_SECS` and `MEDULLA_NL_EXTRA_BODY` (JSON) override the file. A configured `extra_body` replaces the default and cannot set `model` or `messages`. The planner refuses invalid values with the typed `NlConfigError` (`ReadTimeoutOutOfRange`, `ExtraBodyNotObject`, `ExtraBodyReservedKey`, `InvalidValue`). `Method::NlQuery` then refuses with the `ENGINE_UNAVAILABLE` code and a detail that names the bad key. Caller chain: served `NlQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_nl_query_plan` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs:60`) → `resolve_planner` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/nl.rs:45`) → `build_default_from_config` (`nl.rs:97`) → `UreqNlPlanner::with_read_timeout_secs` and `with_extra_body` (`crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl.rs:472`, `:483`) → refusal `nl_config_refusal` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs:46`). Shape: `NlConfigError` and the two builders already exist in `crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl.rs` (`:322`, `:472`, `:483`); `parse_config` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/nl.rs:285`) gains the keys `read_timeout_secs` and `extra_body` and the env override. Reuse `nl_config_refusal`; add no second error path. | Tests against a local stub HTTP server confirm the default and a configured extra body reach the request body. They also confirm a peer slower than the configured read timeout fails within bounds. Refusal tests confirm each `NlConfigError` variant. A config test confirms the keys parse and invalid values are refused. An unparseable env or config value is refused with `InvalidValue`, which carries the source name and the raw text. A handler test confirms the served refusal carries the code and names the key. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl_regressions.rs::planner_sends_configured_extra_body_and_honours_read_timeout`, `crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl_regressions.rs::planner_refuses_invalid_request_config_with_typed_errors`, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs::nl_config_refusal_tests::invalid_planner_config_is_refused_with_code_and_key` and `crates/medulla-h-query/src/nl.rs::tests::unparseable_env_values_are_refused_with_source_and_raw_value`. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.6` | **NL answers are checked against certified definitions.** Stage: query planning, served `NlQuery`. Caller chain: `handle_nl_query_plan` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/nl.rs:58`) → step loop → new `check_certified_definitions` in `crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl/certified.rs` → execution. A `:GlossaryTerm` (`MEDULLA-CQ-CONN-R074.2`) with a certified status holds the one approved definition of a metric or entity. Before execution, the engine compares each term the candidate UQL uses with its certified definition and lineage. A mismatch returns to the planner as typed feedback inside the bounded loop of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.4`. Each result lists the certified terms it used, with their version and lineage. Refusal: `NL_DEFINITION_CONFLICT` when the loop ends with a mismatch; it registers in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs` for `NlQuery` and `NlQueryExplain`. The result that lists the certified terms is `NlQueryExplainResult.certified_terms` (`CertifiedTermUse`: term, version, lineage); plain `NlQuery` answers bare rows. Shape: new `crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl/certified.rs` with `CertifiedTerm`, `certified_terms(view)` and `check_certified_definitions`; a certified term is a `:GlossaryTerm` node of the caller's row-level-filtered snapshot with `status` `certified`, `name`, `definition`, `version` and a `lineage` list of stored names. A term is used when the question names it; the candidate conforms when it references every lineage name. `plan_grounded_certified` runs the check as one more typed step of the existing correction loop (the same `NlPlanStep` feedback), and the planner hint carries each used term; `NlQueryError::DefinitionConflict` ends a loop whose last step was a mismatch. Writes: none. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.4, MEDULLA-CQ-CONN-R074.2. | Test: `tests/nl_query_certified_definitions.rs::mismatch_is_corrected_then_disclosed` (L3). A scripted planner returns a query that uses a term outside its certified definition, then a correct query. The test asserts one feedback step, the certified terms in the result and the refusal when the loop ends. |

The record is one `NlCorrection` node committed through the durable gateway as an `AddNode` upsert; its id is the SHA-256 of the question and both queries, so recording the same correction twice returns the first record. A question matches when it has the same set of words as the recorded one. Writes: one correction record under the caller's authority. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.9` | **Conflicting definitions across sources are reported, not merged.** Stage: source sync (WIRE work). When two sources define a glossary term with the same name and different meanings, the engine records one conflict fact that links both definitions. Planning (`MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.6`) treats a term with an open conflict as uncertified until a certifier resolves it. On main: `TermConflict` (`node_id()`, `properties()`), `reconcile_term_definitions(view: &GraphView) -> Vec<TermConflict>` (`crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl/certified.rs:125`, `:183`) and the open-conflict exclusion in planning (`open_conflict_terms`, `certified.rs:233`). Decided here: the step that writes glossary term nodes from a source is the served `SourceSync`, the only route by which a connector's records become graph nodes; connector-pack onboarding writes the pack's ontology and mappings, never term instances, so the call goes in source sync and nowhere else. Caller chain: served `SourceSync` → `source_sync` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/source_sync.rs`) → `plan_and_batch` (`source_sync.rs:117`) → after `plan_sync` and the direction checks, before `plan.batch` is encoded → `term_conflict_ops(&nodes, &plan.batch, connection_iri)` (new, `source_sync.rs`) → `reconcile_term_nodes` (new, `certified.rs`). Changes: (1) refactor `reconcile_term_definitions` into `pub fn reconcile_term_nodes<'a>(nodes: impl IntoIterator<Item = (&'a str, &'a serde_json::Map<String, serde_json::Value>)>, existing: &dyn Fn(&str) -> bool) -> Vec<TermConflict>`; `reconcile_term_definitions(view)` becomes a two-line wrapper that decodes `view.node_properties` and passes `\|id\| view.node_properties.contains_key(id)`, so one reconciliation body exists. (2) A node is a term when its `type`, `node_type` or `label` equals `GLOSSARY_TERM_LABEL` or `CatalogGlossaryTerm` (the catalog subclass, `metadata_catalog-v1.ttl:258`; the open-metadata sync adapter sets `type` to the class local name, `crates/medulla-virtual-graph/src/open_metadata_sync.rs:122`, and passes the record's properties through); its name is the first non-empty of `name`, `displayName`, `prefLabel`; its definition is the first non-empty of `definition`, `description`, `summary`. (3) `term_conflict_ops` overlays the planned upserts (`plan.batch[].node_id`, `.properties`) on the visible `nodes`, stamps `source = connection_iri` on each planned term upsert that lacks a `source` property (so two connections are two sources), calls `reconcile_term_nodes` over the overlay with `existing` = the visible node ids, and returns one `upsert_node` operation `{ "op": "upsert_node", "id": conflict.node_id(), "properties": conflict.properties() }` per new conflict. (4) `plan_and_batch` appends those operations to the same `BatchUpdate`, so the terms and their conflicts commit atomically; a conflict whose node id already exists is not rewritten. `SyncReport` gains `term_conflicts: usize`. Add `medulla-nlq = { path = "../medulla-nlq" }` to `crates/medulla-h-graph/Cargo.toml` (medulla-nlq depends on no handler crate, so no cycle). Writes: one `TermConflict` node per conflicting pair, inside the sync batch. No new refusal code. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.6. | Test: `tests/nl_query_certified_definitions.rs::conflicting_sources_record_one_conflict` (L3). Two source syncs on two connections write one term name with different definitions; the test asserts exactly one `TermConflict` node after the second sync, none after a third identical sync, `SyncReport.term_conflicts` 1 then 0, and that planning does not treat the term as certified. Unit: `crates/medulla-nlq/src/nl/certified.rs::tests::reconcile_term_nodes_reads_catalog_terms_and_skips_existing` (L1). |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R090.2` | **Behavior: arm fusion and recency.** Stage: retrieval; entry: served `Discover`. Code: `plan_discover` in the new `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/discover_plan.rs`. Mode `standard` keeps the weighted blend of `discover` unchanged. Mode `deep` asks the `ExpansionProvider` of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R651.7` for at most `knob discover.expansions` (default 4, [1, 8], items) expansions. It embeds each one and runs one semantic arm per expansion. Arms fuse by reciprocal rank. Score is the sum over arms of 1 / (`knob discover.rrf_k` + rank). The default is 60, range [1, 1000], unit rank. Recency: boost = 1 + `knob discover.recency_weight` × 0.5 ^ (age_days / half_life_days). `discover.recency_weight` defaults to 0.5, [0, 2], ratio. The half life is the class annotation `recencyHalfLifeDays` of the node type. Otherwise it is `knob discover.recency_half_life_days`, default 30, [1, 3650], days. Age is `as_of` minus the node `updated_at`, else `created_at`. A node with no date, or a date after `as_of`, gets 1.0. Final score = fused score × boost. Writes: none. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R090.1. The named default `keep_on_measured_gain` keeps a candidate when three conditions hold. First, p50 latency or bytes fetched improve by `knob federation.min_gain` (default 10, [1, 90], percent). (3) The read. `Fragment` gains `kept: Option<KeptPlan>`, set in `foreign_scan` and `foreign_named` from `kept_plan(session cache scope owner salt, derived_digest(["shape", remote.label()]))` before `source` or `join` runs. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R650.2` | **Behavior: fuse, cut and select.** Stage: retrieval. Code: `compile` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/context_bundle.rs`. Per-source scores calibrate to [0, 1] by the pinned curve of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R652.2`, else by min-max within the source. Sources fuse by reciprocal rank with `knob context.rrf_k` (default 60, [1, 1,000], rank). The tail cuts at the largest gap between adjacent fused scores. It keeps at least `knob context.min_items` (default 3, [1, 64], items). Surviving items become `ContextPackageCandidate` rows with `value_micro` = fused score × 1,000,000. `compile` calls `lower`, `solve` and `verify` of `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R137.8` with node budget `knob context.solver_node_budget` of `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R137.9`. A greedy fallback never qualifies. Each item gets at most `knob context.proof_edges_per_item` proof edges (default 3, [0, 16], edges). They come from its `hop_path` or its `DERIVED_FROM` edges. Every excluded item carries a reason. Writes: none. Depends: MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R137.8, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R652.2. Caller chain: served `ContextCompile` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → new arm → `handle_context_compile` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/context_compile.rs`) → `medulla_plan::context_bundle::compile` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/context_bundle.rs`) → `lower`, `solve`, `verify` of `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R137.8`. Shape: `compile` and `fuse_and_cut` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/context_bundle.rs`; the knapsack is the solver of `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R137.8` called as a library, so no second solver exists. Reuse the one reciprocal-rank fusion function `medulla_text::rrf_fuse` (`crates/medulla-text/src/lib.rs`), which `fuse_rrf` of `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs` already calls; `medulla-plan` links `medulla-text` without its `tantivy` feature. The solver is `select` of `crates/medulla-decision-policy/src/solve/context_package.rs`, which runs `lower`, `solve` and `verify` and refuses anything but a verified optimum. A candidate is one resolution of one hit; the widest resolution carries the full fused value and a narrower one its token share of it (never below one micro unit), so resolution is a real choice. A hit with no measured span is `out_of_scope`; a hit that cannot fit the 64-candidate solver bound is `over_budget`; an unchosen hit whose content digests all appear in chosen items is `dominated`. The registered knobs `context.rrf_k`, `context.min_items`, `context.solver_node_budget`, `context.proof_edges_per_item` and `context.value_floor_micro_per_token` are in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs`. Items an earlier stage left out (`denied`, `out_of_scope`) arrive in `CompileInputs.pre_excluded`. | L1 `crates/medulla-plan/src/context_bundle.rs::tests::cut_select_and_exclusion_reasons`. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R650.4` | **Refusal.** Stage: retrieval. Code: `handle_context_compile`; codes routed for `ContextCompile`. `CONTEXT_BUDGET_MISSING` refuses a request with no window or no tokenizer. `CONTEXT_ITEM_COST_INVALID` refuses a negative or non-finite cost. `SCOPE_AGENT_UNKNOWN` (the one R704.4 scope check, run by `run_compile`) refuses an `agent_ref` of another tenant or with no definition node. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R702.2` | **Behavior: detectors and refinement.** Stage: evaluation data. Code: `synthesize` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/search_task.rs`. Each detector is a bounded graph query over the evidence graph. Single-clue risk: a clue alone leaves at most 1 candidate answer. Co-cover risk: one evidence node is adjacent to at least 2 clue nodes outside the chain. Exposed constant: a clue text shares at least `knob search_task.expose_overlap` (default 0.5, [0.1, 1.0], ratio) of the answer-name tokens. Prior-knowledge risk: the answer node degree is at least `knob search_task.prior_degree` (default 50, [5, 10,000], edges). Refinement adds one clue or masks one constant, for at most `knob search_task.refine_rounds` (default 3, [0, 8], rounds). Each detector is linear in the subgraph. Writes: none. Caller chain: `handle_synthesize` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/search_task.rs`) → `synthesize` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/search_task.rs`) → the four detectors, reached from served `SynthesizeSearchTask` through `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`). Shape: new `crates/medulla-plan/src/search_task.rs`; its result types are the existing `RiskFinding` and `RiskThresholds` of `crates/medulla-work-types/src/search_task.rs`; the knobs register beside the existing knob table. | Test: `crates/medulla-plan/src/search_task.rs::tests::detector_complexity_on_10_1000_100000_items` (L1) and `crates/medulla-plan/src/search_task.rs::tests::each_detector_threshold_boundary` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R702.3` | **Wiring.** Stage: evaluation data. Caller: `handle_synthesize` reads the row-projected core of the caller and calls `synthesize`. Scope `query:read`. The request is `SynthesizeSearchTaskRequest` (`schema_version`, `tenant_id`, `graph`, `answer_node`) and the answer is `SynthesizeSearchTaskAnswer`, either `Accepted` with a `SynthesizeSearchTaskResult` (`task`, `report`, `thresholds`, `rounds_used`, `evidence_nodes`) or `Rejected` with a `SearchTaskRejection`, all in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/search_task.rs` beside the model. The `tenant_id` must be the verified tenant (`ACCESS_DENIED`) and `graph` the graph the call targets. The handler reads the snapshot narrowed to the caller's tenant and visible units, walks the evidence within `knob search_task.evidence_hops` (default 3, [1, 8], hops) of the answer node and refuses a neighbourhood over `knob search_task.max_evidence_nodes` (default 10,000, [10, 1,000,000], nodes) with `SEARCH_TASK_TOO_LARGE`. An answer node the caller cannot read refuses with `SEARCH_TASK_INVALID`, the same as an absent one. The thresholds come from knobs: `search_task.single_clue_max` (default 0.5, [0.0, 1.0], ratio), `search_task.co_coverage_max` (default 0.5, [0.0, 1.0], ratio), `search_task.expose_overlap` and `search_task.prior_degree` of R702.2. A task over any threshold answers `Rejected` with code `SEARCH_TASK_SHORTCUT` naming the detector. Accepted tasks become `PinnedSet` items through the request of `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.100.2`; the caller sends them. The engine writes nothing. The claim to feed `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R053` is dropped: that gold set maps requirements to assemblies. Depends: MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.100.1, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.100.2. Caller chain: served `SynthesizeSearchTask` → `dispatch_request_method` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router.rs:194`) → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → new arm → `handle_synthesize` (new, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/search_task.rs`) → `synthesize` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/search_task.rs`) → `RiskReport::check` (`crates/medulla-work-types/src/search_task.rs:205`). | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R053.700` | **`Trino` and `Spark` sources with idempotent submission: model and behavior.** Stage: federation planning and job submission. Caller chain: served `Uql` `FOREIGN` over `Trino` or `Spark` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `run_statement` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/serve.rs:50`) → `federation_trino.rs` / `federation_spark.rs` → `SubmissionId::derive` (new, in `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_spark.rs`). A `Trino` source reads against a named snapshot and refuses an unknown snapshot (`SNAPSHOT_UNKNOWN`). A `Spark` submission id is the SHA-256 digest (64 lowercase hex characters) of preset id, input digests and parameters. Resubmission returns the same id and runs once. An unavailable preset refuses with `PRESET_UNAVAILABLE`. The change envelope carries the fence fields `source_id`, `snapshot_id`, `submission_id`, `generation`, `producer_revision`. Cost: `O(input bytes)` hashing and 8 MiB memory. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: the submission record through the jobs path. Depends: R053. Shape: add `SubmissionId::derive` and the fence-field check beside `register_spark` in the existing `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_spark.rs`; the shared snapshot check `check_snapshot` sits beside the fence in `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_spark.rs` (one check for both backends); reuse the SHA-256 helper of `medulla-kernel` and `ForeignSourceRegistry` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation.rs`); no second id function. | Test: `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_spark.rs::tests::submission_id_idempotent_and_fenced` (L1). Verified by the root slice test `tests/query/test_spark_trino_sources_served.py::test_resubmission_returns_same_submission`. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R053.701` | **`Trino` and `Spark` sources: refusal, evidence and hot-path proof.** Stage: federation planning. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `run_statement` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/serve.rs:50`) → `SubmissionId::derive` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_spark.rs`). A missing fence field refuses with `FENCE_FIELD_MISSING`; an unknown snapshot refuses with `SNAPSHOT_UNKNOWN`. Evidence: the response carries `submission_id` and the fence fields. The served submission path is `ForeignReadOpen` with a `submission` (preset id, input digests, parameters, snapshot id, producer revision; `crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/foreign_source_handlers.rs`), whose pages the correlated `Uql` then reads; the one preset a source offers is its approved registration operation, the one known snapshot is the pack digest its approval pins, and the submission id is derived by the server (`SubmissionId::derive`), never chosen by the host. An identical resubmission by the same owner resumes the one live execution (`ForeignReadSessions::open_submission`, keyed by owner and effective-actor scope) and runs once; another owner or tenant never resumes it. A missing fence field refuses `FENCE_FIELD_MISSING`, an unoffered preset `PRESET_UNAVAILABLE`, an unknown snapshot `SNAPSHOT_UNKNOWN`, all registered on `ForeignReadOpen`; the open result carries the fence fields as `submission`. The connector side belongs to `SCION-CONNECTOR-CONTROL-R069`. Benchmark `crates/medulla-federation/benches/spark_submission_id.rs`: p50 under 0.02 ms and p99 under 0.2 ms, memory under 8 MiB; it never gates a PR. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none beyond R053.700. Depends: R053.700. Shape: the refusal codes `FENCE_FIELD_MISSING` and `SNAPSHOT_UNKNOWN` and the response fields come from `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_spark.rs` and `federation_trino.rs` of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R053.700`; the benchmark file is new `crates/medulla-federation/benches/spark_submission_id.rs`; reuse `SubmissionId::derive` and no second path. | Test: `tests/query/test_spark_trino_sources_served.py::test_resubmission_returns_same_submission` (L4). It asserts one id for two identical submissions, both refusals, and a cross-tenant negative. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R005.700` | **Graph integrity validation as named shapes: model and behavior.** Stage: query execution, served `Uql` `VALIDATE SHAPE`. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `Op::ValidateShape` arm (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch.rs:329`) → `run_named_shapes` (new, `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/shape_integrity.rs`). Code: `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/shape_integrity.rs`; types in `crates/medulla-types/src/shape_report.rs`: `IntegrityShape` (`required_property`, `orphan_node`, `dangling_edge`, `invalid_reference`), `Severity` (`info`, `warn`, `fatal`), `ShapeReport{shape_id, severity, focus, message}`. A `fatal` finding sets `ShapeReport.blocks = true`. The scan visits each node and edge once, so time is `O(N+E)`. Memory is `O(F)` for `F` findings, capped at 10000 per shape. Knob: `validate_shape.node_budget` (`validate_shape_node_budget` in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs`), default 1000000 nodes, `[1000, 10000000]`, bound into `Budget::max_shape_nodes` by the served planner (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs`). Severity is fixed per shape by `IntegrityShape::severity`: `orphan_node` is `info`, `required_property` is `warn`, `dangling_edge` and `invalid_reference` are `fatal`. Wire carrier (decided, on main): a kept `KEEP VIOLATING` row carries its most severe finding as text columns `shape.shape_id`, `shape.severity`, `shape.message`, `shape.blocks` (`"true"`/`"false"`) and `shape.findings` (the count), and the served `Uql` answer copies each row's text columns into `UqlRow.provenance` (`rows_of` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/serve.rs` reads `RowSet::row_texts`), so a client reads `row.provenance["shape.blocks"]`. A blocking finding in this row is data on the row, never a refusal: the opt-in refusal `FAIL ON FATAL` → `SHAPE_FATAL_FINDING` and the `coverage = gap` evidence are `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R005.701`, which adds the clause to `parse_validate_shape` (`crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/shape.rs`). Rule: the integrity shapes are run by the existing `Op::ValidateShape` stage; no parallel validator exists. Writes: none. Depends: R005. Shape: on main: `IntegrityShape::parse` in the `Op::ValidateShape` arm (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/shape.rs`) routes `integrity:<name>[:<key>]` to `run_named_shapes` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/shape_integrity.rs`); types in `crates/medulla-types/src/shape_report.rs`. The remaining work is the served test only. | Tests: `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/shape_integrity.rs::tests::finding_per_shape_and_bound` (L1): one violation per shape, severity and blocking flag, one visit per element. `tests/query/test_graph_integrity_shapes_served.py::test_violating_rows_carry_shape_columns` (L4): over a started server, a graph written through served `AddNode` with node `a` `{"owner": "x", "ref": "ghost"}` (no node `ghost` exists), node `b` `{"ref": "a"}` (no `owner`) and node `x` `{"owner": "x"}`; `MATCH () \|> VALIDATE SHAPE 'integrity:invalid_reference:ref' KEEP VIOLATING` returns exactly row `a` with `provenance["shape.severity"] == "fatal"` and `provenance["shape.blocks"] == "true"`; `VALIDATE SHAPE 'integrity:required_property:owner' KEEP VIOLATING` returns exactly `b` with `"warn"` and `"false"`; `KEEP CONFORMING` for `invalid_reference:ref` returns `b` and `x` and not `a`; a second tenant's same query returns no rows. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R005.701` | **Graph integrity validation: refusal and hot-path proof.** BUILD (the `coverage = gap` columns, the budget and the `blocks` flag are on main in R005.700; this row adds the opt-in refusal and the bench). Stage: query execution, served `Uql` `VALIDATE SHAPE`. Grammar (`crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/shape.rs`): `VALIDATE SHAPE <shape> [USING "<turtle>"] [PROJECT "<ns>"] [KEEP CONFORMING|VIOLATING] [FAIL ON FATAL]`; `FAIL ON FATAL` is the last clause, parsed by a new `parse_shape_fail_on_fatal` after `parse_shape_keep` (three keywords `FAIL`, `ON`, `FATAL`). Wire: `Op::ValidateShape` (`crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_core.rs`) gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")] fail_on_fatal: bool`; the same field is carried through the printer (`wire_query_uql.rs` and `wire_query_uql_modal.rs::validate_shape`, which prints ` FAIL ON FATAL` last), `wire_query_samples.rs`, `grammar.rs` line text, `crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/validation.rs:409`, the exec arm (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch.rs:387`) and `shape::validate_shape` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch/shape.rs`), whose signature gains `fail_on_fatal: bool`. Refusal: when `fail_on_fatal` is set and any kept row carries `shape.blocks = "true"`, `validate_shape` returns `Err("SHAPE_FATAL_FINDING: <shape_id> fatal=<n> warn=<n> info=<n>")`, where `<shape_id>` is the shape id of the first blocking row in row order and the counts are the kept rows' `shape.severity` values; no rows are returned. A `coverage = gap` result with the flag set is not a refusal (the graph was not scanned, so nothing is claimed). The code registers in the `"Uql"` list of `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs` beside `SHAPE_UNKNOWN`. No graph revision field is added (the served answer already carries the revision). Benchmark `crates/medulla-exec/benches/graph_integrity_validate.rs` with its `[[bench]]` entry in `crates/medulla-exec/Cargo.toml` budgets p50 under 50 ms and p99 under 250 ms per 100000 nodes, memory under 128 MiB; it never gates a PR. Caller chain: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_uql` (`uql_statement.rs:17`) → `Op::ValidateShape` arm (`dispatch.rs:387`) → `shape::validate_shape` → `run_named_shapes`. Writes: none. Depends: R005.700. | Test: `tests/query/test_graph_integrity_shapes_served.py::test_fatal_finding_blocks` (L4): over the R005.700 graph, `VALIDATE SHAPE 'integrity:invalid_reference:ref' KEEP VIOLATING FAIL ON FATAL` is refused `SHAPE_FATAL_FINDING` naming the shape id, the same statement without the clause returns row `a` with `shape.blocks == "true"`, and `integrity:required_property:owner` with `FAIL ON FATAL` returns row `b` (warn, not fatal); over a graph above `validate_shape.node_budget` every row carries `shape.coverage = gap:0/<budget>` and the clause does not refuse. Parser L1: `crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/shape.rs::tests::fail_on_fatal_round_trips`. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R703.100` | **The bench gate checks p99 ceilings.** Stage: release qualification (CI). `scripts/bench_gate.py` reads only the median (p50) of criterion's `estimates.json` today, so a p99 cap has no gate. The gate reads `target/criterion/<id>/new/sample.json`, computes each sample's per-iteration time as `times[i] / iters[i]` in nanoseconds, takes the nearest-rank 99th percentile, and fails when it exceeds the ceiling. Every listed bench must run with `sample_size(100)` or more; fewer than 100 samples, or a missing `sample.json` for a listed key, is a failure, never a skip. The failure line names the key, the measured p99 and the ceiling, and the exit status is 1, like the existing latency and recall checks. A p50 key and a p99 key may share an id. Writes: none. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R704` | **Agent knowledge scope (rollup).** Stage: retrieval; entries: served `Discover` and `ContextCompile`. Caller: `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/discover_plan.rs::plan_discover` and `handle_context_compile` → `crates/medulla-retrieval/src/knowledge_scope.rs``::scoped_top_k`. An agent profile binds a knowledge scope: knowledge sets, source container subtrees, source kinds and a time floor. Retrieval for that agent intersects the scope with the caller's access. A scope only narrows access. Per-resource grants use the one identity grant ledger (`MEDULLA-IDENTITY-R704.1`), not a second store: a grant row is `grant_id`, `subject` (`Principal` or `Role`), `resource_ref`, `protocol`, `operation`, `scope_ceiling`, `granted_at_ms` and `expires_at_ms`, written through `Method::RbacAdmin` by `RbacAdminOp::GrantResource(grant)` and ended by `RevokeResource { grant_id }`, admin-only under `security:admin` and hash-chain audited like the other `RbacAdmin` writes. The knowledge protocol is `ResourceProtocol::Knowledge`; the resource of a scope is each entry of its `knowledge_sets`, the operation is `discover` for `Discover` and `compile` for `ContextCompile`, and the principal is the verified caller. The decision is `IsolationLayer::resource_access_decision`, read under one policy revision with the query it guards, so a grant revoked before the snapshot never admits it. Access is denied by default: a knowledge set of the scope with no unexpired grant naming the caller (by principal or by one of its roles) for that protocol and operation is refused with `RESOURCE_ACCESS_DENIED`, one code and one detail for none, expired, revoked and unknown (no existence oracle); the code is in `ServerErrorCode` and routed for `Discover` and `ContextCompile` in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`; a refusal returns no rows and writes nothing. A scope with no `knowledge_sets` needs no grant. Children `.1` to `.6` deliver it. Depends: MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R808, MEDULLA-INGEST-WORKER-R904.2. Arbora supplies agent_ref through ARBORA-OPS-R883, which depends on this rollup. | Root L4 journey `tests/retrieval/test_knowledge_scope_served.py::test_agent_sees_only_scoped_sources` (L4). |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R704.4` | **Refusal.** Stage: retrieval. `SCOPE_SET_UNKNOWN` refuses a scope that names a knowledge set the caller cannot see; a set of another tenant and an absent set answer one code with one detail (no existence oracle). `SCOPE_AGENT_UNKNOWN` refuses an `agent_ref` with no definition node readable by the caller, with the same answer for a missing and a foreign node. `RESOURCE_ACCESS_DENIED` refuses a knowledge set of the scope that the caller holds no unexpired grant for, by the identity grant ledger of the rollup (`ResourceProtocol::Knowledge`, operation `discover` or `compile`). All three are routed for `Discover` and `ContextCompile`. A scope can never widen access: the result is always a subset of the unscoped result. A refusal returns no rows. Caller chain: `dispatch_request_method` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router.rs:194`) → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `run_dispatch_pipeline` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/pipeline.rs:391`) → `medulla_h_graph::graph_ops::try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/terminal.rs:51`) → `try_handle_semantic_compute` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/semantic.rs:281`) → `discover` (`semantic.rs:88`) → `plan_discover` (new, `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/discover_plan.rs`) → `scoped_top_k` (`crates/medulla-retrieval/src/knowledge_scope.rs:65`) → refusal. Shape: add `SCOPE_SET_UNKNOWN`, `SCOPE_AGENT_UNKNOWN` and `RESOURCE_ACCESS_DENIED` to `ServerErrorCode` (`crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs`) and to `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs` for both methods; `ContextCompile` has one scope check, the R704 check (its older `CONTEXT_SCOPE_FOREIGN` path is removed); the subset check reuses `KnowledgeScope::contains` (`knowledge_scope.rs:52`). | L4 `tests/retrieval/test_knowledge_scope_served.py::test_scope_refusals` asserts both codes and that the scoped result is a subset. L4 `tests/retrieval/test_knowledge_scope_served.py::test_resource_grants_default_deny` grants (`GrantResource`) and revokes (`RevokeResource { grant_id }`) through `RbacAdmin`, and asserts `RESOURCE_ACCESS_DENIED` with no grant, with an expired grant and after revoke, an unexpired grant admitting the read, and a non-admin `GrantResource` refused. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R704.6` | **Hot-path proof.** Entry: served `Discover`. Bench `crates/medulla-plan/benches/knowledge_scope.rs` adds a 50-container scope to a hybrid search over 1,000,000 documents. The scope never filters the whole corpus: the index leg returns its fused candidates in rank order, `scoped_top_k` receives only the first `knob retrieval.scope_candidates` of them (default 1024, [64, 16384], candidates, `Tunable`), and the bench measures that call, so the added cost is bounded by the candidate count, not the corpus size. A scope that leaves fewer than `k` hits inside that window returns what it found. The 50 container refs are tested through a `BTreeSet` built once per request, replacing the linear `admits` scan for lists above 8 entries. Key `knowledge_scope_50` in `crates/medulla-plan/benches/thresholds.json` caps added p99 at 2 ms. The p99 gate of MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R703.100 enforces the key. Caller chain: bench `crates/medulla-plan/benches/knowledge_scope.rs` calls `scoped_top_k` (`crates/medulla-retrieval/src/knowledge_scope.rs:65`), the call on the served chain `dispatch_request_method` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router.rs:194`) → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `run_dispatch_pipeline` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/pipeline.rs:391`) → `medulla_h_graph::graph_ops::try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/terminal.rs:51`) → `try_handle_semantic_compute` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/semantic.rs:281`) → `discover` (`semantic.rs:88`) → `plan_discover` (new, `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/discover_plan.rs`). | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R705.3` | **Wiring.** Stage: retrieval. Write method: `Method::RetrievalFeedbackRecord { document_ref: String, kind: FeedbackKind, weight: f64, #[serde(default)] stamp: Option<FeedbackStamp> }` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_05.rs`; `FeedbackStamp { tenant: String, principal: String, at: u64, idempotency_key: String }` and `RetrievalFeedback` (the stored event, now seven fields: tenant, principal, document_ref, kind, weight, at, idempotency_key) are in `crates/medulla-result-types/src/feedback_event.rs`, the one definition the handler, the replay and the reader share. A caller sends only `upvote` or `downvote`; `cited` or `ignored` on the wire refuses `INVALID_ARGUMENT: implicit feedback kinds are derived, never recorded`. `weight` must be finite and in `[-1.0, 1.0]`, else `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Write path (the gateway graph-write template is `ArgumentMapPut`; copy it at each site): served `RetrievalFeedbackRecord` → `dispatch_graph_op` → `is_gateway_routed` (`crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation/plan.rs:564`; add the name to `GATEWAY_ROUTED` at `plan.rs:99` and to the name arm at `plan.rs:535`) → `try_handle_gateway` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway.rs:57`) → new `Route::RetrievalFeedback` selected by `is_retrieval_feedback` in `ROUTE_MATCHERS` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph_routes.rs:192`) → `handle_record` (new, `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retrieval_feedback.rs`). `handle_record` overwrites `stamp` with `FeedbackStamp { tenant: verified.tenant(), principal: verified.principal_persistence_id(), at: authoritative_now_ms() / 1000, idempotency_key: ctx.idempotency_key.to_string() }` (a wire value is replaced, as `stamp_retired_by` does; `ctx.idempotency_key` is the request envelope's key, so a retry of one request names one node and two distinct requests never share a node), then commits one node upsert through `commit_gateway`: node id `retrieval-feedback:` + `Digest256::framed(b"eg/retrieval-feedback/v1", &[tenant, principal, document_ref, kind (snake_case), at (u64 big-endian), idempotency_key])` lowercase hex, computed by `RetrievalFeedback::node_id` from the stored `idempotency_key` field and never from the weight (`weight.to_bits()` is deleted from `node_id`: two distinct feedbacks of equal weight would otherwise collapse into one node); properties `type = "RetrievalFeedback"` plus the seven `RetrievalFeedback` fields (`from_node_properties` reads `idempotency_key` too, and the implicit events built by `implicit_events` carry an empty key because they are never stored). Durable replay: an `apply_retrieval_feedback` arm in `crates/medulla-core/src/durable_apply.rs` beside `apply_argument_map` (`:373`) rebuilds the same node from the logged, stamped method. Registration sites, each beside `ArgumentMapPut`: `MethodWriteFamily::GraphElement` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/families.rs:50`); result `RetrievalFeedbackRecord(RetrievalFeedbackRecord) => Json<medulla_retrieval::feedback_boost::RetrievalFeedback>` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/result_contract/query.rs:51`; policy `("RetrievalFeedbackRecord", spec(make_policy(true, DurabilityDomain::GraphRedb, "retrieval:feedback", PolicyFlags { idempotent: false, audited: true, emits_cdc: false }, TxnParticipation::Atomic), PYTHON, Stability::Stable), "one RetrievalFeedback node per explicit event; tenant and principal from the verified envelope")` in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/query.rs`; the write arm in `crates/medulla-server-auth/src/access/write_classification.rs:522`; the canonical arm in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/canonical.rs:679`; the audit line `RETRIEVAL_FEEDBACK_RECORD\|{document_ref}\|{kind}` in `crates/medulla-kernel/src/audit/control_lines.rs:280`; the method count in `crates/medulla-capabilities/tests/contract_registry.rs`; the client `async def record_retrieval_feedback(self, document_ref: str, kind: str, weight: float) -> dict` in `medulla/client.py`; regenerate `medulla/generated/*.py`. Read path: `plan_discover` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/discover_plan.rs`) calls new `load_feedback(view, store, tenant, candidates, now_s, knobs) -> Vec<DocumentBoost>` in `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retrieval_feedback.rs`. Explicit events: the visible `RetrievalFeedback` nodes of the caller's projected view whose `document_ref` is a candidate, newest `at` first, at most `knob feedback.events_per_doc` (default 1,000, [10, 100,000], events, `Tunable`) per document. Implicit events, decided here: the run credit receipt (`RunCreditReceipt`, `crates/medulla-decision-types/src/decision/statistical/run_credit.rs:129`) holds only counts and digests, so no receipt field maps to a row. The rows live in the stored retrieval outcome the receipt was credited from: `StoredRetrievalOutcome` (`crates/medulla-decision-types/src/decision/statistical/retrieval.rs:227`), read with `AgentLibraryStore::decision_artifacts_with_prefix(tenant, RETRIEVAL_OUTCOME_PREFIX, limit)` (`crates/medulla-persistence-records/src/decision_jobs.rs:223`, prefix `:293`), `limit = knob feedback.implicit_outcomes` (default 256, [16, 4,096], outcomes, `Tunable`). Mapping: each id in `outcome.cited` is a `cited` event of weight `knob feedback.cited_weight` (default 0.05, [0.0, 0.5], weight, `Tunable`); each `outcome.returned[].evidence_id` not in `outcome.cited` is an `ignored` event of weight `knob feedback.ignored_weight` (default -0.02, [-0.5, 0.0], weight, `Tunable`); `at = recorded_at_ms / 1000`, `principal = producer`, `tenant` the verified tenant. The pure mapping is `implicit_events(cited: &[String], returned: &[String], at: u64, principal: &str, tenant: &str, weights: (f64, f64)) -> Vec<RetrievalFeedback>` in `feedback_boost.rs`; nothing is written for implicit events (no copy between stores), and `record_run` appends none. Explicit and implicit events for each candidate go to `document_boost(document_ref, events, now_s, DEFAULT_HALF_LIFE_S)`, then `apply_boosts` runs after calibration. The five knobs register in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs`. Writes: `RetrievalFeedback` nodes only. Depends: MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.102.3. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R706.4` | **Refusal.** BUILD. A policy that names an unregistered tag refuses with `POLICY_TAG_UNKNOWN`. The set of known tags is the tenant's marking registry, one rule shared with `MEDULLA-TYPED-PACKS-R652.4`: a tag is known when the tenant graph holds a node `marking:<tag>` (label `Marking`; an ordinary graph write registers one; no promoted claim is needed). So a policy may be written before any claim exists; the order is register the marking, then write the policy, then promote claims. Where it fires: `load_policy_set` (R706.7, `crates/medulla-exec/src/policy_mask.rs`), not `apply_policies` (which stays infallible and pure). The loader first collects every `QueryPolicy` record and every `marking:*` node of the snapshot, then, after reading all records, returns `PolicyLoadError { code: "POLICY_TAG_UNKNOWN", record: <policy node id>, field: "tag" }` for the first policy (node-id order) whose `tag` has no marking node. `handle_uql` and the other read handlers already turn a `PolicyLoadError` into a refusal, so the query returns the error and runs nothing unmasked, the same fail-closed rule as `POLICY_RECORD_INVALID`. The marking label and node prefix are defined once, as `MARKING_LABEL = "Marking"` and `MARKING_NODE_PREFIX = "marking:"` in `crates/medulla-identity-types/src/classification.rs`, which `medulla-exec` reaches through a new dependency `medulla-identity-types = { path = "../medulla-identity-types" }` (that crate depends only on `medulla-types`, so no cycle). Add `PolicyTagUnknown => "POLICY_TAG_UNKNOWN"` to `ServerErrorCode` in `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs` and route it in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs` for the query methods. A query that asks for the raw value of a masked column is not refused; it receives the masked value. Existing proofs gain the registration: `policy_mask.rs::tests::loader_binds_policies_to_promoted_claim_columns_only` and `test_cross_tenant_policy_not_applied` write a `marking:<tag>` node before the policy. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R706.7, MEDULLA-TYPED-PACKS-R652.3 (the marking registry rule). | Test: `crates/medulla-exec/src/policy_mask.rs::tests::loader_refuses_a_policy_naming_an_unregistered_tag` (L2) and `tests/federated/test_policy_mask_served.py::test_mask_refusals` (L4): a policy-first write (policy, no marking) makes a UQL read answer `POLICY_TAG_UNKNOWN`; after the `marking:<tag>` node is written the same read answers masked values; a raw-value request of a masked column returns the masked value. |
Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R706.7, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R706.2

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R708.12` | **Read, list and retire a stored query name.** BUILD. Stage: stored-query workspace reads and retirement, extending R708.3. Entry: three new served methods beside `StoredQueryPut` and `StoredQueryCall`: `Method::StoredQueryGet { name, version? }` answers `StoredQueryView { name, version, language, text, params, materialize, refresh, share, author, created_at_ms }` (the highest published version when `version` is absent; `created_at_ms` is `null` for a version seeded before the put stamped it); `Method::StoredQueryList { limit?, cursor?, author?, include_retired }` answers `StoredQueryPage { queries: [{ name, latest_version, author, retired }], next_cursor }`; `Method::StoredQueryRetire { name, reason }` answers `StoredQueryRetired { name, retired, tombstone { reason, retired_at_ms, retired_by } }`. Scope: `StoredQueryGet` and `StoredQueryList` are reads under `query:read` (policy rows in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/query.rs`, RLS-routed in `crates/medulla-server-auth/src/access/read_classification.rs`); `StoredQueryRetire` is a write under `query:write` on the same staged gateway as put (`crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation/plan.rs`, `conditional.rs`, `crates/medulla-server-auth/src/access/write_classification.rs`). Caller chain: as R708.3, through `dispatch_stored_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/stored_query.rs`) to `handle_stored_get`, `handle_stored_list` and `handle_stored_retire`. Tenant and no existence oracle: every read runs over the caller's row-security view and the share scope of R708.8; a name that is missing, retired, outside the caller's share scope or authored by another principal (for retire), and a version that was never published, refuses (for `StoredQueryCall` too, replacing its former `ACCESS_DENIED` for an out-of-scope name) the one code `STORED_QUERY_NOT_FOUND` with the detail "no stored query" and the requested name, so the refusal does not say which. List: name order, `limit` in 1..=256 (the maximum when absent, else `STORED_QUERY_INVALID`), keyset cursor `encode_list_cursor` / `decode_list_cursor` (`crates/medulla-source-types/src/stored_query.rs`) built on `medulla_types::tenant_cursor` and bound to the reader's tenant (a cursor minted for another tenant refuses `STORED_QUERY_INVALID`); retired names are left out unless `include_retired`, and then carry their tombstone. Retire: a durable tombstone, not a delete. `StoredQueryRetireReason` is `superseded`, `deprecated`, `incorrect` or `unsafe`; the tombstone is the `retired` property of the head node `stored_query_head:<name>`, every version and result node stays, and from then on a call, a get and a put of that name refuse (`STORED_QUERY_NOT_FOUND`, `STORED_QUERY_NOT_FOUND` and `STORED_QUERY_IMMUTABLE`). Retiring an already retired name answers its existing tombstone and writes nothing. `handle_stored_put` stamps `created_at_ms` from the engine clock on each version node. No new `ServerErrorCode`. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R708.3, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R708.8. | Test: `crates/medulla-source-types/src/stored_query.rs::tests::a_list_cursor_resumes_only_for_the_tenant_that_minted_it` and `::the_retire_reason_and_the_tombstone_round_trip_on_the_wire` (L1). Test: `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/split_tests/stored_query_workspace_tests.rs` (L3): `get_and_list_hide_a_private_query_from_another_principal_exactly_like_a_missing_one`, `list_pages_by_cursor_in_name_order_and_refuses_a_foreign_cursor`, `retire_is_a_tombstone_only_the_author_can_write` and `call_refuses_missing_retired_out_of_scope_and_unpublished_names_identically`. Test: `tests/federated/test_stored_query_served.py::test_get_list_and_retire_a_stored_query` (L4). |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R709.7` | **Per-graph spatial index over geo literals.** Stage: SPARQL write and query. Caller chain: write: the commit of a graph that carries a `geo:wktLiteral` object → `SpatialIndex::apply` (new, `crates/medulla-rdf/src/spatial_index.rs`), called by the graph commit hook of `crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/sparql.rs`; query: `Method::Sparql` → `try_handle_sparql` (`crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/dispatch.rs:122`) → `expression.rs` `F::Custom(iri)` branch (`crates/medulla-rdf/src/sparql/expression.rs:223`) → `geosparql::eval_relation` (`crates/medulla-rdf/src/geosparql.rs:85`) → `SpatialIndex::candidates`. Rule: each graph holds one R-tree over the bounding boxes of its WKT literals, built with `crates/medulla-geo` (`SpatialIndex { graph, tree: RTree<(BBox, TermId)> }`, EPSG:4326 only). It is built on first use and then maintained on every write: an insert adds the literal's box, a delete removes it, in the same commit as the triples, so the index never disagrees with the graph. A literal that fails R709.4 is not indexed and the write refuses as R709.4 does. When the index is present, `sfWithin`, `sfIntersects`, `sfContains`, `sfOverlaps`, `sfEquals`, `sfTouches`, `sfCrosses`, `sfDisjoint` and `distance` with a constant bound take the candidate set from the tree and run the exact kernel test on the candidates only; when it is absent they scan as before, and both paths return the same rows. EXPLAIN (R709.5) reports `index_used` true when the tree served the function. Writes: the spatial index rows of the graph, in the graph commit. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R709.2, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R709.4. Shape: new `crates/medulla-rdf/src/spatial_index.rs` declared in `crates/medulla-rdf/src/lib.rs`, using the R-tree and box types of `crates/medulla-geo`; `eval_relation` consults it through one `Option<&SpatialIndex>` argument; no second index exists. | Test: `crates/medulla-rdf/tests/geosparql.rs::indexed_and_scanned_results_are_equal` (planned) (L2) compares both paths on 1,000 fixtures after inserts and deletes; `tests/federated/test_geosparql_served.py::test_explain_reports_index_used` (planned) (L4). |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R720.9` | **UQL union stage and valid-as-of window.** UQL gains a `UNION` stage that combines two match results into one candidate set, and a `VALID AS OF <window>` clause that bounds claims by a duration before the query time. The query time is the engine clock's reading for the request, handed to the parser (`parse_statement_at`); a parse without a query time refuses the duration form, so a parse is a function of its text, parameters and that reading. Both parse into the one plan; there is no second planner. | `crates/medulla-uql/tests/union_and_window.rs::union_and_valid_as_of_window_compile_into_one_plan` (L1). |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R720.6` | **Hot-path proof: one program, five modalities, one plan, modality named per stage.** Class: BUILD. Entry: served `Method::Uql`. One program holds stages of five modalities: graph (`MATCH`), vector (`RANK EMBED` family, `OpKind::RankEmbed`), federated source (`FOREIGN SCAN`, `OpKind::ForeignScan`), SPARQL (`SPARQL '<query>' VAR '<v>'`, `OpKind::SparqlBgp`) and epistemic (`BELIEF AS OF`, `OpKind::BeliefAsOf`). UQL has no stage keyword called SQL: a source read through the federation is the SQL-engine path, and its modality is named `foreign`. Model: the closed enum `UqlModality` in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_core.rs` beside `UqlStageReport` (`#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]`, `#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]`, `#[cfg_attr(feature = "contract-schema", derive(schemars::JsonSchema))]`) with variants `Graph, Vector, Text, Sparql, Foreign, Epistemic, Spatial, Tensor, Stream, Timeseries, Probabilistic, Udf`; `UqlStageReport` gains the field `pub modality: UqlModality` (after `clause`, no serde default: hard cutover). `pub fn uql_modality(op: &Op) -> UqlModality` in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_kinds.rs` beside `op_kind` is an exhaustive match on `OpKind` with no catch-all: `RankEmbed`, `RankMmr`, `ClusterByEmbedding` are `Vector`; `RankText`, `FuseRrf` are `Text`; `SparqlBgp`, `Reason`, `ValidateShape` are `Sparql`; `ForeignScan`, `Foreign` are `Foreign`; `EvidenceFor`, `EvidenceForEach`, `Contradicts`, `SupportedBy`, `BeliefAsOf`, `SourceReliability`, `ConfidenceOp`, `ExplainBelief` are `Epistemic`; `SpatialScan`, `Reproject`, `SpatialOp` are `Spatial`; `TensorScan`, `TensorOp` are `Tensor`; `Cep` is `Stream`; `SensorFuse`, `SensorAlign`, `TsScan`, `Derive`, `Skill`, `Evaluate`, `Motif`, `Events` are `Timeseries`; `Probabilistic` is `Probabilistic`; `Udf` is `Udf`; every other kind (scan, filter, traverse, expand, propagate, rank, order, group, window, limit, project and the rest) is `Graph`. The constructors change to `UqlStageReport::planned(clause: String, modality: UqlModality, estimated_rows: f64)` and `UqlStageReport::profiled(clause: String, modality: UqlModality, estimated_rows: f64, rows: u64, micros: u64)`; every call site passes `uql_modality(op)` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/serve.rs` at the `remote_ops`, `planned`, `profiled` sites and `crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/serve/dag.rs::reports`). Mirror the field and the enum in `medulla/generated/models.py` (`UqlStageReport`, new `UqlModality`). Caller chain: `dispatch_request_method` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router.rs:194`) → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `run_dispatch_pipeline` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/pipeline.rs:391`) → `route_query_gateway` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/gateway.rs:113`) → `medulla_h_query::query::try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:11`) → `dispatch_query_family` (`dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`). Exact program (the one text the test sends, one line, `EXPLAIN` prefix): `EXPLAIN LET g = MATCH (:Note); LET v = MATCH (:Note) |> RANK BY ~ 'alpha' |> LIMIT 5; LET f = FOREIGN SCAN 'peer'; LET s = SPARQL 'SELECT ?x WHERE { ?x a <https://example.invalid/T> }' VAR 'x'; LET e = MATCH (:Note) |> BELIEF AS OF @1700000000; JOIN g, v, f, s, e |> LIMIT 10`. `EXPLAIN` plans the DAG without executing it (`crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/serve/dag.rs::explain`), so the source name `peer`, the embedder and the epistemic store need not exist; the program must parse and plan. The five modalities are the stages `MATCH` and `LIMIT` (graph), `RANK BY ~ 'alpha'` (vector, `OpKind::RankEmbed`), `FOREIGN SCAN 'peer'` (foreign), `SPARQL ... VAR 'x'` (sparql) and `BELIEF AS OF @1700000000` (epistemic). The test target is gated `#![cfg(all(feature = "query", feature = "redb", feature = "security", feature = "federation", feature = "owl", feature = "epistemic"))]` beside R720.5's gate (the shared file gate becomes this list). Shape: the model above, the call-site edits, and one new test fn in `tests/uql_single_plan_all_modalities.rs` (the file exists and holds R720.5's test). The latency benchmark stays owned by MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R042.2 (`crates/medulla-plan/benches/hybrid_queries.rs`). Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R720.4. | Test: `tests/uql_single_plan_all_modalities.rs::five_stage_kinds_one_plan` (L3). It seeds one `Note` node, then sends `EXPLAIN` of a program with five `LET` bindings (the JOIN syntax of R722.6), one per stage kind, over the served path, and asserts: `UqlResult::Explain.stages` holds a stage whose `modality` is each of `graph`, `vector`, `sparql`, `foreign` and `epistemic`; `UqlResult::Explain.plan_root_count` is 1 (one DAG with one root: the program is one plan, R720.4), and `plan_node_count` equals the number of stages in `stages`. Unit proof: `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_kinds.rs::tests::every_op_kind_has_a_modality` (L1) maps `OpKind::all()` and asserts the five named kinds. |
Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R720.4

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R722.6` | **Hot-path proof.** Class: WRITE-TEST. Entry: served `ConnectorPack.Import`, then served `Method::Uql`. A fixture connector with a mock operation backs one data resource. The test approves the mapping through `ApproveSource`. It runs a UQL query that joins a native fact to the connector rows, then counts the stored nodes. The `FixtureSource` of `tests/connector_competency` (`MEDULLA-CQ-CONN-R067.8`) consumes this fixture connector. The mock executor stands for `FleetSourceExecutor` of ARBORA-FLEET-R042; the arbora side is proven by its own served test. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R722.10. Join syntax: the DAG syntax of `crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/program.rs` (`LET name = pipeline;` bindings, then `JOIN a, b`, which intersects the bindings' output rows by node id). The query text is `LET native = MATCH (:Note); LET live = FOREIGN SCAN '<SOURCE_ID>'; JOIN native, live`, where `<SOURCE_ID>` is `SOURCE_ID` of `tests/connector_competency/test_contract_kinds_served.py`. The native fact is one node of label `Note` whose id is `RECORDING_ITEMS[0]["id"]` (`item-1`), so exactly that connector row joins; `item-2` has no native counterpart and is absent from the answer. Test seam: `_run_journey(kind, clean_graph, tmp_path, connector=None, native_id=None)` and `_journey(kind, connector, fixture, client, text=None)` in `tests/connector_competency/test_contract_kinds_served.py` gain the optional parameters. When `native_id` is set, `_run_journey` adds the node (`clean_graph.nodes.add(native_id, {"type": "Note"})`) before the first `_stored_nodes` call, asserts `before == [native_id]` instead of `[]`, and asserts `after == before` at the end (the answer stores no connector row). When `text` is set, `_journey` uses it in place of `FOREIGN SCAN '<SOURCE_ID>'` for the `prepare=True` call, the lifecycle and the final `client.query.uql(text, foreign_executions=[execution])`. The plan digest pinned at open is the digest of the parsed DAG plan that `prepare=True` returned, so the prepare and the run use the same text. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.1` | **Low-storage ANN: manifest and byte accounting.** Stage: ingest, index build. Caller: ingest worker loop → `dispatch` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/dispatch.rs:14`) → ANN build → `LowStorageAnnManifest::admit` (new, `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs`). Types `LowStorageAnnManifest` and `StorageAccounting` sit beside HNSW and IVF-PQ. The manifest binds tenant, source snapshot, source id, revision, text digest, embedding space, encoding policy, generation, build policy and limits. Accounting sums graph, code, metadata, source-reference, cache and scratch bytes with checked addition. The artifact references source text and never copies it. Knob: config `ann_storage_ceiling_bytes` in `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-work-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`), default 2147483648, `[1048576, 1099511627776]`. Refusals: `ANN_MANIFEST_INVALID` (missing identity, mixed policies, non-finite counts), `ANN_BYTES_OVERFLOW`, `ANN_OVER_CEILING`, all before publication. The ANN refusal codes are an ingest-worker set, not served-method errors: they are the closed enum `LowStorageErrorCode` in `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs` (`as_str` gives the wire token), so no served method lists them. Writes: the manifest with the generation. Depends: none. Caller chain: ingest worker loop → `handle` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/dispatch.rs:14`) → `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/worker.rs:20` → ANN build → `LowStorageAnnManifest::admit` (new, `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs`). Shape: new `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs` beside the HNSW and IVF-PQ modules; reuse checked integer addition; config `ann_storage_ceiling_bytes` goes into `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-work-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`). | Test: `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs::tests::manifest_round_trip_and_sum` (L1). It checks every field and an independent byte sum. This row is bound to its L1 test only; the served root test belongs to R040.5.6. |
Depends: none
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.2` | **Low-storage ANN: compact graph artifact.** Stage: ingest, index build. Caller: ingest ANN build → `HnswIndex::to_compact(&self, request: ManifestRequest, tombstones: &[u64]) -> Result<LowStorageArtifact>` (built, `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs`; `HnswIndex` is `medulla_ann::HnswIndex`, `crates/medulla-ann/src/hnsw.rs`); `to_compact` measures the graph bytes (`artifact::encode_compact_without_vectors`) and the SQ8 code bytes (`sq8_encode`), writes them into `request.accounting`, and then calls `LowStorageAnnManifest::admit` (R040.5.1) so an over-ceiling artifact refuses before anything is published. The artifact is `LowStorageArtifact { manifest, graph, sq_min, sq_scale, codes, tombstones }` and `encode` / `decode(bytes, expected_generation)` carry magic `EGLSANN\x02`. Reuse `HnswIndex` traversal, the artifact codec and `IvfPq` SQ8 persistence. The compact mode stores stable row ids, bounded adjacency (at most `M` neighbours per node), compressed codes and tombstones. Format version byte `2`; existing version `1` artifacts stay readable. It adds no second authority and no second store. Refusal: `ANN_ARTIFACT_INVALID` for truncation, duplicates, out-of-range edges, shape mismatch, unknown version or missing source generation. The ANN refusal codes are an ingest-worker set, not served-method errors: they are the closed enum `LowStorageErrorCode` in `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs` (`as_str` gives the wire token), so no served method lists them. Writes: the artifact under the index directory. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.1. Caller chain: ingest worker loop → `handle` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/dispatch.rs:14`) → the ANN build step of the worker (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/worker.rs`; no caller of `to_compact` exists there yet, and R040.5.6 owns wiring it) → `HnswIndex::to_compact` → `LowStorageAnnManifest::admit`. Shape: `to_compact` and the version byte 2 codec are built in `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs`; reuse `HnswIndex` traversal and the `IvfPq` SQ8 persistence; version 1 artifacts stay readable. | Test: `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs::tests::compact_decode_and_refusals` (L1, built). It decodes adjacency, codes, tombstones and ids and compares candidates with the full-vector oracle. Verified by the root slice test. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.3` | **Low-storage ANN: revision-bound regeneration and rescore.** Stage: ingest and query. Caller: ingest worker loop → `dispatch` → `SemanticStore::regenerate_eligible` (`crates/medulla-semantic-index/src/semantic_regeneration.rs:208`). Decision: `regenerate_eligible` is the single write path; `MEDULLA-REPO-INGEST-R006.2` calls it. A replacement publishes only after `SemanticStore`, `GenerationStamp`, fences and `EmbeddingVersionStore` verify a complete generation. Compact search returns candidates without source fetches. Exact rescore fetches authorized rows under three budgets. Keys: `rescore_max_items` (default 200, `[1, 10000]`), `rescore_max_bytes` (default 8388608, `[65536, 1073741824]`), `rescore_max_ms` (default 500, `[10, 60000]`). Config in `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-work-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`). Results bind source digest, space, policy and generation. Refusals: `ANN_SOURCE_STALE`, `RESCORE_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: the replacement generation. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.2, MEDULLA-REPO-INGEST-R006.2. Caller chain: ingest worker loop → `handle` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/dispatch.rs:14`) → `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/worker.rs:20` → `SemanticStore::regenerate_eligible` (`crates/medulla-semantic-index/src/semantic_regeneration.rs:208`). Shape: extend `regenerate_eligible` with the generation checks; add the three `rescore_*` keys to `SchedulerConfig`; reuse `GenerationStamp` and `EmbeddingVersionStore`. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_low_storage_ann_served.py::test_selective_regeneration_and_rescore` (planned) (L4). It changes one source revision and asserts selective regeneration. It asserts no source fetch on compact search. A stale identity keeps the prior generation. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.4` | **Low-storage ANN: bounded embedding provider and cache.** Stage: ingest. Caller: `regenerate_eligible` → `EmbeddingProvider` (`crates/medulla-semantic-index/src/semantic.rs:302`, `BoundedCachingEmbeddingProvider` in `semantic_embedding_batch.rs:75`). The provider batches typed references under finite budgets. Tunables are registry knobs in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs`: `embed.batch_items` default 64 `[1, 1024]`. `embed.batch_bytes` default 1048576 `[4096, 67108864]`. `embed.concurrency` default 4 `[1, 64]`. `embed.calls_max` default 1000 per run `[1, 1000000]`. `embed.cache_entries` default 10000 `[0, 10000000]`. `embed.cache_bytes` default 67108864 `[0, 4294967296]`. The provider takes them as `EmbedLimits` (`semantic_embedding_batch.rs`) and re-checks the same bounds. One batch is cut by both the item and the byte ceiling; a wave of up to `embed.concurrency` batches runs together; the whole wave's calls are reserved against `embed.calls_max` before any call. The refusal codes are the closed `EmbedErrorCode` set. The cache key is `(tenant, source_identity, digest, embedding_space, policy_revision)`. The cache is a recomputable accelerator, not a store, and does not reuse `medulla-kvcache`. `medulla-core` stays independent of `medulla-plan`. Query text keeps using `TextEmbedder`. Refusals: `EMBED_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`, `EMBED_VECTOR_INVALID` (wrong width or non-finite), `EMBED_PROVIDER_FAILED`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none beyond the generation. Depends: R040.5.3. Caller chain: ingest worker loop → `handle` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/dispatch.rs:14`) → `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/worker.rs:20` → `regenerate_eligible` (`crates/medulla-semantic-index/src/semantic_regeneration.rs:208`) → `EmbeddingProvider` (`crates/medulla-semantic-index/src/semantic.rs:302`). Shape: extend `BoundedCachingEmbeddingProvider` (`semantic_embedding_batch.rs:75`) with the stated batch and cache keys in `SchedulerConfig`; the cache is an in-memory accelerator keyed as stated. | Test: `crates/medulla-semantic-index/src/semantic_embedding_batch.rs::tests::budgets_keys_and_duplicates` (L2). A hit never crosses tenant, digest, space or policy; duplicates count one effect. Verified by the root slice test. |
Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.3
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.5` | **Low-storage ANN: optional hub-preserving pruning.** Stage: ingest, index build. Caller: `HnswIndex::to_compact` → `prune_hubs` (new, `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs`). `HubPruningPolicy{degree_threshold, retention, tie_rule, revision, seed_digest}`; config key `ann_hub_pruning` in `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-work-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`), default `disabled`. The default fixture policy: `degree_threshold` 32, `retention` 0.5, `tie_rule` node id ascending. Pruning removes redundant non-hub edges only and keeps hubs. The manifest records the policy; a caller cannot infer it from untrusted text. `prune_hubs(index, manifest, policy)` maps the policy onto the existing `PrunePolicy` transform (`crates/medulla-ann/src/hnsw/pruning.rs`): `degree_threshold` is the hub threshold, `retention` scales the layer-0 degree kept on non-hub nodes. The manifest records the policy and a policy that differs from it refuses `ANN_PRUNING_INVALID`. Disabled pruning leaves topology byte-identical. Refusals: `ANN_PRUNING_INVALID` (bad bounds, missing digest, mismatched policy), `ANN_PRUNING_DISCONNECTED`. The ANN refusal codes are an ingest-worker set, not served-method errors: they are the closed enum `LowStorageErrorCode` in `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs` (`as_str` gives the wire token), so no served method lists them. Writes: the pruned artifact. Depends: R040.5.2. Caller chain: ingest worker loop → `handle` (`crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/dispatch.rs:14`) → `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/semantic_index/worker.rs:20` → `HnswIndex::to_compact` → `prune_hubs` (new, `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs`). Shape: add `prune_hubs` and `HubPruningPolicy` to `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs`; config key `ann_hub_pruning` in `SchedulerConfig`; disabled leaves topology byte-identical. | Test: `crates/medulla-ann/src/low_storage.rs::tests::pruning_disabled_identical_enabled_connected` (L1). Each fixture reports recall and storage with no universal target. Verified by the root slice test. |
Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.2
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.6` | **Low-storage ANN: authenticated integrated path and measured proof.** Stage: query execution. Caller: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `run_unified_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:325`) → `medulla_plan::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) → `LowStorageAnnManifest` receipt fields via `PlanCtx`. The receipt is the `ann` member of `RetrievalReceipt` (`TraceAnn`): `examined` (rows the rank visited: every candidate of an exact scan, or the window each widening round of the filtered walk asked of the index), `compacted` (the resident index holds no tombstones) and `visit_bound_hit` (`examined` above `4 * result_rows + 64`, `ann_visit_bound`). It is read from the `RowSet` the vector rank returns and reaches the answer through `execute_ranked`. The binding of source, model, space, build workspace, policy, generation and accounting rides on the manifest of R040.5.1, which the index owns: when an index is built its tenant, snapshot, source id, revision, text digest, encoding policy, build policy, source generation and byte ceiling (`IndexIdentity`) are persisted with the index, and the compaction worker (`SemanticStore::compact_low_storage`, called from the index `full_rebuild`) reads them from there to call `HnswIndex::to_compact`. No caller passes them in, and an index with no identity refuses `ANN_MANIFEST_INVALID`. It adds no served operation, MCP server, harness store or source registry. The proof compares the path with an exact full-scan baseline on fixed workloads. It records bytes, rows, visits, latencies, rescore, batch, cache and recall. No external storage figure is a target. Refusals: budgets exhausted, stale generations, unauthorized reads and incomplete receipts fail before any effect. Depends: R040.5.1, R040.5.2, R040.5.3, R040.5.4. Caller chain: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_request_method` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router.rs:194`) → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `run_dispatch_pipeline` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/pipeline.rs:391`) → `route_query_gateway` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/gateway.rs:113`) → `medulla_h_query::query::try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:11`) → `dispatch_query_family` (`dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `run_unified_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:325`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`). Shape: add the receipt fields to the `PlanCtx` carried through `execute`; no new method, server or store; the proof reuses the exact full-scan baseline already in the retrieval served tests. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_low_storage_ann_served.py::test_selectivity_bound_and_readback` (L4). A temporary engine builds, updates, restarts, queries and reads back. It asserts the `4 * result_rows + 64` visit bound at the three corpus sizes and every refusal. The restart step is proved in process by the L3 test `crates/medulla-semantic-index/tests/low_storage_restart.rs::compaction_worker_binds_the_identity_the_index_persisted_across_a_reopen`, which reopens the store and reads the manifest identity back. |
Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.1, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.2, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.3, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.5.4

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.103.2` | **Binning kernel and caps.** Kernel: `bin_scatter` and `bin_line` in new `crates/medulla-decision-stats/src/viewport.rs`. Scatter cap is `min(pixel_width * pixel_height, 262144)` bins. Line cap is `min(4 * pixel_width, 65536)` bins. The response byte cap is `min(16777216, 65536 + 96 * max_bin_count)`, measured after serialization. The effective ceiling is `min(byte_ceiling, response cap)`. A ceiling too small for the envelope refuses `BYTE_CEILING_TOO_SMALL` before any scan. Scatter bins return stable id, count and axis extrema, using a uniform grid of the viewport domain. Line bins use one bin per pixel column and return first, last, min, max, count and a gap flag. A bin set never truncates silently. A cap overflow refuses `VIEWPORT_CAP_EXCEEDED`. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.103.5` | **Block aggregates for series viewport reads.** Subsystem: time-series store. Each stored chunk of a series keeps its count, first and last values, minimum and maximum per field, written in the same transaction as the points it summarizes. `bin_line` over a viewport that fully covers a chunk folds that chunk's aggregate instead of its points, and decodes only the chunks the window cuts. Caller chain: `handle_viewport_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/viewport.rs`) → `SeriesStore::range_blocks_scoped` (new, `crates/medulla-tsdb/src/store.rs`). The stored chunk format changed, so a store written before this row is rewritten by the operator. The result equals the point scan; only the cost changes. Needed by the 1,000,000,000-point nightly of R081.103.4. Depends: R081.103.3. | Test: `crates/medulla-tsdb/src/store.rs::ordered_chunk_tests::block_aggregates_match_point_scan` (L2), and `crates/medulla-decision-stats/src/viewport.rs::tests::block_fold_equals_point_fold` (L2). |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R722.11` | **Served `Method::Uql` binds many approved foreign sources.** Stage: query. Entry: served `Method::Uql` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_05.rs`, variant `Uql`). Caller chain: a client or arbora call `medulla/client.py` `uql(..., foreign_executions=[...])` → wire dispatch `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs` (the `Method::Uql` arm) → `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs::handle_uql` → `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs::served_legs_with_foreign_executions` → `ServedPlanLegs::resolve` → `Op::ForeignScan` in `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch.rs` → `FederatedExec::foreign_scan` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt/federated.rs`). Rule: `Method::Uql` replaces its single `foreign_execution: Option<ForeignReadExecutionRef>` with `foreign_executions: BoundedVec<ForeignReadExecutionRef, 16>` (`#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "BoundedVec::is_empty")]`), the shape `Method::UnifiedQuery` already carries. Hard cutover: the singular field is removed from `Method::Uql` in the same change, with no alias and no compatibility reader; `Method::UnifiedQuery` is unchanged. `handle_uql` takes `foreign_executions: &[ForeignReadExecutionRef]` in place of the `Option`, and calls `served_legs_with_foreign_executions` directly. Every execution's `query_digest` must equal the server-computed `foreign_uql_query_digest(text, params)`. Each execution binds one approved source through R722.9 and R722.10, and one query joins all of them. Approved `owl:sameAs` links stay a `Method::SourceIngest` step (MEDULLA-CQ-XSRC-R004.5); this row writes none. `CALL STORED` and the harness-metric statement take no executions, and `prepare` carries none. Refusals: `FOREIGN_READ_QUERY_MISMATCH` when any digest differs (existing code); `FOREIGN_READ_EXECUTION_DUPLICATE` when two entries carry the same `execution_id`; `INVALID_ARGUMENT` when `prepare` or `CALL STORED` carries executions, when more than 16 entries arrive (bounded decode), or when a request still names the removed `foreign_execution` field (the `Uql` variant refuses unknown fields). `FOREIGN_READ_EXECUTION_DUPLICATE` is added to `ServerErrorCode` and the refusal table beside `FOREIGN_READ_PLAN_MISMATCH`.
