| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R730` | **The unified planner earns its scope through a falsification gate.** Caller: the nightly suite (`MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R178`) runs `benches/unified_vs_contracts.rs`. Workload: 10,000 claims with 1,024-dimension embeddings and 8 attributes, under 4 concurrent writers. The query is a two-hop neighbourhood joined with the top 50 vector matches and an attribute filter. Arm A runs one R720 plan in one engine. Arm B runs the vector match and the graph query on two engine instances. The client joins their results over the public contract. Rule: arm A passes when its p95 latency is at most 0.7 times arm B's. Recall at 50 must be equal, and no write may be lost. A failed run records a `PLANNER_SCOPE_FROZEN` decision (`MEDULLA-SDLC-R005`). While that decision stands, no new R720 or R721 operator may land. The thresholds are `Fixed` knobs. Caller chain: nightly suite (`MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R178`) → `benches/unified_vs_contracts.rs` → served `Method::Uql` and the two-engine client join. Shape: new bench `benches/unified_vs_contracts.rs` plus the checker `tests/test_unified_planner_gate.py`; the freeze decision is recorded through `MEDULLA-SDLC-R005`. The bench writes a report of both arms and `scripts/unified_planner_gate.py` judges it. The thresholds are the `Fixed` knobs `planner.gate.*` of `knob-table.md`. The planner scope is the explicit state machine `PlannerScopeMachine` (states `clear` and `frozen`, events `gate_failed` and `gate_passed`, one transition function, described in `state-machines-v1.ttl`). The decision is stored as a `PlannerGateRun` node whose `decision` is `PLANNER_SCOPE_FROZEN`, and `landing_refusal` refuses a new operator while the state is `frozen`. | Test: `benches/unified_vs_contracts.rs` (L6), checked by `tests/test_unified_planner_gate.py::test_failed_gate_records_the_freeze_decision`. |
Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R720, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R178, MEDULLA-SDLC-R005
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R731` | **Queries declare a latency class.** Caller: the served `UnifiedQuery` and SPARQL handlers. Every query carries the class `interactive` (the default) or `analytical`. An interactive query reads only materialized entailments and runs no live reasoning. It must answer within p99 200 ms at the reference load and within `knob query.interactive_node_budget` (default 100,000 nodes, range [1,000, 10,000,000]). Exceeding the budget refuses with `QUERY_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, with a hint to resubmit as analytical. An analytical query runs as a durable job and stores its result. Entailment maintenance runs incrementally off the write path. BUILD; the knob `query.interactive_node_budget` is registered (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs:541`) and read by nothing. Wire: `#[serde(default)] latency_class: LatencyClass` on `Method::UnifiedQuery` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_05.rs:100`) and `Method::Sparql` (`method_07.rs:137`), with `LatencyClass { #[default] Interactive, Analytical }` (snake_case) new in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_core.rs`. Interactive: `Budget` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/budget.rs:13`) gains `max_interactive_nodes: Option<usize>` (`None` for an analytical run), set by `run_unified_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:406`) from the knob; `execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) adds each executed stage's output row count and each traversal's visits to one meter and refuses `QUERY_BUDGET_EXCEEDED: the query touched more than {n} nodes; resubmit with latency_class analytical` the moment it passes the bound; an interactive plan holding `Op::Reason` (live reasoning) refuses the same code before execution. For `Sparql` the meter is the solution count before projection. Analytical: the handler (`handle_unified_query`, `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`; `try_handle_sparql`, `crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/dispatch.rs:122`) submits a job instead of running the query, through the existing analytics-job submit path (`handle_submit`, `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs.rs`) with new `JobKind::AnalyticalQuery { query: AnalyticalQuery }` in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/jobs.rs`, `AnalyticalQuery` = `Unified { plan_msgpack: Vec<u8> }` (the named MessagePack of the `Plan`, because `medulla-work-types` does not carry the `query` feature that defines `Plan`) \| `Sparql { query: String, base_iri: String, type_convention: String }`, and `JobKind::AnalyticalQuery` also carries the stamped `tenant_scope` and `agent` as the other governed kinds do; the handler builds the `SubmitJobSpec` itself with `graph` the request graph and `tenant` and `actor` from the verified context (never from the client), and the query is the job's input exactly as `MineAssociate.transactions` is (the `input_payload` doc in `crates/medulla-jobs/src/model.rs:231` names `AnalyticalQuery` as the one kind whose payload holds a query, read only by the in-engine executor and never logged). The served call answers the freshly durable `Submitted` job record (its `job_id`), the same `AnalyticsJobRecord` that `AnalyticsJob { op: Submit }` answers (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/result_contract/coordination.rs:163`); the result contract of both methods becomes an untagged answer: the existing result for `interactive`, `AnalyticsJobRecord` for `analytical`, declared in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/result_contract/query.rs`. Execution: the in-engine claim executor `execute_claim` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs/executor_claim.rs:7`) gets an `AnalyticalQuery` arm that runs the query with no node budget through `run_unified_off_lock_with` (`planning.rs:877`) or the SPARQL evaluator, reading rows only through the job actor's row-level view (the `executor_quality::ActorRows` pattern), and stages then publishes a `TypedJobResult` whose `rows` are the result rows (one map per row: `id`, `score`, the channels by name); `reads_graph_rows_server_side` (`jobs.rs:651`) returns `false` for it because submit reads no rows. Read: `AnalyticsJob { op: JobOp::Status { job_id } }` returns the job and, once published, its `TypedJobResult` output; no second read method exists. Register `QueryBudgetExceeded => "QUERY_BUDGET_EXCEEDED"` in `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs` and add it to the `UnifiedQuery` and `Sparql` lists in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Python client: `unified_query(..., latency_class="interactive")` and `sparql(..., latency_class="interactive")` in `medulla/client.py`; regenerate `medulla/generated/*.py`. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R732` | **The unified planner improves through the evolution loop (rollup).** Stage: query execution and downtime evolution. Children deliver it, in build order: `.1` the plan record store, `.2` the cost head revision, `.3` the shadow run, `.4` the `planner_evolution` job and `.5` promotion. The planner's cost-model coefficients and operator choices are knobs (`MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R179`) and a fitted cost head ranks candidate plans; a revised knob set or cost head is a challenger in the `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R805` lifecycle that runs only in shadow and is promoted only on shadow evidence, so no live query is answered by an unpromoted planner. Depends: MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R805, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R811.7, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R091.3. | The root journey test is `tests/query/test_planner_evolution_served.py::test_recorded_plans_train_a_challenger_that_promotes_only_on_improvement` (L4, bound to `.5`). |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R732.3` | **Shadow run.** Class: BUILD. Caller chain: `dispatch_request_method` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router.rs:194`) → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `run_dispatch_pipeline` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/pipeline.rs:391`) → `route_query_gateway` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/gateway.rs:113`) → `medulla_h_query::query::try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:11`) → `dispatch_query_family` (`dispatch.rs:66`) → `dispatch_sql_query` (`dispatch.rs:127`) → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:532`) → the shadow branch of `execute`. The challenger never answers a query. `execute` serves the champion's rows exactly as without a challenger. When `PlanRecordStore::challenger()` is `Some`, `PlanCtx` marks the run interactive (`PlanCtx::with_interactive(true)`, set by `handle_uql`; downtime, replay and R730 gate runs leave it `false` and are never sampled) and the `plan_digest` hashes below `knob query.planner_shadow_fraction` (default 0.05, [0.0, 0.2], fraction, `Tunable`) of the digest space, `execute` also plans and runs the plan under the challenger revision on the same `PlanCtx` and snapshot after the champion rows are final, and records `ShadowComparison { tenant, shape_digest, champion_plan_digest, challenger_plan_digest, champion_estimated_cost: f64, challenger_estimated_cost: f64, champion_cost_us: u64, challenger_cost_us: u64, challenger_result_digest: Option<String>, result_match: bool, challenger_failed: bool, recorded_at_ms: i64 }` in `PlanRecordStore` (same per-tenant bound and eviction as the records). The challenger's rows are digested with `RowSet::result_digest` and dropped; a challenger error or panic sets `challenger_failed` and never reaches the caller, and the served answer is returned whether or not the shadow run succeeds. The old `query.planner_canary_fraction` knob and any canary run that served the challenger's result are removed; no knob or flag lets a challenger answer a query. Writes: the in-memory store only. Depends: R732.1, R732.2. | Test: `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs::tests::shadow_challenger_never_serves_its_result` (L2) runs a challenger that returns different rows and one that errors, with `knob query.planner_shadow_fraction` at 0.2 over 200 interactive runs, and asserts every returned `RowSet` equals the champion's, a non-interactive run records no comparison, and the differing challenger records `result_match` false and the failing one `challenger_failed` true. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R732.4` | **The `planner_evolution` downtime job.** Class: BUILD. New downtime intent `planner_evolution`, added to `DOWNTIME_INTENTS` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs.rs:124`) and declared as a `dw:JobType` in the downtime ontology of MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.1; `run_tick` runs it in process like `plan_evolution` (MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R091.3, `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime_plan_evolution.rs` gains `run_planner_evolution`). It refits each `op_kind` correction as the median of `measured_us / estimated` over the tenant's `PlanExecutionRecord`s (a challenger revision stored in `PlanRecordStore` as `challenger()`), needing at least `knob query.planner_min_records` (default 64, [16, 4,096], records, `Tunable`) records, else it does nothing. Writes: the in-memory challenger only. Depends: R732.1, R732.2. | Test: `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime_plan_evolution.rs::tests::planner_evolution_refits_corrections_from_records` (L2: records with a constant 2x measured/estimated ratio give a challenger correction of 2.0; fewer than the minimum gives none). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R732.5` | **Promotion on shadow evidence.** Class: BUILD. Estimate error is scored on the `ShadowComparison`s, not by re-execution: per comparison `|challenger_estimated_cost - champion_cost_us| / max(champion_cost_us, 1)` against `|champion_estimated_cost - champion_cost_us| / max(champion_cost_us, 1)`, p95 over the comparisons; cost is `challenger_cost_us` against `champion_cost_us` of the same comparison, p95 over the comparisons; each side needs at least `knob query.planner_min_records` comparisons. When the challenger's p95 estimate error and p95 cost both improve and no comparison has `result_match` false or `challenger_failed` true, the job writes a `PolicyEvaluation` (`crates/medulla-decision-outcome-types/src/policy_evolution/records.rs:308`) with `subject: RevisionSubject::KnobSet { policy_id: "planner" }`, `revision_ref` the challenger digest, `champion_revision_ref` the champion digest, the shadow evidence (comparison count, both p95 pairs, mismatch count) and a passing verdict, then `LearningWrite::MovePointer` (`crates/medulla-decision-types/src/decision/statistical/retrieval.rs:358`) moves the `planner` pointer through the decide path (`stat_learning.rs:535`); `execute` reads the champion through that pointer, so only a promoted revision ever answers a query. A challenger that loses either measure, or shows one differing result digest, gets the same `PolicyEvaluation` with a failing verdict and stays a challenger. The R730 gate runs against whatever revision the pointer names. Each cost-head revision is a component revision of kind `PlannerCostHead` (component id `planner`, body in the `planner.cost_head` attribute, `content_digest` `sha256:<cost-head digest>`) published through the component-revision store the R805 gate reads; the gate's published-revision lookup admits that kind beside `DecisionPolicy`. The champion pointer is the engine-wide `KnobSet { policy_id: "planner" }` champion pointer, kept for the engine's service tenant in the commons graph, and the store's champion is rebuilt from it at startup. The job runs as the engine's service principal `engine:planner-evolution` with exactly the scopes the path checks: `query:decide`, `agent:decision-write`, `admin:decision-head`, `admin:policy-evaluation-write`, `admin:decision-policy`, and `admin:decision-catalog` (for the one `FeatureSchema` pin `Decide` requires). The evaluation names `GraderKind::TraceMetric`, the deterministic metric grader (no model judge). The metric mapping: `task_success_ppm` and `baseline_task_success_ppm` are the mean estimate accuracy `1 - min(error, 1)` of the challenger and of the champion over the comparisons; `trace_completeness_ppm` is the row-digest agreement rate; `unsupported_replay_mass_ppm` is the share of failed challenger runs; `cost_micros` is the challenger's p95 cost and `latency_p50_ms` / `latency_p95_ms` its p50 and p95 in milliseconds; each comparison is one held-out id `shadow:<digest>` and `evaluation_set_digest` is the digest of the ordered id list. Shadow comparisons are cleared whenever the challenger or the champion changes. Writes: `PolicyEvaluation` and the pointer move only. Depends: R732.3, R732.4, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R805, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R811.7. | Test: `tests/query/test_planner_evolution_served.py::test_recorded_plans_train_a_challenger_that_promotes_only_on_improvement` (L4): served queries fill the record store; with a fixture that makes the champion's corrections wrong, a tick promotes the challenger (a passing `PolicyEvaluation`, the pointer moved); with corrections already right, the challenger stays and its failing evaluation is readable; every served answer in the test equals the champion's, byte for byte, before and after the promotion. |
Depends: MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R805, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R811
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R737` | **Typed tabular channel on RowSet.** `RowSet` carries named, typed columns and rows of typed values in column order. `id` and `score` are ordinary columns. `GROUP` and every projection write through this one channel; there is no second result shape. Caller chain: `handle_unified_query` → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` → `RowSet`. Shape: `crates/medulla-exec/src/rowset.rs` holds one cell table (`Cell` = `Int`, `Float`, `Text` or `Bool`, keyed by row id and column name, columns in first-written order); `RowSet::columns` returns `Column { name, ty }` with `id` (text) and `score` (float) first and `RowSet::typed_rows` the values in that order; the float value channels (`values`, `with_values`) and the text columns (`texts`, `with_texts`) are typed views of that one table; `GROUP` writes its keys and aggregates with `RowSet::with_cell`; `RETURN` (`Op::Project`) narrows the table with `RowSet::project` (text columns, the row's source provenance, stay); the UQL encoder reads every numeric cell (`RowSet::numeric_values`). Depends: none. | `crates/medulla-exec/tests/rowset_columns.rs::group_and_projection_share_one_typed_row_shape` (L1). |
Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R730, MEDULLA-SDLC-R021.3
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R733` | **UQL aggregates rows by key: the GROUP stage.** Subsystem: UQL grammar and executor. The browser graph views need counts by label, degree counts and object-set aggregates, and UQL has COUNT only as a time-window aggregate. Add the stage `"GROUP" "BY" name { "," name } "AGGREGATE" agg_item { "," agg_item }` with `agg_item = ( "COUNT" [ "(" name ")" ] | ( "SUM" | "MIN" | "MAX" | "AVG" ) "(" name ")" ) [ "AS" name ]`. One row per key group; columns are the keys then the aggregates. Plan node `Group{keys, aggs}`. Example: `MATCH () |> GROUP BY label AGGREGATE COUNT AS n |> LIMIT 100`. Bounds: at most 100000 groups per query (refusal `UQL_GROUP_LIMIT`); the knob `uql.group.max_groups` starts at 100000. A key or aggregate field that does not exist refuses `UQL_UNKNOWN_FIELD`. Caller chain: served `Method::Uql` -> `crates/medulla-uql/src/parser` -> plan -> executor `crates/medulla-exec`. Shape: one production in `crates/medulla-uql/src/grammar.rs` (the single source of the EBNF block, the NL prompt and the expected-token lists), one `Op::Group { keys, aggs: Vec<GroupAgg> }` variant in the plan AST (`crates/medulla-types/src/wire_query_core.rs` (planned), where `Op` lives) and one executor arm (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/group.rs`). A key or aggregated field is read from the row's typed cells, else its node's properties; `label` reads the node label from the fields `Scan` reads. Output: one row per key tuple in key order, row id the key tuple joined by `|`, keys as text cells, `COUNT` as int and `SUM`/`MIN`/`MAX`/`AVG` as float; an aggregate's column is its alias, else `count` or `<func>_<field>`, and a later `RETURN` may name it. A served `Uql` answer with `GROUP` and no `RETURN` returns the aggregate columns. The bound is `Budget::max_groups`, set by the served planner from the knob. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R730, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R737. Decision: `GROUP` returns its rows through the typed tabular channel of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R737`. | Test: `crates/medulla-uql/tests/group_stage.rs::group_by_label_counts_rows`, `crates/medulla-uql/tests/group_stage.rs::unknown_field_is_refused` and `tests/query/test_uql_group_served.py::test_node_type_counts_over_the_wire` (L2, L4). |
Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R730, MEDULLA-SDLC-R021.3, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R737
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R734` | **UQL orders and pages rows: the ORDER and SKIP stages.** Subsystem: UQL grammar and executor. Add `"ORDER" "BY" name [ "DESC" ] { "," name [ "DESC" ] }` and `"SKIP" ( int | param )`. `ORDER BY` is a stable sort with the row id as the last key, so a page is repeatable; `SKIP n |> LIMIT k` is the page. Paging past 100000 rows refuses `UQL_SKIP_LIMIT`. Plan nodes `Order{keys}` and `Skip{n}`. Example: `MATCH (:Doc) |> ORDER BY updated DESC |> SKIP 100 |> LIMIT 100`. Caller chain: served `Method::Uql` -> parser -> executor. Shape: two productions in `grammar.rs`, two `Op` variants and two executor arms. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R733. | Test: `crates/medulla-uql/tests/order_skip_stage.rs::order_is_stable_with_row_id_tiebreak` and `crates/medulla-uql/tests/order_skip_stage.rs::skip_past_the_cap_is_refused` (L2). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R735` | **UQL reads edges as rows: the EDGES source.** Subsystem: UQL grammar and executor. `TRAVERSE` returns nodes, and the relationship and graph3d views need edges. BUILD: nothing of this source exists on main (no production, no `Op` variant, no printer arm, no executor arm). Add the source `"EDGES" [ ":" name ] [ "WHERE" pred ]`. Example: `EDGES :dependsOn WHERE weight >= 0.5 |> ORDER BY id |> LIMIT 200`. Caller chain: served `Method::Uql` → `handle_uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/uql_statement.rs`) → `medulla_uql::parse_statement` → `Op::ScanEdges` → `run_unified_with` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/planning.rs:339`) → `medulla_exec::exec::execute` → the `Op::ScanEdges` arm of `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/dispatch.rs` → `scan_edges`. Build, one file at a time, following the landed `SKIP`/`DECISIONS` shape: (1) wire: `Op::ScanEdges { rel: Option<String>, preds: Vec<Pred> }` in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_core.rs` beside `Op::DecisionScan`, with a doc comment naming this row; `OpKind::ScanEdges` in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_kinds.rs` (the `Op` → `OpKind` arm, the `None` modality-family list beside `OpKind::DecisionScan`, and the `ProofRole::Unproved` list beside it); a sample in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_samples.rs` (`Op::ScanEdges { rel: Some("dependsOn".into()), preds: vec![] }`); the printer arm in `crates/medulla-query-types/src/wire_query_uql.rs` printing `EDGES[ :rel][ WHERE <preds>]` with the same predicate printer `decision_scan` uses; the identity arm in `crates/medulla-uql/src/print.rs` beside `Op::DecisionScan`. (2) grammar: one production `p("edges", Source, &["EDGES"], None, "\"EDGES\" [ \":\" name ] [ \"WHERE\" pred ]", "ScanEdges{rel, preds} (one row per visible edge)", "EDGES :dependsOn WHERE weight >= 0.5 \|> ORDER BY id \|> LIMIT 200")` in `crates/medulla-uql/src/grammar.rs` after `decisions`; `("EDGES", Self::edges)` in `Parser::stage_table` (`crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/mod.rs`, the array length grows by one); `fn edges(&mut self) -> Result<Op, UqlError>` in `crates/medulla-uql/src/parser/basic.rs`: an optional `:` then a name for `rel`, then `WHERE` → `self.pred_list()?` as `decisions` does. (3) executor: new file `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/edges.rs` (declared `mod edges;` in `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs`) with `pub(super) fn scan_edges(ctx: &PlanCtx, rel: Option<&str>, preds: &[Pred]) -> Result<RowSet, String>`, and the arm `Op::ScanEdges { rel, preds } => super::edges::scan_edges(ctx, rel.as_deref(), preds)` plus `Op::ScanEdges { .. }` in the source-op list of `dispatch.rs`. `scan_edges` refuses a non-relational predicate exactly as `decision_scan` does (`pred_eval::is_relational`). It walks `ctx.view.edge_properties` in `(source, target)` key order and each key's blobs in stored order with ordinal `ord` (the same `(source, target, ord)` triple `GetEdgesPage` pages by), decodes each blob with `medulla_types::msgpack::decode_property_value`, keeps it when `pin_admits(ctx.as_of_pin, &props)` holds (as `EdgeFilter::admitted` in `crates/medulla-exec/src/exec/expand.rs` does) and, when `rel` is given, `props["relationship"] == rel`. The row map is the decoded properties plus `id = "{source}\|{target}\|{ord}"`, `source`, `target` and `rel` (the `relationship` property, else empty); it is kept when `pred_eval::all_hold(&row, preds)`. Each scanned edge counts against `ctx.budget.check_traversal(scanned)` (the existing budget refusal). Output: `RowSet::from_ids` of the kept ids in walk order, with typed cells (R737 `RowSet::with_cell`): `id`, `source`, `target`, `rel` as `Cell::Text`; each scalar property as `Cell::Int` (integral JSON number), `Cell::Float` (other number), `Cell::Bool` or `Cell::Text`; non-scalar properties are not projected. Scope: `ctx.view` is the served snapshot already narrowed by the caller's read authority (`filter_view` in `crates/medulla-isolation/src/isolation/policy_lease.rs` removes hidden `edge_properties`), and it is the same commons-plus-tenant view `MATCH` reads, so `EDGES` sees exactly the edges a node read would traverse; no second scope check is added. No new error code. Docs: one line in `docs/uql.md`; the generated client models are regenerated, not hand-edited. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R733 (built), MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R737 (typed cells, built). | Test: `crates/medulla-uql/tests/edges_source.rs::edges_rows_carry_source_target_rel_and_properties` (L2): a graph with `a -dependsOn{weight:0.7}-> b`, `a -dependsOn{weight:0.2}-> c` and `b -owns-> c`; `EDGES :dependsOn WHERE weight >= 0.5` returns exactly the row `a\|b\|0` with cells `source=a`, `target=b`, `rel=dependsOn`, `weight=0.7`; `EDGES` alone returns all three in key order; a `Pred::JsonPath` predicate (not relational per `pred_eval::is_relational`) refuses; the parsed op prints back to the same text. `tests/query/test_uql_edges_served.py::test_edge_rows_respect_edge_read_scope` (L4): over a started server, an agent whose read authority hides node `c` gets only the `a\|b\|0` row from `EDGES`, and an agent with full read gets all three. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R736` | **Query-aware evidence forest for context selection (rollup).** Subsystem: retrieval. The engine expands seed memories along schema and semantic relations into a candidate graph. It scores each candidate's direct support apart from the cost of verifying its relation. It penalises redundant and conflicting evidence. It selects a compact forest of evidence under a token budget. Children `.1` to `.5` deliver it. Research: [research.md](research.md) section on evidence forests. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081. | Satisfied only when every child is satisfied. Root journey: `tests/query/test_evidence_forest_served.py::test_forest_fits_budget_and_keeps_supported_evidence` (planned) (L4). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R736.1` | **Model: candidate graph, scores and forest.** Add `EvidenceCandidate{memory_id, support, verify_cost, tokens}`, `ForestSelection{roots, edges, tokens_used, dropped}` and `ForestRefusal` in new `crates/medulla-result-types/src/evidence_forest.rs`. Fields `support` and `verify_cost` are separate f32 values in 0 to 1. Refusal codes: `FOREST_BUDGET_ZERO`, `FOREST_NO_SEEDS`, `FOREST_CANDIDATE_CAP`. Caller chain: `run_round` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs`) → `select_forest` (R736.3). Shape: one new file in the result-types crate; follow `retrieval_verdict.rs`; the test is new `crates/medulla-result-types/tests/evidence_forest.rs`. Depends: none. | Test: `crates/medulla-result-types/tests/evidence_forest.rs::scores_stay_separate_and_bounded` (planned) (L2). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R736.2` | **Behavior: expand seeds and score candidates.** Add `expand_candidates(seeds, schema, hops, cap) -> Vec<EvidenceCandidate>` in new `crates/medulla-plan/src/evidence_forest.rs`. It walks schema relations and semantic-similarity relations from each seed. Defaults: `hops` 2 and `cap` 2000, replaceable knobs `retrieval.forest.hops` and `retrieval.forest.candidate_cap`. A walk past the cap refuses with `FOREST_CANDIDATE_CAP`. Direct support is the seed-to-candidate score. Verify cost is the cost of checking the relation that links the candidate to its parent. Caller chain: `run_round` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/retrieval_round.rs:665`) → `select_forest` (R736.3) → `expand_candidates(seeds, &PlanForestGraph::new(ctx.view, ctx.semantic, knobs.forest_similar_k), hops, cap)`. On main: `expand_candidates`, `ForestRelation`, `RelationKind` and `trait ForestGraph { fn relations(&self, memory_id: &str) -> Vec<ForestRelation>; }` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/evidence_forest.rs`. Reader, decided here: the served round implements the trait over the round's own `PlanCtx` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/exec.rs:97`), the caller's row-projected `GraphView` and its `SemanticStore`, the same pair `GraphHierarchySource::new(ctx.view, ctx.semantic, query)` reads (`retrieval_round.rs:533`); no second neighbour index. New `pub struct PlanForestGraph<'a> { view: &'a GraphView, semantic: &'a SemanticStore, similar_k: usize }` in `evidence_forest.rs`, with `relations(id)` returning, in order: (a) schema relations, one per distinct neighbour of `id` over `view.graph` in either direction (`view.node_map[id]`, `neighbors_undirected`), `kind: Schema`, `strength` = the heaviest finite `weight` over `view.edge_properties[(id, n)]` and `[(n, id)]` clamped to `[0, 1]`, or 1.0 when none, computed by new `pub fn edge_weight_in_view(view: &GraphView, a: &str, b: &str) -> f64` beside `edge_weight` in `crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/core_expand.rs:27` (the same rule over a view; `edge_weight` keeps its body), `verify_cost` 0.1; (b) similarity relations: when `semantic.get_embedding(id)` is `Some(e)`, `semantic.semantic_search_filtered(&e, similar_k + 1, \|c\| c != id && view.node_map.contains_key(c))` truncated to `similar_k`, `kind: Similarity`, `strength` = the score clamped to `[0, 1]`, `verify_cost = 1.0 - strength`; a neighbour already listed as a schema relation is not repeated. `tokens = max(1, ceil(len(view.node_properties[target]) / 4))` bytes-per-token, 1 when the node has no blob. A target not in `view.node_map` is never returned, so the walk stays inside the caller's projection. Knob `retrieval.forest.similar_k` (default 8, [1, 64], neighbours, `Tunable`) registers in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs` and is read into `RoundKnobs` as `forest_similar_k: usize`. Shape: `PlanForestGraph` in `evidence_forest.rs`; `edge_weight_in_view` in `core_expand.rs`; one knob. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R736.1. | Test: `crates/medulla-plan/tests/evidence_forest.rs::expansion_respects_hops_and_cap` (L2) and `crates/medulla-plan/src/evidence_forest.rs::tests::plan_forest_graph_reads_projected_view_and_similarity` (L2): over a view with weighted and unweighted edges, a node outside the projection and stored embeddings, it asserts schema strengths (weight and 1.0), similarity strengths and costs, no duplicate neighbour, `similar_k` truncation, the token estimate and that the hidden node is never a target. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R736.3` | **Behavior: select a forest under a token budget.** BUILD. Type change first (R736.1 type, same row): `EvidenceCandidate` in `crates/medulla-result-types/src/evidence_forest.rs` gains `pub parent: String` (the seed id or candidate id whose relation gave the candidate its kept support; `EvidenceCandidate::new(memory_id, parent, support, verify_cost, tokens)` refuses an empty parent with `INVALID_ARGUMENT`), and `expand_candidates` (R736.2) sets it to the walk's `parent` whenever it keeps a candidate. New in `crates/medulla-plan/src/evidence_forest.rs`: `pub struct ForestPenalty { pub redundancy_weight: f32, pub conflict_weight: f32 }` with `Default` 0.5 and 1.0 (knobs `retrieval.forest.redundancy_weight` and `retrieval.forest.conflict_weight`, both `Tunable`, range [0, 4], registered in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs` and read into `RoundKnobs`), `pub trait ForestRelations { fn similarity(&self, a: &str, b: &str) -> f32; fn conflicts(&self, a: &str, b: &str) -> bool; }` and `pub fn select_forest(candidates: &[EvidenceCandidate], seeds: &[String], relations: &dyn ForestRelations, budget_tokens: u32, penalty: ForestPenalty) -> Result<ForestSelection, ForestRefusal>`. `PlanForestGraph` (R736.2) also implements `ForestRelations`: `similarity(a, b)` is the cosine of `semantic.get_embedding(a)` and `get_embedding(b)` clamped to [0, 1], 0.0 when either has none; `conflicts(a, b)` is true when `view.edge_properties` or the graph holds an edge between `a` and `b` in either direction whose relationship is `CONTRADICTS` or `ATTACKS` (the two names `medulla-retrieval/src/knowledge.rs` already treats as bearing on both endpoints). Algorithm: refuse `FOREST_BUDGET_ZERO` through `check_budget`; repeat: for every unchosen candidate whose `parent` is a seed or already chosen, `redundancy` = max `similarity(c, chosen)` over chosen (0 when none), `gain = (support - verify_cost) * (1 - redundancy_weight * redundancy)`, zeroed (the candidate is skipped for good and pushed to `dropped`) when `conflict_weight > 0` and `conflicts(c, chosen)` for any chosen; `score = gain / max(tokens, 1)`; pick the highest positive score, tie-break lower `memory_id` (byte order); stop when the best candidate's `tokens` exceed the remaining budget (that candidate and every unchosen one go to `dropped`, in the order considered) or no positive score remains. A chosen candidate whose parent is a seed is a root, otherwise it is an edge `(parent, child)`. Same inputs give the same forest. If no candidate is chosen the result is `Ok(ForestSelection::default())` (empty roots; `ForestSelection::new` is for validated construction and is not used for the empty case). Caller chain: `run_round` (`retrieval_round.rs`) → `select_forest(&candidates, &seed_ids, &plan_forest_graph, budget, penalty)`. Token counter: the candidate's own `tokens` (R736.2 computes it); no second counter. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R736.2. | Test: `crates/medulla-plan/tests/evidence_forest.rs::selection_is_a_forest_within_budget_and_drops_redundant_and_conflicting` (planned) (L2): with a stub `ForestRelations`, asserts the budget bound, a near-duplicate dropped, a conflicting candidate dropped, every child's parent chosen, the lower-id tie-break and equal output on a rerun. |

The round passes the forest as its evidence rows. The sufficiency judge (R081.2) reads those rows. A request field `context_budget_tokens` turns the step on. Without it the round keeps its present rows. Budgets: p99 150 ms and 128 MiB for 2000 candidates and a 4000-token budget. Bench: group `retrieval/evidence_forest`. Shape: one call site in `run_round`; reuse `judge_sufficiency`; no new method. No other crate holds a `GeoTask`, and no served method reaches it today. Hard cutover in `crates/medulla-geo/src/geotask.rs`: the enum `TaskStatus` is deleted. `GeoTask.status` becomes `TaskStatusKind`. `GeoTask::new` sets `TaskStatusKind::initial()`. `GeoTask::with_status` is deleted. `GeoTask::apply(&mut self, event: TaskEvent) -> Result<(), TransitionRefused>` is new; it is the only writer after construction. `TaskStatus::is_pending` becomes `GeoTask::is_pending`, which reads `status == TaskStatusKind::Pending`. The serde form of `status` becomes the snake_case stored form (`pending`, `in_progress`). This is a wire change, with no alias. Tests that built tasks with `with_status` reach the state through `apply` events. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R738.10` | **HierarchyVisit/DescentLevel is a classification, not a lifecycle: the type and field are named for what they are.** Stage: model. Language: Rust. A function applied to a wrong-typed argument gives an error value, which a `FILTER` treats as false. `REPLACE` with a pattern that matches the empty string gives an error value. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R055. Caller chain: `Method::Sparql` → `dispatch_request_method` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router.rs:269`) → the evaluator → `expression.rs`. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R742.3` | **Behavior: a solution binding carries an RDF term.** Stage: query evaluation and result serialization. Today `Binding` is `Node(String)` or `Literal(String)`: a literal keeps only its lexical form, so `LANG` is always empty, `DATATYPE` guesses from the lexical form, and `STRDT` and `STRLANG` drop their second argument. `Binding` becomes the RDF term: `Iri(String)` (the absolute IRI, no angle brackets), `Blank(String)` (the label, no `_:`), `Literal(Literal)` and `Triple(Box<[Binding; 3]>)` (a quoted triple). `Literal` holds `lexical: String`, `datatype: String` and `language: Option<String>`; a plain literal has datatype `http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#string`, and a language-tagged one has `http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#langString` and a lower-case tag. This is a hard cutover with no lexical-only path: `as_str`, `is_node` and every `Binding::Node` and `Binding::Literal(String)` construction are removed. Every producer builds a typed term: a query literal and a `VALUES` term keep the datatype and tag they were written with; a typed graph cell (`value`, `datatype`, `lang`) keeps them; a native scalar cell is `xsd:integer` for a JSON integer, `xsd:double` for another number, `xsd:boolean` for a boolean and `xsd:string` for a string; an aggregate is `xsd:integer` for `COUNT`, and `SUM`, `AVG`, `MIN` and `MAX` follow the numeric tower (integer, decimal, double); arithmetic promotes the same way and `/` of two integers is `xsd:decimal`; a remote `SERVICE` result keeps the `datatype` and `xml:lang` its JSON carries; an SQL-mapped row is an `Iri` when its id column names an IRI and a typed literal otherwise. Every consumer reads the term: `STR` returns the lexical form of an IRI or literal; `LANG` returns the tag or the empty string; `DATATYPE` returns the literal's datatype IRI; `STRLANG(lexical, tag)` returns a language-tagged literal; `STRDT(lexical, datatype)` returns a typed literal; `isIRI`, `isBlank`, `isLiteral` and `isNumeric` test the variant and the numeric datatype; `=` compares numerics by value and every other pair by term identity; `sameTerm` is term identity; `ORDER BY` follows the SPARQL ordering of unbound, blank, IRI, literal; string builtins (`UCASE`, `LCASE`, `STRLEN`, `SUBSTR`, `CONCAT`, `CONTAINS`, `REGEX` and the rest) accept only a simple or language-tagged string literal and give an error value for an IRI, a blank node or a non-string typed literal. There is no Turtle fallback: no consumer re-parses a lexical form to recover a term. The wire result carries the term: `SparqlResult.rows` is `list[list[SparqlTerm | None]]`, where `SparqlTerm` has `kind` (`iri`, `bnode`, `literal` or `triple`), `value` (the IRI, the label, the lexical form or the quoted-triple text) and, for a literal, `datatype` and `language`; the JSON, XML, CSV and TSV serializations of a solution table write the typed term (`"type": "literal"` with `datatype` or `xml:lang`, `<literal datatype=...>`, the lexical form for CSV and the N-Triples form for TSV); the result-cache key carries the result shape revision, so no entry written before the cutover is served. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R742.1. Caller chain: `Method::Sparql` → `dispatch_request_method` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router.rs:269`) → `handle_sparql` (`crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/sparql.rs`) → `medulla_rdf::sparql::execute` → `expression.rs`; the SPARQL protocol endpoint (`crates/medulla-server-blob/src/sparql_http.rs`) serializes the same `SparqlResult`. Shape: edit `crates/medulla-rdf/src/sparql.rs` (the term), `sparql/expression.rs`, `sparql/scalar.rs`, `sparql/algebra.rs`, `sparql/aggregate.rs`, `sparql/graph_match.rs`, `sparql/graph_result.rs`, `sparql/bool_builtins.rs`, `sparql/service.rs`, `sparql/proof.rs`, `update/terms.rs` and `contract.rs` in `medulla-rdf`; the consumers in `medulla-h-sparql`, `medulla-virtual-graph`, `medulla-exec`, `medulla-plan`, `medulla-server`, `medulla-server-blob` and `medulla-protocol-types`; and `medulla/generated/models.py`. No new method, server or store. | Test: `crates/medulla-rdf/tests/sparql_builtin_conformance.rs::typed_terms_match_the_w3c_vectors` (planned, L2). Acceptance: `LANG`, `DATATYPE`, `STRLANG` and `STRDT` give the W3C vectors in full: `LANG("chat"@fr)` is `"fr"`, `LANG("abc")` is `""`, `DATATYPE("abc")` is `xsd:string`, `DATATYPE("abc"@en)` is `rdf:langString`, `DATATYPE(42)` is `xsd:integer`, `STRLANG("chat", "fr")` is `"chat"@fr` with `LANG` `"fr"`, `STRDT("42", xsd:integer)` is `"42"^^xsd:integer` with `isNumeric` true, `UCASE(<http://ex/a>)` is an error value. |

A graph-visualization client names the nodes it draws; the engine runs the algorithm on the subgraph those nodes induce, limited to the caller's visible set. A node the caller cannot see answers byte-identically to an unknown id. The three results become the typed views `CommunityDetectionView`, `PageRankView` and `ShortestPathView` (hard cutover: the bare `Vec<Vec<String>>`, `Vec<(String, f64)>` and `Option<Vec<String>>` results are removed). Depends: none. | Rollup closes when its children are verified. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R743.7` | **Wiring: the scope of a graph algorithm follows the request shape.** Stage: authorization. `medulla_capabilities::policy` (`crates/medulla-capabilities/src/lib.rs`) is the one place a method's scope is decided, and both the request gate and the batch inner-scope check read it. For `PageRank`, `GetShortestPath` and `CommunityDetection` it returns `authz_action` `compute:graph-read` when the request carries `node_ids` (including `Some([])`, a scope of nothing) and the ledger row's `compute:graph-algo` when it does not. The scoped call is bounded (MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R743.4) and limited to the caller's visible set (MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R743.2), so a human session in the visualizer may run it; a whole-graph call stays service-only. The rule is one function chosen by request shape: no second method, no second scope check. Writes: none. Depends: MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R743.1. Caller chain: served `PageRank`, `GetShortestPath` or `CommunityDetection` → `dispatch_request_method` → `medulla_capabilities::policy` → `check_scope_and_admin_authority` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary/authorization.rs`) → `allows_method`. Shape: one function `scoped_graph_algorithm_policy` called first in `policy_for_method`. | Tests: `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/scopes/tests.rs::a_graph_algorithm_scope_is_chosen_by_whether_the_request_names_node_ids` (L1); `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/split_tests/graph_algorithm_scope_tests.rs::a_graph_read_principal_is_allowed_a_scoped_call_and_refused_a_whole_graph_call` (L3). Acceptance: a principal holding only `compute:graph-read` is allowed each scoped call and refused each whole-graph call with `ACCESS_DENIED` naming `compute:graph-algo`. |
